**429.** When you give up inertness and become full of consciousness,
then you will be able to see Krishna's form.

**430.** Listen: by Krishna's will power has been given to matter,
but only one who has devotion can see it.

**431.** A knot has formed between matter and consciousness;
only one whose rajas and tamas are gone can untie it.[^02-30]

**432.** Who can understand the nature of the inert world?
People keep going round like the oil-presser's bullock.

**433.** The oil-presser's bullock walks a short track, it is true,
but, caught in that plight, it never finds the end.

**434.** Blinkered, it goes round one path until it dies;
just so the soul goes round within worldly life.

**435.** Wearing the blinkers of maya, the soul goes round till it dies,
and therefore it cannot see the subtle truth.

**436.** Coveting others' property and others' wives,
how can one then practise the worship of Krishna?

---

### Translator's notes

[^02-18]: "To take on the misfortunes (*balai*)" is a Bengali formula of admiration: "may every evil that threatens this beauty fall on me".
[^02-19]: Keshava Bharati, Chaitanya's sannyasa guru.
[^02-20]: *Ulu-dhvani*, the ritual ululation of Bengali women at solemn moments.
[^02-21]: Nandana is Indra's heavenly garden.
[^02-22]: Advaita Acharya's house at Shantipur. Govinda passes very briefly over an episode known from other biographies: after his sannyasa Chaitanya spent several days at Shantipur, where Shachi came to see him.
[^02-23]: Kashi Mitra, not to be confused with Kashi Mishra of Puri. The Damodar river flows near Burdwan.
[^02-24]: *Beto shak*: bathua or white goosefoot, a popular Bengali leafy green.
[^02-25]: *Chukra/chukai*: a sour dish (a broth of sour leaves or fruit), sweetened with molasses.
[^02-26]: A harsh image from street speech: the dead body thrown out where the leather-workers (*muchi*) work.
[^02-27]: The line is corrupt in the edition ("…na chala mora jai"). From the context (the next page) Narayangarh is meant.
[^02-28]: The *sattvika bhavas* are the eight bodily signs of ecstasy: stupor, perspiration, hair standing on end, faltering voice, trembling, change of colour, tears and fainting.
[^02-29]: The lines before this, explaining where the laddus came from, are missing in the edition.
[^02-30]: *Rajas* and *tamas*, the gunas of passion and ignorance.

---

# Part 3

*Through Orissa to Puri. Departure for the South. Meeting with Ramananda Raya. The Buddhists of Trimandal, Dhundhiram, the rich man Tirtharam (pp. 41–60)*

*[p. 41]*

**437.** Krishna is the truth beyond change, so say the Vedas;
he cannot be grasped by a changeful mind."

**438.** Thus, having blessed many lands,
merciful Chaitanya hastened to Jaleshvar.

**439.** At Jaleshvar there is a Shiva named Billeshvara;
seeing him, devotion welled up in the Lord's heart.

**440.** A sannyasi lived in Shiva's temple,
and the Lord went slowly up to him.

**441.** Coming before the sannyasi, he bowed down,
and the sannyasi, seeing the Lord, started.

**442.** The sannyasi said, "Who are you? You are no ordinary man.
Why do you bow before me?

**443.** By the fruit of what merit have I seen you today?
The moment I saw you, my bonds were cut.

**444.** You are the fruit of my austerities, O merciful one;
seeing you, all my sins have been destroyed."

**445.** Thus the excellent sannyasi, gazing at the Lord,
choked with love and burst into tears.

**446.** At once my Lord, to hide his identity,
raised his arms and began to dance, crying "Hari!"

**447.** Now crying "Krishna!" he leaps and runs,
now falls to the ground and rolls about.

*[p. 42]*

**448.** A great crowd gathered for the sankirtana of the Name,
and my Chaitanya passed the night awake.

**449.** Next day, coming to the bank of the Suvarnarekha,
he was thrilled to see Raghunatha Dasa.[^03-31]

**450.** Then we went on to Hariharpur,
and there Nimai became intoxicated with the Name of Hari.

**451.** He fell crashing to the ground,
*[the second line of the couplet is missing in the edition]*

**452.** and so that day passed,
the Lord weeping, drunk with bliss.

**453.** The next day we went to Baleshvar;
seeing Gopala, the Lord was in great bliss.

**454.** Next morning we went to Nilagarh,
and at Nilagarh Nimai became drunk with the Name.

**455.** As he danced, he gradually lost consciousness
and fell crashing to the ground.

**456.** Thus that day passed;
countless onlookers came and sat around him.

**457.** As he sang the Name his bliss increased,
and the Lord fell senseless to the ground.

**458.** Thus the devotees gathered together
and became intoxicated, tasting supreme bliss.

**459.** Next day, coming to the bank of the river Vaitarani,
he burst into tears, crying, "Krishna, take me across!"[^03-32]

*[p. 43]*

**460.** His body choked with love, always as if crazed,
he walks on saying "Hari", looking neither right nor left.

**461.** The next day we crossed the Mahanadi,
and on the way we saw the deity Gopinatha.

**462.** We all received Gopinatha's maha-prasada,
and with that prasada joy surged in our hearts.

**463.** Then, all filled with love,
we set off to see Sakshi-Gopala.[^03-33]

**464.** Raising his arms and crying "Hari!", he began to run,
and his falling tears turned the earth to mud.

**465.** Seeing Sakshi-Gopala from afar,
he sprawled on the ground, choked with love.

**466.** Seeing Gopala, it was as if he remembered something,
and at once, gazing at his face, he began to weep.

**467.** "Gopala, Gopala!" he called again and again.
Who can describe the force of his love?

**468.** Then, going to the temple of Lingaraja,
the Lord burst into tears in some mood unknown.[^03-34]

**469.** Leaving Lingaraja we came to Atharanala;[^03-35]
seeing the temple flag, my Lord fell to the ground.

**470.** Never had I seen such a flood of tears:
*[p. 44]*
the Lord turned the earth to mud with the stream of his tears.

**471.** Crying, "Ah, ah, Lord Jagannatha!", Sri Hari
flooded the ground, shedding tears.

**472.** He falls and flails and weeps aloud;
whoever he sees before him he clasps in his arms.

**473.** "Look, there is my Krishna dancing as a cowherd boy!
Ah, how beautiful his hair is!"

**474.** Seeing the Lord's temple he weeps aloud,
now and then falling crashing to the ground.

**475.** He rushed to the Lord's door and rolled in the dust,
but for the stream of his tears he could not see Vishnu's form.

**476.** Falling, flailing, crying out, rolling on the ground —
a hundred thousand people came to see that state.

**477.** With great difficulty the Lord checked the flow of his love,
but on seeing Mahavishnu he burst into tears again.

**478.** The devotees were startled by the sound of his weeping,
and he ran and embraced Gadadhara.

**479.** He went to the Garuda pillar and clung to it;
his forehead was cut and blood began to flow.

**480.** Seeing this, [illegible][^03-36] with his upper cloth
wiped the Lord's blood away.

**481.** Having had darshan, he went to Mishra's house,[^03-37]
and the devotees began to arrive in procession.

*[p. 45]*

**482.** Thus, staying some time in Puri,
we enjoyed ever new delights each day.

**483.** The Avadhuta, Krishnadasa and Haridasa
stayed by the Lord, relishing supreme bliss.

**484.** Puri was filled with the sound of the Name all eight watches,[^03-38]
and everyone rolled on the ground.

**485.** Some string garlands, some grind sandalwood,
some prepare the bhoga offering.

**486.** Gradually all the companions gathered,
and Puri's splendour surpassed Vaikuntha.

**487.** The brahmana Krishnadasa and pot-bellied Shyamadasa
guard the Lord on either side.

**488.** Now he falls, drunk with love,
now he leaps into the sea.

**489.** Premadasa, Gopadasa and the brahmana mahanta
shower nectar, reading the Bhagavata.

**490.** Raghunatha Dasa and Acharya Shekhara,
Damodara, Narahari and Gadadhara —

**491.** every day all of them go together to the holy temple,
taking my Lord along in their midst.

**492.** Sweet mridangas play, sometimes kartals too;
he is always drunk with the Name, with no thought of time.

*[p. 46]*

**493.** Thus my Lord, in Mishra's house,
was always rejoicing with the devotees.

**494.** Every day Kashi Mishra brings prasada,[^03-39]
its fragrance stealing the heart, its taste sweet.

**495.** All kinds of fried and roasted dishes — how many!
How much prasada I stuffed into my belly!

**496.** Fried chickpeas, crunchy mixtures, mung, beans,
sesame, linseed, wheat and barley — I would die for them!

**497.** Hundreds of fruits and roots, scraped coconut —
every day the Gora of Nadia gives them to me with his own hands.

**498.** Sugar crystals, dates, laddus and gaja,
adhasa, pitha, puli, juicy gaja,

**499.** rice soaked in ghee, bhuta-ghonto, nota greens —
after eating such prasada I am lost for words.

**500.** Speechless, every day I eat my fill;
whatever I get goes straight into my belly.

**501.** Thus the days went by,
and gradually all the devotees began to arrive.

**502.** Shankara Bharati and Paramananda Puri,
Damodara Svami, Pradyumna Brahmachari,

**503.** Chidananda Giri, Premananda Sarasvati
came and went to the Lord every day.

*[p. 47]*

**504.** Many devotees gathered together at Nilachala
and worshipped with great delight.

**505.** At this time Sarvabhauma appeared,
and with him many devotees came and joined them.

**506.** Seeing Mahavishnu, the Lord was filled with longing
and bowed again and again in devotion.

**507.** Seeing Govinda,[^03-40] the Lord fainted
and lay there like a corpse.

**508.** Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya was there;
he lifted him in his arms and carried him to his own house.

**509.** How much service he rendered the Lord!
The rasa of devotion overflowed in Sarvabhauma.

**510.** Then, having given devotion to Sarvabhauma,
the Lord made ready to set out on his southern journey.

**511.** For three months my Chaitanya Gosai
stayed in Puri together with Nitai.

**512.** Then, on the seventh day of Vaishakha,
he set out for the South, floating in the nectar of love.

**513.** At the time of departure Nitai grew anxious
and, humbled by devotion, began to speak:

**514.** "Do not go alone," said Nityananda Raya;
"let all of us go along with you."

*[p. 48]*

**515.** Very eager to go was Gadadhara, dear as his own life,
and Premananda Sarasvati and Shankara Bharati.

**516.** Hearing this, my Lord smiled a little
and said, "I shall go alone, without companions."

**517.** The Avadhuta Nityananda, hearing these words,
began to speak, shedding tears:

**518.** "You are going far away to the South.
Let the brahmana Krishnadasa Thakura go with you.

**519.** The brahmana is pure and will do
whatever you tell him to do."

**520.** "How can we stay here without you?
That is why we all say: let us go with you."

**521.** Hearing this, Mahaprabhu smiled a little
and, giving them instructions, dissuaded them all.

**522.** Hearing that, they all said,
"Then let your servant Govinda go with you."

**523.** Hearing this, my Lord said, smiling,
"I am very fond of Govinda's company.

**524.** Whoever goes or does not go, Govinda will go;
whatever work I have, Govinda will do."[^03-41]

**525.** Saying this, Sri Chaitanya took his leave
and set off southwards, all the devotees running after him.

*[p. 49]*

**526.** Little by little, very slowly,
we all reached the temple of Alalanatha.

**527.** Seeing Alalanatha, his feelings overflowed,
and he soaked the earth of that place with his tears.

**528.** Dancing, the Lord lost consciousness
and fell crashing to the ground.

**529.** Next morning, taking leave of everyone,
we three set out on our southern journey.[^03-42]

**530.** At this time Sarvabhauma said softly:
"On the bank of the Godavari you will meet Raya.

**531.** Ramananda Raya is the best of devotees who know rasa;
at Krishna's name his eyes are always wet with tears.

**532.** Rama Raya enjoys pure bliss;
at the Name of Hari his heart rejoices."

**533.** Hearing this, he hastened to the bank of the Godavari,
and there Ramananda came and met him.

**534.** Seeing the young sannyasi, devotion arose in him;
Rama Raya clasped his feet and began to weep.

**535.** Ramananda Raya said, "You are God himself.
I am most fortunate to have seen you."

**536.** The Lord said, "Raya, speak about Krishna;
by your conclusions the ache of my heart will go."

*[p. 50]*

**537.** Raya said, "Lord, I know nothing;
unless you make me speak, no words will come."

**538.** "Dwelling in the heart, you teach everything;
by your mercy you make the dumb eloquent."

**539.** The Lord asked, "By what truth is the mind purified?"
Raya said, "That truth is meeting with the saints."

**540.** "I want from you a truth subtler still."
Raya said, "There is no truth beyond renunciation."

**541.** The Lord said, "The subtle truth is attachment to God."
Raya said, "Higher even than that is prema-bhakti."

**542.** The Lord said, "Tell me a deeper essence still, O wise one."
Raya said, "The essence of all is Rai, full of rasa."[^03-43]

**543.** Rama Raya wanted to tell a deeper essence still,
but Gora Raya at once covered his mouth.

**544.** The Lord said, "Butter lies hidden in milk;
he who churns will get its taste."

**545.** The Lord said, "Raya, I know nothing.
Speak, speak of Krishna; let me hear it from your mouth.

**546.** You, Rama Raya, are a Vaishnava detached from the world.
Speak, speak the truth of Krishna, let my heart be soothed."

**547.** Hearing the Lord's words, Ramananda Raya
folded his hands in humility and said:

*[p. 51]*

**548.** "Why do you play tricks again and again, Lord of the universe?
Be merciful and make this servant your follower.

**549.** You have spread the rasa of devotion all over the land
and in your mercy sanctified this earth.

**550.** Have mercy on this wretched one, Jagannatha;
give detachment to my heart and take me with you."

**551.** Hearing this, the Lord embraced Raya,
and Rama Raya, clasping his knees, wept.

**552.** Ramananda's breast was flooded with tears,
and seeing this the Lord said in a choked voice:

**553.** "You, Rama Raya, are the crest-jewel of Vaishnavas."
Ramananda, his face lowered, kept saying "Rama, Rama".

**554.** The Lord said, "Raya, you are most fortunate;
your devotion is beyond description."

**555.** Raya said, "I am the lowest of sinners;
you have been defiled by touching me.

**556.** Mercifully forgive me that offence,
and, seated in my heart, make me taste devotion."

**557.** That night Ramananda passed
in such conversation with Gora-chand.

**558.** Next day Raya clasped the Lord's feet,
*[p. 52]*
took his leave and went off to his own duties.

**559.** The Lord said, "Ramananda, now I shall go on;
come to Nilachala and stay with me.

**560.** You, I and the Bhatta[^03-44] will live in seclusion
and, discussing the truth of Krishna, find peace in life."

**561.** So saying, the Lord sent Raya away,
and Ramananda bowed and went home.

**562.** Of all that Raya said with the Lord,
not a hundredth part has remained in this book.

**563.** Thus Ramananda came for ten days
and rejoiced to see the sannyasi of Nadia.

**564.** The Lord felt great affection on seeing Ramananda,
and Raya lost consciousness on seeing the Lord.

**565.** Taking leave of Raya,
the Lord entered the town of Trimandal.[^03-45]

**566.** Many Buddhists live in the town of Trimandal;
they all came to meet beautiful Gauranga.

**567.** The Lord debated with the Buddhists,
and the king of Trimandal came to act as arbiter.

**568.** The Buddhists admitted defeat in the debate,
and all the pandits and onlookers burst out laughing.

**569.** All said, "This sannyasi is no man!
The way he debated is beyond words."

*[p. 53]*

**570.** The chief of the Buddhists, Ramagiri Raya,
bowed and said, "Show me the way.

**571.** You are no man, young sannyasi;
I long to stay with you.

**572.** In the world I was the crest-jewel of unbelievers;
mercifully show me the path of devotion."

**573.** Smiling, Lord Chaitanya said kindly:
"You are my master, Ramagiri Raya.

**574.** One who thrills with joy saying 'Hari'
is my master — that is the practice."

**575.** Hearing the Lord's words, Ramagiri Raya
at once fell crashing to the ground.

**576.** Lying at his feet, Ramagiri said,
"What have you said to this lowest of men, O merciful one?

**577.** Dwelling in all beings, you see everything.
Mercifully give me a place at your crimson feet."

**578.** Devotion arose in Ramagiri the unbeliever,
and seeing this my Lord was filled with joy.

**579.** All the Buddhists, crest-jewels of pandits,
followed Ramagiri's path.

**580.** The young sannyasi left the disputants without hope;
seeing this, Ramananda[^03-46] looked all around.

*[p. 54]*

**581.** At last, eager to debate,
came Dhundhiram Tirtha, who lived by the Tungabhadra.

**582.** Ever drunk with pride, vain of his learning,
he did not understand the path of devotion — learned only in dry logic.

**583.** Dhundhiram was indeed a great pandit,
but he was very much afraid of debating.

**584.** Svami Dhundhiram went to debate,
but the Lord, shedding tears, rolled on the ground again and again.

**585.** The Lord said, "Listen, listen, Svami Dhundhiram:
I have lost the argument with you.

**586.** I will write you a certificate of victory in secret:
'Chaitanya has now lost to you.'

**587.** By the grace of Vani[^03-47] you are a learned Gosai;
who could beat you in logic and scripture?

**588.** Nyaya, Sankhya, Patanjala and Vedanta —
you are expert in every shastra, good sir.

**589.** I am an ignorant sannyasi and know nothing;
again and again I admit defeat before you.

**590.** You are more learned than the Dhundhi of old;[^03-48]
your learning is famous throughout the world."

**591.** So saying, he sent Dhundhiram away,
but Dhundhi did not want to go and looked all around.

*[p. 55]*

**592.** After looking this way and that, Dhundhi
fell at the Lord's feet with a heart made wholly pure.

**593.** Having given devotion to the unbeliever Dhundhi,
the Lord set out for Pantha-guha, remembering Sri Hari.[^03-49]

**594.** Dhundhiram became known by the name Haridasa,
and the unbelievers whispered and said all sorts of things.

**595.** Smiling, the Lord called me,
and I lifted the two pairs of sandals onto my shoulders.

**596.** The sandals, water-pot and whatever belongings we had,
and also what Raya had given, I gathered up and took.

**597.** Not far away there was a banyan called Akshaya;
at dusk the Lord arrived at that place.

**598.** A Shiva named Vateshvara is there,
and Gora-chand hastened there with devotion.

**599.** With devotion the Lord bowed to Vateshvara
and spent the night there without food.

**600.** In the morning the Lord went to bathe,
and I went from door to door begging alms.

**601.** I came back with the alms at midday;
my Gora Raya cooked and made the offering.

**602.** I received prasada like nectar,
and just then the wealthy Tirtha[^03-50] arrived.

*[p. 56]*

**603.** Two harlots came with him to look,
to test the sannyasi's vaunted firmness.

**604.** The harlots named Satyabai and Lakshmibai
came up to the Lord and talked of many things.

**605.** Coached by Tirtha, those two harlots
made many attempts to see through the Lord.

**606.** Tirtharam said all sorts of things to himself:
"Now by a trick I will rob this sannyasi of his power."

**607.** Lakshmi played all manner of tricks, and Satyabala laughed;
Satyabala sat down beside the Lord with a smile.

**608.** Satya undid her bodice and bared her breasts,
and the Lord addressed Satya as "Mother".

**609.** At the Lord's words Satya trembled all over,
and seeing this Lakshmi was much afraid.

**610.** There was not the slightest agitation in the Lord's mind.
Satyabala ran and fell at his feet.

**611.** "Why do you make me an offender, Mother?"
Saying only this, the Lord fell to the ground.

**612.** His matted hair came loose, he was grey with dust,
his body trembling all over with longing.

**613.** Everything about my Lord was in disarray;
he no longer saw where Lakshmi was, where Satya was.

*[p. 57]*

**614.** Saying "Hari, Hari!" the Lord began to dance,
his hair standing on end, tears streaming down.

**615.** His kaupina had slipped off; where was his outer cloth?
He danced naked, breathing hard and fast.

**616.** He falls, heedless of thorns and splinters;
the bunch of garlands at his neck has broken.

**617.** From fasting he is nothing but skin and bone;
streams of blood flow over his thin limbs.

**618.** Drunk with the Name of Hari, Gora Raya dances,
and a wondrous radiance comes forth from his body.

**619.** Seeing this, that rich man was shaken within;
he fell at his feet and took refuge.

**620.** The Lord, unaware of the outer world, tramples him underfoot;
crying "Hari!" with arms upraised he dances onwards.

**621.** Clasping Satya in his arms he says, "Say 'Hari'!
Say 'Hari', lord of life, Mukunda, Murari!"

**622.** Where is the Lord, where is Mukunda-Murari?
Seeing such a state, all lost consciousness.

**623.** Drunk with the Name of Hari, the Lord has no outer awareness;
his head droops, his soul in turmoil.

**624.** Froth at his mouth, dust on his limbs, no cloth,
his hair bristling, his eyes closed.

*[p. 58]*

**625.** Seeing this state, all the Buddhists cry, "Hari, Hari!"
Hearing it, tears flow from Gora's eyes.

**626.** The tears began to spurt like a squirt-gun;
seeing this, Tirtharam burst into tears.

**627.** "I am a great unbeliever," says Tirtharam;
"be merciful, Lord, and give me the Name of Hari."

**628.** Embracing the unbeliever Tirtharam,
the Lord says, "Tirtharam, you are a holy man.

**629.** I have been purified by touching you.
'You are a foremost devotee,'" he says again and again.

**630.** Then the rich Tirtharam fell at his feet
and wept long in great distress.

**631.** When, through his weeping, devotion arose,
the Lord at once took his hand and embraced him.

**632.** The Lord said, "Count riches as straw,
and then you will gain the priceless jewel of devotion.

**633.** Throw your clothes and ornaments far away;
leave transient wealth and worship the eternal treasure.

**634.** Coming and going again and again you will suffer —
this is the counsel for those free from desire.

**635.** This cherished body, covered with skin,
will rot in a few days' time.

*[p. 59]*

**636.** When the bird of life flies from the body,
it becomes either worms, or ashes, or excrement.

**637.** In the three worlds there is nothing to be proud of;
the only glory lies in worshipping God.

**638.** Knowing luxury and wealth to be transient,
throw them away one by one.

**639.** Faith in God brings God to you;
no other proof can be put into words.

**640.** Countless worlds are proof of it,
yet the learned gosai wants proof.

**641.** There is no need for much debate and wrangling;
Krishna comes and meets the seeker through faith.

**642.** What need is there to discuss many shastras?
Have faith and worship Krishna.

**643.** One who boasts of wealth again and again
suffers unceasing sorrow day by day.

**644.** One who prides himself for the sake of honour —
tell me, who will relieve his sorrow?

**645.** 'This is mine, I am his' — so everyone says,
but when the eyes close, no one belongs to anyone.

**646.** All people pretend to kinship for nothing;
mourning a dead body is like mourning a broken doll.

*[p. 60]*

**647.** The son is born of the father, everyone knows,
but the Vedas do not teach that the two minds are one.

**648.** When life leaves the son's body,
that does not mean the father dies.

**649.** The son takes birth from the mother's body,
but know for certain that the two are not one.

**650.** No one belongs to anyone — this is the course of what is to be known,
and it cannot be established by proofs.

**651.** God is the object of knowledge, and the proof of him
is present within the human heart.

**652.** Fools think him farther than the far;
the wise accept that he is very near.

**653.** I have told you the essence of truth, the teaching of the Vedas;
foolish people do not seek it.

**654.** Whoever knows all these true principles
does not die again and again."

**655.** Hearing all this from the Lord's lips, Tirtharam
gave up attachment to worldly things and chanted the Name of Hari.

**656.** The Lord became intoxicated with the sankirtana of Hari,
and gradually his companions came and gathered round.[^03-51]

**657.** "The rich Tirtharam has fallen into trouble" —
so the unbelievers said of him, gossiping in many ways.

---

### Translator's notes

[^03-31]: According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Raghunatha Dasa (Gosvami) of Saptagram fled to Chaitanya at Puri only some years later. A meeting at the Suvarnarekha in 1510 is one of the details of the *Karcha* that critics regard as anachronistic.
[^03-32]: The Vaitarani is a real river in Orissa and also the mythical river at the border of Yama's realm which must be "crossed"; hence the double sense of the cry.
[^03-33]: Sakshi-Gopala, "Gopala the witness", is a deity near Puri. Gopinatha, mentioned just before, is on the traditional route at Remuna (near Baleshvar), not after the Mahanadi; Govinda may have confused the order.
[^03-34]: Lingaraja is the chief Shiva temple of Bhubaneswar.
[^03-35]: Atharanala is the eighteen-arched stone bridge at the entrance to Puri, from which the flag of Jagannatha's temple is first seen.
[^03-36]: The word is corrupt in the edition (*dhyanapura*); perhaps the name of one of the companions.
[^03-37]: Kashi Mishra, in whose house Chaitanya later lived in Puri.
[^03-38]: The eight watches (*at-pahar*): round the clock.
[^03-39]: The beginning of this line is partly lost in the edition and is restored from the sense.
[^03-40]: Probably Jagannatha, whom Govinda here calls Govinda; possibly a separate deity of the temple complex.
[^03-41]: A key passage for the controversy. According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 7) only the brahmana Krishnadasa went south with Chaitanya. The *Karcha* acknowledges Krishnadasa's candidacy but asserts that Govinda went too.
[^03-42]: "We three": Chaitanya, Govinda and apparently Krishnadasa, who is hardly mentioned afterwards.
[^03-43]: Rai is Radha. A compressed version of the famous dialogue of Chaitanya and Ramananda (CC Madhya 8), structured quite differently from the *Charitamrita*.
[^03-44]: Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya.
[^03-45]: Trimandal (*Trimanda*) is a place near the Godavari, perhaps Trimalgiri or Tirumalla in Sen's reading; the identification is uncertain.
[^03-46]: So in the edition; perhaps Ramagiri is meant.
[^03-47]: Vani is Sarasvati, goddess of learning.
[^03-48]: An allusion to some famous Dhundhi of the past, probably a commentator.
[^03-49]: Pantha-guha, "the traveller's cave", is a place-name; cf. the robber Pantha Bhil in the next part.
[^03-50]: Tirtharam, the rich man of the following episode.
[^03-51]: The line is unclear; perhaps Tirtharam's companions are meant.

---

# Part 4

*Vateshvara, Munna, Venkata. The robber Pantha Bhil. Girishvara and the silent ascetic. Tripadi, Pana-Narasimha, Vishnukanchi, Pakshitirtha (pp. 61–80)*

*[p. 61]*

**658.** Tirtharam gave up worldly things like a straw,
and, crying "Hari!", danced with both arms outstretched.

**659.** He marked his whole body with tilaka and wore a kaupina;
devotion made him utterly humble and meek.

**660.** Hearing of this, his wife
came running at once, weeping.

**661.** Clasping Tirtha's feet she wept,
but Tirtharam paid no heed to her words.

**662.** Her name was Kamala Kumari, and she was very beautiful;
her beauty lit up everything around.

**663.** Tirtha took Kamala by the hand and said,
"I have given all my property to you.

**664.** I have escaped from hell with my life;
you enjoy all the wealth and luxury."

**665.** Hearing these words, Kamala Kumari
fell crashing to the ground.

**666.** Seeing the net of maya that Kamala was casting,
Tirtharam smiled a little and said, "Chant the Name of Hari."

**667.** Weeping, Kamala Kumari
went back home, and Tirtha became a beggar on the road.

**668.** Having delivered Tirtharam, beautiful Gauranga
then left the holy place of Vateshvara.

*[p. 62]*

**669.** How many people brought how many clothes!
But the Lord did not touch a single piece.

**670.** At last the Lord called out "Govinda!"
and gave me a clap on the back:

**671.** "We have spent seven days at this Vateshvara.
Let us go to Nandishvara to see him."

**672.** Hearing this, I took the sandals on my shoulder
and, leaving Vateshvara, went with the Lord.

**673.** On the way we came to a vast jungle,
and seeing it my heart failed me.

**674.** That jungle stretched for ten kroshas,[^04-52]
and I grew anxious: how would we get through?

**675.** My all-knowing Lord smiled slightly
and went on ahead, while I fell behind.

**676.** I followed the Lord along the footpath,
and by his will I felt no fear.

**677.** How many beasts had their lairs there,
yet not one showed itself before or behind us!

**678.** Having crossed the jungle, near the town of Munna
he sat beneath a tree to rest.

**679.** Two householders of Munna came
and carefully brought some flour.

*[p. 63]*

**680.** The Lord said nothing to them, good or bad,
and gradually the two sat down close by.

**681.** Gazing at the young sannyasi, the two of them
looked at him fixedly, without blinking.

**682.** Gradually towards evening a great hubbub arose:
the townspeople came in droves to see him.

**683.** A fiery radiance flowed from the Lord's body,
and seeing it all the people stood stock-still.

**684.** Gradually the people of Munna came forward
and one by one bowed down to him.

**685.** With devotion all the people began to say,
"Come into the town, leave this tree."

**686.** My Lord, absorbed in love, did not listen to their words;
inwardly saying "Hari", he was weeping all the while.

**687.** Gradually feeling arose within him,
and at once the Lord got up and began to dance.

**688.** Crying "Hari, Hari!", he falls to the ground,
and with him the village people grew excited.

**689.** All of them, clapping their hands, began to dance,
and seeing this my Lord burst into tears.

**690.** Any unbeliever who saw that state with his own eyes
felt devotion overflowing into his heart.

*[p. 64]*

**691.** Thus half the night went by,
with all the people dancing and crying "Hari, Hari".

**692.** At last the respectable married women, leaving their homes,
came to that place to see Gauranga.

**693.** Having seen beautiful Gauranga with their own eyes,
the women could not go back home.

**694.** Looking at one another, one said to the next,
"Seeing the sannyasi, my heart is in a turmoil.

**695.** Sister, never have I seen anyone so handsome!
This is the one everybody calls Chaitanya Gosai.

**696.** Ah, from fasting he is nothing but skin and bone!
Why at such an age has he bound up a load of matted hair?"

**697.** Saying this, all the women of Munna
were overcome with weeping, tears flowing from their eyes.

**698.** So the night passed, and no one slept.
In the morning my Lord rose and set off southwards.

**699.** The people of Munna begged him in crowds to stay,
but my Lord would not listen to any entreaty.

**700.** There was a very wretched woman there,
weeping beneath that tree, tears streaming from her eyes.

**701.** When the Lord made ready to depart,
that old woman, weeping, begged for food and clothing.

*[p. 65]*

**702.** She wore torn clothes and had no food in her belly.
Seeing her, Chaitanya Gosai stopped.

**703.** Seeing her condition and understanding it in his heart,
the Lord, smiling a little, began begging alms all around.

**704.** He said, "Give me alms, brothers of Munna;
when I have alms of food and clothing, then I shall go."

**705.** The men and women of Munna, floating in joy,
brought heaps upon heaps of food and clothing.

**706.** All said, "He wants provisions for the road,"
and so they brought them in heaps.

**707.** Everyone was eager to put clothing into the Lord's hands,
and seeing the commotion the Lord began to laugh.

**708.** All said, "Clothing is a trifle, worth nothing;
let Chaitanya Gosai take my cloth first!"

**709.** No one knew what was in the Lord's mind,
and so they all hurried to bring food and clothing.

**710.** The Lord said, "Listen, listen, people of Munna:
I have accepted your alms.

**711.** Here beneath the tree sits this wretched woman;
give all this food and clothing to her."

**712.** All the people were amazed at his compassion.
Someone said, "He begged alms for the old woman's sake."

*[p. 66]*

**713.** Saying this, my Lord [adjusted] his outer cloth
and set off, saying "Hari, Hari".

**714.** The Lord looked at me and made a sign,
and I followed behind with the water-pot and sandals.

**715.** Many people began to walk along with him,
but the Lord did not once look back.

**716.** One by one all the people turned back,
but Ramananda Svami would not leave him.

**717.** Ramananda Svami is a man of great virtue;
privately I asked him what was in his heart.

**718.** Ramananda said, "Brother, on seeing the Lord
my hard heart has melted.

**719.** If the Lord does not make me his disciple,
I shall give up my life at once and not remain in the world."

**720.** Then my Lord came to the town of Venkata,
arriving there at midday.

**721.** There lived a learned gosai there,
a great Vedantic scholar without an equal.

**722.** The foremost of pandits wanted to debate,
and the Lord replied, "I have lost."

**723.** Even so the svami would not give up the debate,
and the Lord, his face beaming, began to laugh.

*[p. 67]*

**724.** However much the svami argued for advaita-vada,
Chaitanya explained to him the doctrine of dvaita-advaita.[^04-53]

**725.** At last a fierce debate broke out,
and gradually the danda-svami admitted defeat.

**726.** His name was Ramananda, and he was a great pandit;
Ramananda was initiated into the Name of Hari.

**727.** The Lord poured the nectar of the Name into his ears,
and devotion overflowed in the svami's heart.

**728.** Then Ramananda Svami bowed down
and at the Lord's command went back to his matha.

**729.** The svami gave the Name of Hari to all his disciples,
and his mind grew drunk with the rasa of devotion.

**730.** Staying three days in the town of Venkata,
the Lord gave the Name of Hari guilelessly from house to house.

**731.** Men and women alike — all grew drunk,
and with them danced my Chaitanya Gosai.

**732.** Town and villages, boys and girls grew drunk;
who could keep count of how many came and went?

**733.** He gave everyone teachings on the truth of devotion,
and lifelong fools rolled at his feet.

**734.** When the Lord saw an unbeliever, he would embrace him first,
and having embraced him, say to him, "Say 'Hari, Hari'."

*[p. 68]*

**735.** There was a robber there named Pantha Bhil.
Hearing of him, the Lord went off towards him.

**736.** Everyone said, "Holy man, do not go there!
What if Pantha Bhil kills you?

**737.** Pantha Bhil is a villain with no sense at all;
finding you, he may do you some harm."

**738.** Heeding no one's words, Chaitanya Gosai
hurried towards Bagula, to Pantha Bhil.

**739.** Pantha Bhil lives in a forest called Bagula;
whenever he finds a traveller he brings him to ruin.

**740.** Ignoring every warning, into the fearful forest
the Lord went, to see the sport.[^04-54]

**741.** I followed behind with the water-pot,
and Chaitanya Gosai said nothing to me.

**742.** Finding the Lord, Pantha received him as a guest,
and Mahaprabhu stayed there three nights.

**743.** The Lord said, "Pantha, you are a worthy holy man;
seeing you, all my sins are destroyed.

**744.** You do not live in a house like a householder;
you are a supreme sadhu, a detached sannyasi.

**745.** You are no worm of worldliness like a householder;
I see your heart is content with whatever comes.

*[p. 69]*

**746.** You have no son, no daughter, no wife;
you are not drunk with worldly things and have no attachment.

**747.** Blessed are you, King Pantha, crest-jewel of sadhus!
Seeing you, my heart has become glad.

**748.** You regard worldly wealth as straw
and could give up all you have this very moment.

**749.** You do not live with a woman —
that is why I came here, to fulfil my wish.

**750.** You are always surrounded by disciples,
and on seeing you the heart thrills with joy.

**751.** Noble one, you are not bound by maya and delusion;
I think you are the best of sadhus."[^04-55]

**752.** Silently hearing the Lord's words,
he bowed down at once with devotion.

**753.** Hearing the Name of Hari from the Lord's lips again and again,
an ocean of devotion surged up in his heart.

**754.** The Bhil fell rolling at the Lord's feet,
and the Lord embraced him and let him hear the Name.

**755.** All the robbers, drunk with the Name of Hari,
turned that forest into a garden of joy.

**756.** From that day Pantha put on the kaupina
and became the best of sadhus, mature in knowledge.

*[p. 70]*

**757.** By the Lord's grace Pantha gave up his sinful deeds
and always went about dancing and chanting the Name of Hari.

**758.** As soon as he took Hari's name, tears would come.
The young sannyasi was drunk with joy.

**759.** All the robbers there were in the forest
gave up their wicked ways and together cried "Hari, Hari".

**760.** All together they grew drunk with joy in that forest;
for the Lord's sake they all gave up their sinful deeds.

**761.** Having thus purified Pantha Bhil,
my hero of dharma went on, floating in joy.

**762.** His body wasted by fasting, he can hardly walk,
yet still the Lord gives the Name of Hari from house to house.

**763.** The people of that country say "khai, mai",[^04-56]
yet still Chaitanya Gosai distributes the Name.

**764.** My Lord has no desires whatever,
yet wherever and whenever he goes, there is plenty of everything.

**765.** Whoever sees the Lord even once
no longer has the strength to go away.

**766.** Such is the Lord's power — what more can I say?
He has broken the dam of the ocean of devotion once again.

**767.** Surging up, the ocean of devotion flooded the land;
some became sannyasis, some dervishes.

*[p. 71]*

**768.** Some became detached Vaishnavas there,
some, turned aul and baul,[^04-57] dance in the courtyards.

**769.** Thus my Lord, drunk with the Name,
rolls on the ground, lost in ecstasy.

**770.** Sometimes he is like inert matter, with no outer awareness,
his body bristling like a kadamba flower.

**771.** His eyes half-closed, like a corpse;
no one has ever seen such a wondrous state.

**772.** Heedless of thorns and splinters, he falls to the ground;
think as I may, I cannot tell what mood he is in.

**773.** Three nights passed beneath a tree;
he fasted and ate nothing.

**774.** Tears streamed down over his breast;
call him a hundred times and not a word, like a madman.

**775.** Sometimes he rolls on the ground, naked,
and I carefully take him up in my arms.

**776.** On the fourth day a woman came
and showed hospitality, giving flour and parched grain.[^04-58]

**777.** Another old woman brought milk;
the Lord mixed the flour with the milk and made an offering.

**778.** Three kroshas from there is a temple
where a linga called Girishvara was installed by the Creator.

*[p. 72]*

**779.** People say that Vishvakarma built the temple
and the Grandsire[^04-59] worshipped Shiva with his own hands.

**780.** There stands a huge bilva tree,
its branches spreading over a quarter of a krosha.

**781.** I had heard that this tree bears no fruit;
going there, I saw that wonder for myself.

**782.** On three sides the temple is surrounded by hills,
and on the south side the bilva tree stands in splendour.

**783.** Gathering bilva leaves with his own hands, my Lord,
lost in love, offered them to Shiva by the handful.

**784.** Then, drunk with love, Gora Raya
flailed about and fell crashing to the ground.

**785.** Now laughing, now weeping, like a madman,
tears streaming unceasingly down his breast.

**786.** His hair standing on end, almost inert;
the wondrous state of love is beyond words.

**787.** The Lord has no desire; he is drunk with the Name of Hari.
Some two days passed in that abode of Shiva.

**788.** On the third day a sannyasi with matted hair
came down from the mountain top and showed himself.

**789.** That excellent sannyasi, keeping a vow of silence,
performed his worship and went back to the mountain top.

*[p. 73]*

**790.** That solitary sannyasi had nothing on his body;
whoever sees him, even a worldly man becomes detached.

**791.** When the Lord regained consciousness,
I told him everything about the sannyasi, one thing after another.

**792.** Hearing about the sannyasi, my Gora Raya
hurried up the mountain to see him.

**793.** I ran behind him in wonder,
and gradually we reached that place.

**794.** Climbing the mountain, we saw
that sannyasi gosai beneath a tree.

**795.** No cloth, no bowl — nothing beside him.
I stood behind Chaitanya.

**796.** The excellent sannyasi, sunk in meditation, had no outer awareness;
whoever saw him became virtuous.

**797.** However humbly Gora Raya spoke to him,
the sannyasi's meditation did not break.

**798.** Then the Lord, with folded hands, began a hymn of praise,
and at that the excellent sannyasi began to look at him.

**799.** Seeing the Lord, that sannyasi thakura
smiled, and great joy welled up in his heart.

**800.** Who knows why the sannyasi smiled;
a moment later the Lord sat down beside him.

*[p. 74]*

**801.** Two other detached sannyasis came there,
and the sannyasi wished to offer hospitality.

**802.** He brought fruits called parata,
and the Lord took two of them.

**803.** He gave me four fruits to eat.
"Unless it is prasada, I will not accept it," I said.

**804.** Hearing this, my Lord Chaitanya Gosai
made the fruits into prasada and gave them to me.

**805.** Parata fruits are very sweet, like nectar;
having eaten them, my mind grew restless.

**806.** These sinful eyes of mine, out of greed,
kept glancing again and again at the Lord's fruits.

**807.** Beautiful Gauranga smiled slightly
and held out two neem fruits[^04-60] to me.

**808.** "How shall I eat these fruits?" I was afraid,
and at once I remembered the son of Anjana.[^04-61]

**809.** Seeing me hesitate and not eat the fruits,
Chaitanya Gosai said with a smile:

**810.** "The stone won't stick in your throat, Govinda.
Don't play tricks when you are given prasada.

**811.** Your desire to eat the fruit has grown strong,
but you are beside yourself for fear of a stone getting stuck."

*[p. 75]*

**812.** When Gosai spoke out what was in my mind,
I at once put the fruit down and fell at his feet.

**813.** In the end, at the Lord's order, I had to eat them,
and the sannyasi came and brought two more fruits.

**814.** After eating, cupping my hands at the spring,
I drank water with a joyful heart.

**815.** The cool, crystal-clear water of the spring —
drinking it, my whole body was refreshed.

**816.** Drunk with the Name of Hari, the Lord was filled with love,
and his body bristled like a kadamba flower.

**817.** In the surge of love the knot of his matted hair came loose,
his feet tangled together and he fell.

**818.** His forehead was cut by a blow from a stone,
and streams of blood fell to the ground.

**819.** Saliva flows from his mouth, water from his nose;
Gora Raya lies like a lifeless thing.

**820.** Seeing this, devotion arose in the sannyasi;
he fell at the Lord's feet and began to weep.

**821.** His body like a charred log, no cloth on his limbs;
his matted hair came loose and he breathed heavily.

**822.** Tears streamed down through his beard,
and in love that charred log seemed to swell into blossom.

*[p. 76]*

**823.** Then, regaining consciousness, my excellent Lord
rose and sat up, grey with dust.

**824.** The excellent sannyasi was in a fever of agitation
and, gazing at the Lord, said, "You are God."

**825.** At the sannyasi's words the Lord put his hands over his ears
and said again and again, "Sannyasi, give up such talk."

**826.** The sannyasi said, "You are by no means a man."
The Lord said, "Sannyasi, you are my God.

**827.** Wondrous is your love for God;
seeing you, even the unbeliever remembers him.

**828.** No cloth, no bowl, no craving for wealth —
millions of obeisances to your feet.

**829.** You are not subject to earthly pleasures;
beside you even heaven seems paltry."

**830.** Then the Lord went to the town of Tripadi[^04-62]
and, seeing the image of Sri Rama, fell in the dust.

**831.** Many Ramat Vaishnavas[^04-63] live there,
and they go about every which way looking for debate.

**832.** A Ramat pandit named Mathura
was known in the town as a great disputant.

**833.** He came before the Lord and demanded a debate,
and the Lord, with folded hands and shrinking, replied:

*[p. 77]*

**834.** "Mathura Thakura, I do not know how to debate;
a hundred times I admit defeat before you.

**835.** You are a devotee of Sri Rama, a Vaishnava gosai;
by serving you, how many truths I shall learn!

**836.** You are a detached Ramat, yet ruled by the lust for victory —
why smear soot with both hands over your spotless name?[^04-64]

**837.** Tell us something of the truth, so that, hearing it,
the people may be purified by your words.

**838.** I hear you are very skilled in logic,
but there is no merit at all in dry logic.

**839.** The truth of God, the truth of the soul, the doctrine of maya —
explain them and let us taste the nectar.

**840.** Explain what makes living beings fulfilled,
and tell us that, worthy sir.

**841.** There is no need for much debate and wrangling;
kindly tell me the subtle truth."

**842.** As he was speaking, the Lord cried "Hari bol!"
and, growing excited, became drunk with the Name.

**843.** Where was his cloth, where his upper wrap?
His hair standing on end, he breathed fast.

**844.** Then he fell crashing to the ground
and lost consciousness, the Lord becoming almost inert.

*[p. 78]*

**845.** All the Ramats, seeing that state,
surrounded the Lord and began to dance.

**846.** Some said, "This sannyasi is no man";
some fell at his feet and rolled on the ground.

**847.** After that he left the place,
and Mathura ran after him a long way.

**848.** Smiling, he sent Mathuranatha away,
and the Lord hurried on to see Pana-Narasimha.[^04-65]

**849.** Lord Nrisimha is offered a sugar drink as bhoga,
and so everyone calls him Pana-Narasimha.

**850.** The merciful Lord sang a hymn to Nrisimha,
and all the people were amazed to see it.

**851.** Nrisimha's priest is Madhavendra Bhuiyan;[^04-66]
every day he comes and worships Lord Nrisimha.

**852.** He brought a tulasi garland and put it round the Lord's neck,
and wearing the garland, my Lord said "Hari, Hari".

**853.** The pujari quickly brought some prasada,
and the Lord took just a particle in his hand.

**854.** Holding the prasada in his hand, he sang its praises,
and as he took the prasada tears flowed from both his eyes.

**855.** He had sugar drink brought for me,
and I drank and drank until my belly was full.

*[p. 79]*

**856.** Nrisimha's drink is like nectar;
whoever sees Lord Nrisimha realises the state of Brahman.

**857.** With closed eyes, the Lord says the Name of Hari;
gradually we arrived at the abode of Vishnukanchi.

**858.** At Vishnukanchi a sheth[^04-67] named Bhavabhuti
serves Lakshmi-Narayana with great care.

**859.** The sheth is a great devotee, a crest-jewel of saints;
his very life is given to Lakshmi-Narayana.

**860.** Every day the worthy sheth serves with devotion
and spends much money on that service.

**861.** His wife washes the temple every day;
that crest-jewel of saints is always busy with service.

**862.** Every day sweet rice is made from two maunds of thickened milk,
and how many wandering ascetics go to receive the prasada!

**863.** Seeing Lakshmi-Narayana, beautiful Gauranga
bowed down and sang long hymns of praise.

**864.** Six kroshas from Lakshmi-Narayana
stands Shiva Trikaleshvara in the open plain.

**865.** His gauripatta[^04-68] measures four cubits;
seeing that Shiva, the Lord was astonished.

**866.** From there Pakshigiri[^04-69] can be seen;
below it is Pakshitirtha, where the river Bhadra flows.

*[p. 80]*

**867.** Beautiful Gauranga bathed in that place
and ate the champi fruits that I obtained by begging.

**868.** We lay down to sleep beneath a tree,
and in the night a tiger attacked us.

**869.** Seeing its snarling and roaring, my Gora-chand
smiled and spread the snare of the Name of Hari.

**870.** Hearing the cry of "Hari!", the tiger tucked in its tail
and with one bound retreated into the forest.

**871.** Having seen this wondrous power with my own eyes,
I took up the dust of his feet and placed it on my head.

**872.** Five kroshas from the bank of the Bhadra
is a holy place called Kalatirtha.

**873.** There is an image of Lord Varaha of wondrous workmanship,
seeing which the sages and rishis are enchanted.

**874.** Having had darshan, the Lord bowed down,
and a pujari with a happy face brought him a garland.

**875.** Receiving the sanctified garland, the Lord was thrilled at heart;
his body began to tremble and his eyes to stream.

**876.** The tears spurted out like a squirt-gun,
and, sobbing, the Lord was quite overcome.

**877.** Five kroshas to the south is Sandhitirtha;[^04-70]
the Lord set off, and I followed behind.

---

### Translator's notes

[^04-52]: A krosha (krosh) is a measure of distance of about 3–3.5 km.
[^04-53]: *Dvaita-advaita*, the doctrine of "unity in difference", as opposed to the strict monism (*advaita-vada*) of the Shankara school.
[^04-54]: "To see the sport": Govinda stresses Chaitanya's fearless playfulness.
[^04-55]: The whole speech is ironic: Chaitanya praises the robber for "virtues" that are really his vices (he has no family and is indifferent to others' property), and so disarms him.
[^04-56]: "Khai, mai" imitates an unfamiliar language (Telugu or Kannada) that a Bengali could not understand.
[^04-57]: Auls and bauls are Bengali wandering ecstatic singers and ascetics; Govinda applies to the South the categories familiar to him.
[^04-58]: *Chuna*: probably parched or crushed grain.
[^04-59]: The Grandsire (*Pitamaha*) is Brahma.
[^04-60]: Neem (*nimba*) is a bitter fruit; Govinda is apparently afraid of its hard stone.
[^04-61]: The son of Anjana is Hanuman, whom Govinda invokes in his fright.
[^04-62]: Tripadi is Tirupati.
[^04-63]: The Ramats (Ramanandis) are Vaishnavas who worship Rama.
[^04-64]: Literally, "why smear ink with both hands over your whiteness?"
[^04-65]: Pana-Narasimha is the Nrisimha temple at Mangalagiri (Andhra Pradesh), where the deity is offered the sweet drink *pana*.
[^04-66]: *Bhuiyan* (*bhuñā*) is probably a surname or a landholder's title.
[^04-67]: A *sheth* (*shethi*) is a merchant.
[^04-68]: The *gauripatta* is the base (yoni) of the Shiva-linga.
[^04-69]: Pakshigiri and Pakshitirtha are Tirukkalukundram, the "hill of birds" near Chengalpattu, famous for the legend that two eagles come there every day to be fed.
[^04-70]: The name is corrupt in the edition (*sahita ghaṁ*); probably the next tirtha listed in the index of the 1926 edition.

---

# Part 5

*Sandhitirtha, the Kaveri, Nagar. Tanjore. Padmakot and the healing of the blind man. Tripatra and the Shaiva Bharga. The great forest. Srirangam and the brahmana who reads the Gita (pp. 81–100)*

*[p. 81]*

**878.** There the two rivers Nanda and Bhadra meet;
going there, he bathed at their confluence.

**879.** The master of that tirtha is Sadananda Puri;
he turned out to be a very learned pandit.

**880.** Sadananda Puri raised the doctrine of advaita,
but with a single argument the Lord broke all Puri's pride.

**881.** In the end Sadananda, amazed,
fell rolling at the Lord's feet in a surge of devotion.

**882.** Having given him the truth of devotion, my sannyasi
went to Chaipalla-tirtha to see its ways.

**883.** The dwellers of that tirtha are very virtuous;
there the sannyasi, son of Shachi, arrived.

**884.** There was a beautiful bhairavi[^05-71] named Siddheshvari,
radiant, a great ascetic, like Maheshvari herself.

**885.** From austerities she was nothing but skin and bone;
she sat at the foot of a bilva tree, repeating her mantra.

**886.** She sat motionless, absorbed in meditation;
seeing her, a sinner gains much knowledge.

**887.** She had reached a hundred years of age,
yet no one could tell it from her appearance.

**888.** There was another, a bhairavi named Shrigali;
she lived on the bank of the river.

*[p. 82]*

**889.** Having reverently seen Shrigali,
the son of Shachi went on to the bank of the Kaveri.

**890.** Having bathed in the Kaveri, young Gauranga
became lost in drinking the nectar of the Name of Hari.

**891.** In the afternoon he told me to go begging,
and I went into the town for alms.

**892.** Collecting a little parched grain and flour,
I brought them and set them before the Lord.

**893.** The Lord made rotis and offered the bhoga,
and on receiving the prasada I was refreshed.

**894.** My merciful Lord, in the town of Nagar,
rose in the morning and went on, full of love for Krishna.

**895.** Smeared with dust, hair matted, with no other talk,
Nimai goes along the road saying "Krishna, Krishna".

**896.** In the town of Nagar there are Sri Rama and Lakshmana;
going there, the Lord bowed to them.

**897.** Many people live in Nagar,
and there he made the Name of Hari manifest.

**898.** Seeing the movement of the Lord's love, the townsfolk,
from children to women, all turned from worldly things.

**899.** For three days he sang and danced there,
and the news of it spread from town to town.

*[p. 83]*

**900.** People gathered from ten kroshas around,
and one by one the Lord gave them all the Name of Hari.

**901.** Never have I seen so merciful a Lord:
Chaitanya Gosai gives the Name from house to house.

**902.** There was a wicked brahmana there;
calling the Lord a fraud, he tried to drive him away.

**903.** That brahmana thakura with his gang
shouted at the merciful Lord, "Away, away, away!"

**904.** The brahmana thakura said, "You cheat!
Dressed up as a false sannyasi, you're making trouble!

**905.** On the pretext of religious teaching you are ruining the village people.
I'll expose you and drive you out this very minute!"

**906.** He came before the Lord and heaped abuse on him,
and the Lord laughed his harsh words away.

**907.** At last Chaitanya Gosai called the brahmana
and said, "Beat me, brother, but say 'Hari'."

**908.** All the other people who were around him,
seeing the brahmana's behaviour, rushed to beat him.

**909.** Merciful Chaitanyadeva, reflecting,
began to speak to the brahmana, addressing him:

**910.** "Listen, merciful brahmana thakura:
say 'Hari, Hari' and you will find great happiness.

*[p. 84]*

**911.** In this transient body there is no happiness;
sink yourself in the Name of Hari and rejoice, brother.

**912.** This body is an inert lump; at the time of death
no one will go with you — this is certain.

**913.** Brothers, friends, wife, sons — no one belongs to anyone;
all of them are clothes, ornaments and heaps of money.

**914.** Jackals and dogs will eat the transient body;
it will rot and decay — be sure of this.

**915.** Raise your arms, say 'Hari' and dance with me,
and you will never again have to go to Yama's abode.

**916.** Wife or son — it is a juggler's game,
a gathering in the world for a couple of days.

**917.** The family plays tricks for the sake of food.
Think, brother: who is who to you?

**918.** Throwing the noose of love round his neck, the woman pulls hard,
and by that pull the foolish master of the house dies.

**919.** Sweet words on her lips and poison in her heart;
when she gets no money, she grinds her teeth.[^05-72]

**920.** She never lets him take the path of truth;
she binds him in bonds and destroys all his aspirations.

**921.** A woman's love is like poison,
yet the fool drinks it, calling it nectar.

*[p. 85]*

**922.** At the hour of death sons and daughters come near
and say, 'Father, what have you left for us?'

**923.** Think of all this, good brother brahmana,
and say 'Hari' with devotion — this is the alms I beg.

**924.** Strike me — that is no sorrow;
say 'Hari' with all your heart — this is the alms I beg.

**925.** Say 'Hari' with devotion; the Name will go with you,
and through it you will gain eternal happiness for endless time."

**926.** All the people who stood around
grew excited at the Lord's words.

**927.** Crying "Hari bol!", they all began to dance,
and the heart of the unbelieving brahmana was purified.

**928.** By the Lord's grace the brahmana grew drunk with the Name of Hari
and fell to the ground at the Lord's feet.

**929.** Thus having blessed the brahmana,
Chaitanyadeva left Nagar.

**930.** As he set out, the best of sannyasis
signalled me to get up quickly.

**931.** I tied the two pairs of sandals onto my head
and took the two loads on my two shoulders.

**932.** How many respectable women ran to see the Lord!
He went on to the town of Tanjore, seven kroshas away.

*[p. 86]*

**933.** In Tanjore lives a Vaishnava brahmana
named Dhaleshvara, who serves Krishna.

**934.** In his temple there are images of Radha and Krishna,
and there my Gora went slowly.

**935.** In the middle of the brahmana Dhaleshvara's courtyard
stands a huge bakula tree in its splendour.

**936.** Many Vaishnava sannyasis live there;
whoever sees that place, even a householder, becomes detached.

**937.** To its left is the Shiva Gosamaja;
the Lord had darshan of Shiva with love.

**938.** Nearby was a delightful lake,
and the brahmana Dhaleshvara showed him the way.

**939.** The lake is at Kumbhakarna-kapara;[^05-73]
seeing it, the Lord was amazed.

**940.** Near it is a hill called Chandalu,
standing as if painted on a canvas.

**941.** All round it are many caves,
where many sannyasis live to practise austerities.

**942.** How many sannyasi gosais, absorbed in meditation,
sit with closed eyes and ash-smeared bodies!

**943.** There an excellent brahmana named Bhatta
took the Lord to his own house.

*[p. 87]*

**944.** Hearing the name of Krishna, the brahmana went mad,
and merciful Chaitanya bestowed his grace on him.

**945.** The worthy Bhatta is always drunk with the Name of Hari;
when he takes Krishna's name, tears fall.

**946.** Seeing his ecstasy of love, beautiful Gauranga
said, "Brahmana, you are the best of sadhus.

**947.** Seeing you, even great sins are destroyed.
You, worthy brahmana, are my master;

**948.** seeing you, no sorrow or suffering remains."
At the praise the brahmana was deeply ashamed,

**949.** and he ran and fell at the Lord's feet.
He said, "Lord, why do you embarrass me so?

**950.** Words of praise only increase this wretch's pain.
I am a worm of hell, the crest-jewel of sinners;

**951.** you yourself have saved me by your grace.
Whoever touches me goes to hell;

**952.** today by your grace my sins are destroyed."
Seeing the brahmana's humility, the son of Shachi

**953.** said, "Brahmana, you are blessed, you are a holy man."
In an ecstasy of love the Lord dances in the brahmana's house,

**954.** and seeing it the brahmana rejoices at heart.[^05-74]

*[p. 88]*

**955.** There was a chief sannyasi named Sureshvara
who served Hari unceasingly.

**956.** Six others were under him;
all of them, detached, worship in the forest.

**957.** Huge trees stand in splendour all around;
the wondrous beauty of that forest is beyond words.

**958.** In the middle of that forest is a little river,
most charming, flowing with a murmuring sound.

**959.** The waters of the springs all join together
and become a river cutting through the forest.

**960.** There they all live and go nowhere,
and the village people bring them alms there.

**961.** That lovely place is a great holy land,
and there Mahaprabhu went on.

**962.** Seeing the Lord, that detached sannyasi
thrilled with joy, floating in bliss.

**963.** Gora Raya stayed there for some days,
and, drunk with bliss, sang the glories of Hari.

**964.** Then the sannyasi Sureshvara, amazed,
danced with the Lord, floating in the waters of love.

**965.** That place is in the kingdom of King Jaysingh;
the king takes no tax from sannyasis.

*[p. 89]*

**966.** That place is like the abode of Vaikuntha;
when one enters it, the heart is soothed.

**967.** Leaving that forest, the son of Shachi
went on to see the tirtha of Padmakot.

**968.** The goddess of Padmakot is the eight-armed Bhagavati;
going there, the Lord bowed down to her.

**969.** My Gora Raya sang many hymns,
and hundreds of people ran to see him.

**970.** Sitting there, the Lord gave teachings,
and hundreds of people gathered there.

**971.** The Lord said, "Brothers, all of you chant the Name of Hari;
by the power of the Name, brothers, you will all reach the eternal abode.

**972.** Tell me, what need is there of the inert body?
When you die, jackals and crows will eat it.

**973.** All of you have fallen into the net of maya;
tear the net, brothers, by chanting the Name of Hari.

**974.** Whose daughter, whose son — all a false pretence;
saying 'mine, mine', everyone has lost his senses.

**975.** Whose are you, who is yours, who is kin and who a stranger?
Maya is playing like a juggler.

**976.** Those who harbour worldly desires in this world

*[p. 90]*

suffer much torment, coming and going.

**977.** In the womb they live among excrement
and satisfy their cravings by eating faeces and urine.

**978.** Those who take the inert body to be consciousness —
how will they get out of hell?

**979.** Those who take the part for the whole
dwell forever in hell.

**980.** Not knowing that worldly life is a cruel trap,
people fall into that trap and suffer much grief.

**981.** The soul has no death; the sinful body dies.
Yet, deluded and enchanted by maya, the creature loves the body."

**982.** At this teaching everyone was amazed,
and the eight-armed goddess seemed to tremble.

**983.** As soon as the cry of "Hari" came from Lord Chaitanya's lips,
at once an echo rang out all around.

**984.** Boys, girls and young men went wild,
and the eight-armed goddess seemed to sway.

**985.** The fragrance of lotus began to waft all around,
and suddenly a rain of flowers fell there.

**986.** All the women threw flowers,
playing with flowers out of devotion.

**987.** There was a blind holy man there;
with devotion he clasped the Lord's feet.

*[p. 91]*

**988.** The Lord said, "Let me go, best of sadhus."
The blind man said, "Have mercy, Lord of the world."

**989.** The Lord said, "The Lady of the world is here."[^05-75]
The blind man said, "Have mercy on the wretched, Hari.

**990.** Have mercy on me, merciful Lord;
my heart weeps because I cannot see your form.

**991.** I am a blind wretch; I cannot see.
Show me your form, Chaitanya Gosai."

**992.** The Lord said, "You have no fleshly eyes,
but with the eye of knowledge you see everyone's heart.

**993.** The ignorant see with their eyes;
the wise see everything with eyes closed.

**994.** You are that wise one, worthy blind man;
I believe you see everything within."

**995.** The blind man said, "Why do you play tricks, treasury of compassion?
Have mercy on me as a blind man; you are Bhagavan.

**996.** For many years I have been lying in the temple,
and in a dream Bhagavati made it clear to me.

**997.** You are that Bhagavan, the refuge of the helpless —
so Bhagavati told me in a dream.

**998.** Then I shall know you, merciful one,
if you show your form to the blind.

*[p. 92]*

**999.** The feet of an ant uproot mountains,
the lame cross the Himalayas, remembering you.

**1000.** By your grace Agastya drank up the ocean,
and Prahlada did not die when he drank poison.

**1001.** In the form of cloth you saved Draupadi's honour;
you gave sight to the blind Bilvamangala."

**1002.** Hearing the blind man's words, Chaitanya Gosai
said, "Brother, why do you make me an offender?

**1003.** Hari dwells in every heart;
ask her and see — Bhagavati will tell you.

**1004.** What you have said is not to be heard.
Why make me an offender for nothing, brother?

**1005.** I am an ordinary man, a lowly sinner;
your heart has fallen into the well of illusion."

**1006.** The blind man said, "There is no more use in words;
show me your form — this is the alms I beg."

**1007.** The blind man wept in distress for the Lord,
and the Lord went over to the blind man.

**1008.** Seeing the blind man's devotion, beautiful Gauranga
slowly took his hand.

**1009.** Spreading his arms, Gora embraced the blind man,
and at the Lord's touch the blind man shuddered.

*[p. 93]*

**1010.** Opening his eyes as quick as lightning,
the blind man saw the Lord and was fulfilled.

**1011.** The very moment he saw my hero of dharma,
the blind man fell and gave up his body.

**1012.** Crying "Hari bol!" and circling the blind man,
the Lord began to dance, mad with love.

**1013.** Having buried the blind man's body[^05-76] in that very courtyard,
Gauranga left Padmakot.

**1014.** Leaving Padmakot, the Lord, in the town of Tripatra,[^05-77]
had darshan of Shiva Chandeshvara.

**1015.** If one cries "Bom!" in his temple,
the echo of the sound goes round for a whole danda.[^05-78]

**1016.** In that courtyard is a huge bilva tree;
everyone calls it the perfected bilva tree.

**1017.** Many Shaivas live there,
and their leader is the most learned Bhargadeva.

**1018.** Bhargadeva is a great scholar of philosophy;
every day he worships Hara with joy.

**1019.** There my Lord, the son of Shachi,
closed his eyes and sang hymns with devotion.

**1020.** Old Bhargadeva, seeing the son of Shachi,
called all the renunciants and said:

*[p. 94]*

**1021.** "You have all heard of a wonderful sannyasi
who is wandering this country in quest of holy places.

**1022.** Everyone tells of his marvellous greatness;
this is that sannyasi, the son of Shachi.

**1023.** In his eyes there is always the shambhavi mudra;[^05-79]
seeing him, no sinner or sufferer remains.

**1024.** By the gift of the nectar of the Name he has flooded the land
and made children, women and the old drunk with the Name.

**1025.** I have heard that he has left his own country
and come to deliver the unbelievers by giving them the Name of Hari.

**1026.** Look, brothers, here is that young sannyasi;
this is the one everyone calls Chaitanya Gosai.

**1027.** As I heard of him, so today I have seen him.
Ah, what a form! I have never seen anything like it.

**1028.** This best of sannyasis is not a man;
otherwise why should my heart melt at the sight of him?

**1029.** This person is an incarnation of God;
all of you bow down, clasping his feet."

**1030.** Saying this, Bharga bowed down,
and the Lord bit his tongue and drew back.

**1031.** The Lord said, "For shame, Bharga, what have you said!
My birthplace is the town of Nadia.

*[p. 95]*

**1032.** I am an ordinary man, that is certain.
Why do you frighten me for nothing by calling me an avatara?

**1033.** Why do you all again and again call me
an incarnation of God and make me an offender?

**1034.** I have come to you on pilgrimage.
Raise your arms, brothers, say 'Hari' and dance, all of you.

**1035.** Why make a fuss, calling me an avatara?
Come, let us all together say 'Hari, Hari bol'.

**1036.** Never call me an incarnation of God.
You yourself are Shankara in person, lord of the world."

**1037.** The Lord returned the obeisance with folded palms,
and Bhargadeva started up in fright.

**1038.** Bharga rolled at his feet,
fallen on the ground, grey with dust.

**1039.** Bharga said, "Listen, listen, Chaitanya Gosai:
have mercy on me, an old man — this is the alms I beg.

**1040.** I know nothing of worship or spiritual practice;
I am always proud of being a detached sannyasi.

**1041.** Go and play your tricks, Lord,
on someone who has not seen through the cunning of your lila.

**1042.** Go to someone who has not recognised you,
and there hide your form by your stratagems.

*[p. 96]*

**1043.** I am old, and because of the defect of my eyes my sight is dim;
that is why I see you as a dark-complexioned youth.[^05-80]

**1044.** People say your colour is like gold,
but this unfortunate one sees you dark, as the fruit of his fate.

**1045.** Just once, be merciful, Chaitanya Gosai,
and show your form, if I may be granted to see it.

**1046.** Be merciful, Lord, and give me the gift of sight;
be kind and make me fortunate.

**1047.** If in your mercy you have shown yourself to this lowly one,
lift your foot and place it on my head."

**1048.** Hearing the old man's words, Shachi's son
said, "Why do you make me an offender again and again?

**1049.** I came here to see a holy man;
never mind merit, I have become a partaker of sin."

**1050.** Hearing this, Bharga folded his hands
and said, "Take your meal here today — that is my request."

**1051.** The Lord stayed a week in the town of Tripatra,
and many people gathered there.

**1052.** For seven days the Lord performed Hari-sankirtana,
and everyone became drunk with the Name of Hari.

**1053.** Many people there became Vaishnavas,
and many swung tulasi garlands round their necks.

*[p. 97]*

**1054.** What more can I say about my Lord?
Wondrous is his power, marvellous his form.

**1055.** At the end of the day Gora Raya eats a little food;
from not eating, his body is as thin as a stick.

**1056.** He is nothing but skin and bone,
and yet the radiance of his body is like fire.

**1057.** All are enchanted by the beauty of his body;
without any effort, the fragrance of lotus always comes from it.

**1058.** Whoever opens his eyes and looks at him
is dazzled by the power of his effulgence.

**1059.** After seven days, having bestowed his grace on Bharga,
my sannyasi left Tripatra.

**1060.** Bharga follows behind as a companion;
taking Bhargadeva by the hand, he sent him back.

**1061.** Hundreds of thousands come to see the Lord,
and the Lord never tires of giving Krishna's name.

**1062.** No one speaks of anything but the Name of Hari;
seeing so many devotees of Krishna, all are beside themselves.

**1063.** Everyone calls the Lord "the mad Hari-bola",[^05-81]
and many cry "Hari bol!" to tease him.

**1064.** How many people run after him crying "Hari"!
Hearing the Name, my Lord smears his body with dust.

*[p. 98]*

**1065.** Gone mad with the Name of Hari, Gora-chand
now and then falls in the dust and rolls about.

**1066.** When the Lord sent Bhargadeva away,
just then many children came to that place.

**1067.** Someone said, "Hey, brothers, there goes that madman!
All of you shout 'Hari, Hari' and drive him wild!"

**1068.** All the children began to tease him,
and the Lord, the son of Shachi, danced along with them.

**1069.** Sometimes he laughs, sometimes he weeps,
sometimes he falls and drops to the ground.

**1070.** Gradually all the people went away who knows where,
and in our path lay a vast forest.

**1071.** There is no end to it — thickets for fifty yojanas,
and into it the son of Shachi went.

**1072.** With no fear in my heart I walk along the footpath;
ahead goes my Chaitanya Gosai.

**1073.** We stay under trees; there is no one there.
We satisfy our hunger by eating the fruits of the trees.

**1074.** How can I tell how many wondrous fruits there were!
I carefully eat those fruits that surpass nectar.

**1075.** After three days we came upon a band of sannyasis,
and I was greatly delighted.

*[p. 99]*

**1076.** Joining them, we went on slowly,
and after a fortnight we came out of the forest.

**1077.** Beyond the forest lies the pure Ranga-dhama;[^05-82]
going there, the Lord gives the Name of Hari.

**1078.** In Ranga-dhama is the image of Lord Narasimha;
seeing it, devotion arises even in an unbeliever's heart.

**1079.** Prahlada stands before him with joined palms,
while the Lord slays the king of the demons.

**1080.** Never have I seen such an image.
Seeing it, Chaitanya Gosai went mad.

**1081.** The son of Shachi now falls, now rises,
now the Lord is sunk in meditation with eyes closed.

**1082.** Seeing Nrisimha, the ocean of love surges up,
and now and then he falls crashing to the ground.

**1083.** Sometimes the Lord, like a madman, babbles incoherently,
and foam comes from his mouth in gushes.

**1084.** Sometimes his upper cloth is soaked with tears,
sometimes he falls to the ground, trembling.

**1085.** Some say, "Where has this madman come from?"
Some come and fall at the Lord's feet.

**1086.** There was a practising brahmana named Yudhishthira,
the crest-jewel of Vaishnavas, saintly in conduct.

*[p. 100]*

**1087.** The brahmana worships and serves in this holy place,
and every day by reading the Gita frees himself from the fever of the world.[^05-83]

**1088.** Everyone laughs at the unlettered brahmana reading the Gita,
but the brahmana pays no heed, and that is why he was dear to the Lord.

**1089.** He heeds no one as he studies the Gita;
setting his heart on it, he reads in solitude.

**1090.** As long as he reads the Gita, the brahmana weeps;
seeing his tears, the Lord's heart melted.

**1091.** The Lord said, "Why do you weep, brahmana thakura?"
The brahmana said, "When I read the Gita, I am full of joy.

**1092.** I see Krishna on Arjuna's chariot;
for the delight of that I read the Gita, sannyasi gosai."

**1093.** The Lord said, "You get to see Krishna —
then be kind and embrace me.

**1094.** I have never seen a holy man like you;
by serving you I shall get to see Krishna."

**1095.** The brahmana gazed fixedly at the Lord
and fell prostrate at the Lord's feet.

**1096.** The Lord said, "Listen, listen, worthy brahmana:
do not tell this to anyone and everyone.

**1097.** You are most fortunate, crest-jewel of saints;
Krishna himself shows himself to you every day."

---

### Translator's notes

[^05-71]: A *bhairavi* is a female ascetic of the Shaiva or Tantric tradition.
[^05-72]: Literally "makes *khish-mish*", an expression of grumbling discontent.
[^05-73]: Kumbhakarna-kapara ("Kumbhakarna's skull") is a local name; the index of the 1926 edition lists *Kumbhakarna-karpara*.
[^05-74]: The division of lines into couplets seems to be disturbed in the edition here; the translation follows the order of the lines.
[^05-75]: Chaitanya hints that mercy should be asked of the goddess of the temple.
[^05-76]: *Samadhi*, the burial of a holy man (the bodies of sadhus are buried, not cremated).
[^05-77]: Tripatra is perhaps Tiruvattur or Tiruppattur; the identification is uncertain.
[^05-78]: A *danda* is a measure of time, 24 minutes.
[^05-79]: The *shambhavi mudra* is the yogic "gaze of Shambhu", in which the open eyes do not see the outer world.
[^05-80]: Govinda alludes to the traditional idea of Chaitanya as Krishna concealed by Radha's golden colour: Bharga sees him dark, like Krishna.
[^05-81]: *Khepa Hari-bola*, "the madman who shouts 'Hari bol'".
[^05-82]: Ranga-dhama is Srirangam.
[^05-83]: Compare the famous episode of the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 9) about the illiterate brahmana at Srirangam who weeps over the Gita because he sees Krishna on Arjuna's chariot. In the *Karcha* this brahmana is named Yudhishthira.

---

# Part 6

*Rishabha-parvata, Ramnad, Rameshvaram. The silent ascetic of Madhavi-vana. The Tamraparni and Kanyakumari. Travancore and King Rudrapati. Shringeri, the source of the Kaveri, Chandapur: the debate with Ishvara Bharati (pp. 101–122)*

*[p. 101]*

**1098.** The brahmana said, "You are Krishna; you have fulfilled me,"
and saying this he clasped both his feet tightly.

**1099.** On leaving, the Lord told the brahmana [to stay],
but the brahmana left everything and ran after the Lord.

**1100.** Sending the brahmana back, the son of Shachi
then went on to the Rishabha mountain.

**1101.** On the Rishabha mountain lives Paramananda Puri;
the Lord hastened to see him.

**1102.** He spoke much of Krishna with Puri,
and then came to the town of Ramnath.

**1103.** In the town of Ramnath are Rama's footprints;
seeing them, the Lord shed tears.

**1104.** His body filled with thrills and began to tremble,
and the Lord fell senseless to the ground.

**1105.** Touching the lotus feet, my merciful one
shudders again and again, breathing hard.

**1106.** Having seen the lotus feet, the son of Shachi
went on to see other holy places.

**1107.** Going to the tirtha of Rameshvara and bathing there,
my Gaura-Hari had darshan of Shiva.

**1108.** The Shiva named Rameshvara is of wondrous form;
seeing Shiva, Mahaprabhu bowed to him.

*[p. 102]*

**1109.** Many sadhus always live there,
and then all the sadhus came one by one.

**1110.** Seeing the Lord, a learned renunciant
became very eager to debate.

**1111.** The Lord said, "I do not wish to debate;
I admit I have been defeated by you in argument."

**1112.** Seeing his wondrous humility with his own eyes,
the sannyasi was dumbfounded and pondered inwardly.

**1113.** The Lord said, "What are you thinking, sannyasi thakura?
Put away all this idle speculation.[^06-84]

**1114.** Putting idle speculation aside, with devotion
sing the glories of Krishna, brother, with a pure heart.

**1115.** Worship Krishna, speak of Krishna, take Krishna's name;
taking Krishna's name, go to the eternal abode.

**1116.** There is no way but Krishna — this is my counsel;
coming and going again and again, you will suffer.

**1117.** What is the use of pride, good man?
What need is there to become a pandit in debate?

**1118.** A sinful pandit builds his house in hell —
so everyone says, so the shastras have written.

**1119.** One who knows many shastras yet lives by his whims —
what will that fool achieve by debating?

*[p. 103]*

**1120.** One who cheats for the sake of money —
that unbeliever does not understand the words of scripture.

**1121.** For one whose mind is busy with women and gold,
the study of the Vedas is a mockery.

**1122.** One in whose heart envy is always at play
is punished by the lord of the ancestors[^06-85] with his own hand.

**1123.** One whose heart melts at the Name of Hari —
he, in my view, is the great pandit.

**1124.** One from whose eyes streams of bliss flow
when he chants the Name of Hari — he is called truly learned.

**1125.** One who, however much he reads and hears, has no taste for Krishna —
he, brother, is a fool and always impure."

**1126.** Hearing these words from the Lord's lips,
the yogi stayed silent for some time.

**1127.** All the detached sannyasis, surrounding the Lord,
listened to his words, lost to themselves.

**1128.** At last Gora-chand raised both his arms
and, drunk with the Name of Hari, toppled over.

**1129.** Lord Chaitanya fell crashing down,
and the blow of a stone cut his chin.

**1130.** Blood began to flow in streams,
and the excellent pandit carefully wiped it away.

*[p. 104]*

**1131.** After three days of kirtana at Setubandha,
he went to the left to see Madhavi-vana.

**1132.** In Madhavi-vana lives an ascetic under a vow of silence;
my beggar[^06-86] went to see him.

**1133.** That sannyasi has a wondrous radiant form,
his white beard covering his chest.

**1134.** His long nails have curled over;
he sits in silence, naked.

**1135.** No cloth, no staff, no water-pot beside him.
Chaitanya Gosai gazed at him steadily.

**1136.** He is utterly serene, his eyes closed;
the tree is his house, the sky his clothing.

**1137.** He has no desires and is absorbed in austerity;
my Lord stood before him with folded hands.

**1138.** Chaitanya spoke many humble words and praises,
yet the excellent sannyasi did not even look round.

**1139.** Every three days all the renunciants
bring him alms of fruits and roots.
