**1140.** Every three days that great yogi
takes food and so sustains his life.

**1141.** Breaking his meditation, the excellent yogi looked round,
and at that moment the Lord began to speak.

*[p. 105]*

**1142.** The yogi understood nothing of the Lord's words,
and then he conversed with him in Sanskrit.

**1143.** The worthy yogi listened calmly to his words
and spoke a few words with the Lord.

**1144.** Having spoken a few words, the great yogi
cried "Chambani shiori!"[^06-87] and laughed.

**1145.** Crying "Chambani shiori!", with a wholly pure heart
he bowed with a smile at the Lord's feet.

**1146.** My Gora Raya bowed in return
and, floating in joy, sang the glories of Krishna.

**1147.** Seeing the excellent yogi bow down,
all the sannyasis then clasped the Lord's feet.

**1148.** Having spent time there in spiritual conversation,[^06-88] Gora Raya
left that place after seven days.

**1149.** There is a tirtha called Tattvakundi[^06-89] there;
the Lord went there to bathe.

**1150.** Then the river Tamraparni came into view,
and the Lord went there to bathe.

**1151.** On the full-moon day of Magha, on the bank of the Tamraparni,
many pilgrims come to bathe.

**1152.** Waiting there a fortnight,
on the full moon of Magha we went and bathed.

*[p. 106]*

**1153.** Crossing the Tamraparni, the Lord
went along the seashore to see Kanyakumari.

**1154.** There are no hills, forests or villages there;
only the sound of the sea can be heard.

**1155.** Huge waves, rolling in there,
seem to be singing the praises of God consciously.

**1156.** Seeing that, the heart is gladdened;
with the rising of feeling, his body thrilled.

**1157.** The sand, heaped up like mountains,
seems to proclaim the glories of God.

**1158.** The sea roars unceasingly, "hoo, hoo";
what more can I say — everything there is beautiful.

**1159.** There is nothing to see, yet it is lovely;
there beauty is seen by one whose mind is pure.

**1160.** Calling me, "Govinda!",
the Lord, with a slight smile, told me to bathe.

**1161.** Waves like mountains come rushing in;
there he bathed with devotion.

**1162.** After bathing, my Lord weeps, crying "Hari";
the love of his heart seemed to overflow.

**1163.** His hair stood on end, his brow broke into sweat,
and his emaciated body was filled with thrills.

*[p. 107]*

**1164.** After bathing, Gora Raya pondered
and, calling me, said, "Which way will you go?"

**1165.** I said, "Whichever way the Lord goes,
that way the servant will go, to serve him."

**1166.** After bathing, a large band of sannyasis
turned back towards Mount Sitala.

**1167.** Nimai joined them and went along,
and I followed behind like a shadow.

**1168.** After going fifteen kroshas we reached Sitala,
and there the band of sannyasis halted.

**1169.** Chaitanya Gosai went beneath a tree,
and I could not think what alms to beg or where.

**1170.** Understanding my mind, with a slight smile
the Lord said, "What are you worrying about?

**1171.** We shall pass the night drinking the nectar of the Name,
and in the morning we shall get up and go wherever we like."

**1172.** Saying this, Gora-chand closed his eyes
and sat motionless, leaning against the tree.

**1173.** All the sannyasi thakuras, playing their khanjanis,[^06-90]
began to sing, very sweet to hear.

**1174.** Just then a merchant came there
and, giving alms to all, went away.

*[p. 108]*

**1175.** Whole fruits and roots, milk and sugar —
he gave them as alms to everyone with devotion.

**1176.** Receiving the alms, my heart was thrilled,
and the Lord took the milk and sugar and offered them.

**1177.** All the sannyasi thakuras rose in the morning
and set off for the land of Trivinku,[^06-91] crossing the mountains.

**1178.** The king of the land of Trivinku is very virtuous;
he cares for his subjects as for his own sons.

**1179.** All the townspeople are skilled in hospitality;
they make a great commotion over guests.

**1180.** Everyone tugs at the guests, vying for them,
and serves the guests with great affection.

**1181.** The king here — his name is Rudrapati —
is father and mother to the poor, refuge of the helpless.

**1182.** In this king's realm the subjects are very happy,
and all their hearts are full of love for the king.

**1183.** How many elephants and horses are tied at the king's gate!
There is no shortage of food in his storehouses.

**1184.** In three places in the town there are food-kitchens;
guests and travellers come and stay at them.

**1185.** Each stays there as long as he likes;
all praise him, saying, "Blessed, blessed is the king!"

*[p. 109]*

**1186.** In the evening we came to the town of Trivinku,
and the Lord sat beneath a tree with a joyful heart.

**1187.** A villager brought parched grain,
and the Lord passed the night beneath the tree.

**1188.** Next day the news spread,
and gradually the townspeople gathered.

**1189.** Seeing Gora's wondrous state, everyone
came and stood there with folded hands.

**1190.** Gora chants the Name of Hari with closed eyes,
and all, standing, praise him with pure hearts.

**1191.** The Lord sits there, not a limb moving,
tears flowing from the corners of his eyes.

**1192.** His hair on end, thrilled at heart;
seeing his state, the village people offer so many praises.

**1193.** Some say, "Come to my house, sannyasi";
others say, "I love to look at you."

**1194.** Some bring and offer fruits and roots,
but my Lord does not open his eyes to look.

**1195.** Some say, "This sannyasi is no man.
Why else should such devotion arise on seeing him?

**1196.** Seeing him, I want to give up worldly things;
my mind no longer wishes to live a household life."

*[p. 110]*

**1197.** Some say, "Today the night passed happily;
seeing the sannyasi, my heart has been purified."

**1198.** An old man came and said with devotion,
"Where is the sannyasi? Show him to me."

**1199.** Seeing his eagerness, my Gora Raya
quickly got up and went over to him.

**1200.** The old man bowed before the Lord
and brought him fruits, roots and parched grain.

**1201.** Everyone whispered about this,
and hundreds of learned men came wishing to see him.

**1202.** A brahmavadin came up
and raised the doctrine of advaita, and Chaitanya smiled.

**1203.** Citing the Vedas, Vedanta and the proofs of scripture,
he explained it to him, and hearing it the man lost himself.

**1204.** The Lord said, "Listen, listen, worthy sage:
the perfection of all practice is the love of Radha.

**1205.** Radhika's subtle love is like a mountain;
without devotion no one can find the way to it.

**1206.** Giving up her own happiness, beautiful Radhika,
mad for Krishna, renounced everything.

**1207.** One who understands Sri Radha's deep love
does not die again and again.

*[p. 111]*

**1208.** For one who is always floating on the doctrine of maya,
the truth of devotion does not dawn.

**1209.** The choicest essence of love is mahabhava,
and Sri Radha is surely full of mahabhava.

**1210.** Whoever understands this truth, worthy elder,
is not born and does not die again and again."

**1211.** Then the Lord's glory spread through the land,
and gradually all sorts of people began to gather.

**1212.** The king of this country, with great eagerness,
sent men to fetch the Lord.

**1213.** The Lord said, "I have no business there;
I do not go to worldly men."

**1214.** The royal messenger said, "Listen, sannyasi thakura,
why not go? You will get plenty of wealth.

**1215.** Clothes, ornaments and whatever you want —
there you will get such riches without effort."

**1216.** Understanding from the messenger's words what his intention was,
the Lord began to reason with him.

**1217.** With a slight smile the Lord said:
"Listen, royal messenger, I have no need of wealth.

**1218.** Into the company of those who are worms of worldliness
I never go. What use are riches to me?

*[p. 112]*

**1219.** Worms of worldliness crave wealth;
wealth is the root of evil — this is my conviction.

**1220.** Those who are drunk with wealth and forget the truth
always remain in the hell of worldliness.

**1221.** The rich man does not know that the body is transient,
and regards wealth as the purpose of life."

**1222.** Hearing this, the messenger grew angry
and went off to the king's gate to get his revenge.

**1223.** Hearing the report from the messenger's mouth, King Rudrapati
was not at all angry with the sannyasi.

**1224.** Hearing it word for word from the messenger,
he himself wished to see the sannyasi.

**1225.** King Rudrapati set off to see the sannyasi;
full of devotion, he came out in haste.

**1226.** Leaving his elephants and horses far behind,
he came to the sannyasi in very humble dress.

**1227.** With two or four ministers, the worthy king
came near the Lord and said with devotion —

**1228.** with folded hands Rudrapati said again and again:
"Be merciful and forgive my offence.

**1229.** Without understanding, I summoned you;
forgive me that offence this time.

*[p. 113]*

**1230.** Give me teaching in knowledge, saviour of the fallen:
why do living beings suffer grief and sorrow?"

**1231.** The king is very learned in many shastras;
everyone says he is a great scholar of the Bhagavata.

**1232.** Two or four learned gosais
came with him to receive instruction.

**1233.** The Lord said, "King, you are most fortunate.
You know the Bhagavata — what more can I say?

**1234.** You are well versed in many shastras, a great sage,
while I know nothing but Radha and Krishna."

**1235.** As soon as he took Krishna's name, love welled up,
and tears began to stream down.

**1236.** Drunk with love for Krishna, the Lord rose at once
and began to dance with arms outstretched.

**1237.** Crying "Hari bol!", Gora lost consciousness
and, dancing, fell crashing to the ground.

**1238.** Then the king rushed forward and lifted the Lord,
and at the same moment the maharaja grew drunk.

**1239.** Crying "Hari!", the maharaja began to dance,
and his chest was flooded with tears.

**1240.** His hair stood on end, he was filled with thrills;
falling in the dust, his body turned grey.

*[p. 114]*

**1241.** Seeing the king's devotion, my Nimai
embraced the king and said, "Come, brother.

**1242.** One whose eyes shed tears at the Name of Hari
is the apple of my eye.

**1243.** Seeing your devotion, worthy king,
know for certain that my heart is soothed."

**1244.** Saying this and sending the maharaja away,
the Lord went off to bathe.

**1245.** The king sent many fruits and roots,
and the Lord, after his daily rites, made the offering.

**1246.** Leaving people to serve the Lord,
the king went back to his capital with a happy heart.

**1247.** Some bring fruits and roots, some bring flour,
some bring parched grain to the guest's lodging.

**1248.** For Vishvambhara's sake people bustle about,
and now and then many people come and sit down.

**1249.** Each brings and offers whatever he likes,
but Gora Raya says nothing, good or bad.

**1250.** The land, ringed by mountains, is beautiful to see;
the spring waters flow most charmingly.

**1251.** All around are huge neem trees;
their wondrous beauty is beyond words.

*[p. 115]*

**1252.** There is a mountain there called Ramagiri;[^06-92]
everyone extols its wondrous greatness.

**1253.** All say that on its summit Ramachandra
stayed three days with Sita.

**1254.** Having won the battle of Lanka, Rama, abode of virtues,
climbed this peak and rested.

**1255.** Ramachandra with Sita and Thakura Lakshmana —
all three rested here.

**1256.** Hearing this, the Lord was filled with longing
and climbed the mountain to see the place.

**1257.** Where Rama and Sita had rested,
there my Gora went and bowed down.

**1258.** Hundreds of people climbed with the Lord
to gaze with devotion at that Ramagiri.

**1259.** This country is very extensive in breadth and length;
a fortnight passed while we crossed it.

**1260.** Then he entered the town of Payoshni[^06-93]
and was delighted to see Shiva-Narayana.

**1261.** In the matha of Shringari[^06-94] live the disciples of Shankara;
going there, the Lord met with them.

**1262.** All the disciples of Shankara, gathering together,
sat down to debate, examining the truth.

*[pp. 116–117 — from the 1926 edition; these pages are missing in the base edition]*

**1263.** In the debate all the disciples admitted defeat,
and from the matha he went to see Matsya-tirtha.

**1264.** Having visited Matsya-tirtha, the Lord came to Kachad,
and at Kachad had darshan of Bhagavati.

**1265.** Here flows the river named Bhadra, daughter of the Krishna;[^06-95]
having bathed, he went on to Naga-panchapadi.

**1266.** All the people here are devotees of Rama;
here the Lord told me to beg alms.

**1267.** I went round three houses begging,
and they all gave me plenty of flour.

**1268.** Staying here three nights, my Lord
went on with everyone to Chitol,[^06-96] crossing the mountains.

**1269.** Leaving Chitol, he went again to the bank of the Tungabhadra,
walking slowly, to bathe.

**1270.** Having bathed on the bank of the river Tungabhadra,
my Gora, delighting all hearts, sings Krishna's glories.

**1271.** The birthplace of the Kaveri is Kotigiri;[^06-97]
he arrived there after wandering through many lands.

**1272.** Having bathed at the source of the Kaveri,
the Lord Bhagavan went to the village of Chandapur.

**1273.** On the left a mountain called Satya stands in splendour —
Satyagiri; I cannot describe its beauty.

**1274.** From afar a blue line can be seen;
looking at its beauty soothes the eyes.

**1275.** Seeing Satyagiri, the Lord bowed down
and, keeping Satyagiri on the left, went to the right.

**1276.** Coming near the town of Chandapur,
he went and sat beneath a banyan tree.

**1277.** In Chandapur lives a detached gosai;
hearing of him from people, Nimai went to meet him.

**1278.** The gosai is indeed learned and knows many shastras;
a golden earring swings in one of his ears.

**1279.** Gradually the gosai began quoting scripture
and started a conversation full of pride.

**1280.** The sannyasi's name is Ishvara Bharati;
people say this gosai is an abode of all virtues.

**1281.** Perceiving the sannyasi's pride in his heart,
the Lord smiled a little and turned his face away.

**1282.** Lord Vishvambhara says nothing, good or bad,
and at last, irritated, the excellent sannyasi

**1283.** says to the Lord, "You don't say a word;
I do not accept you as a learned pandit.

**1284.** Everyone says you are very learned,
but I see in you not a trace of knowledge.

*[p. 118]*

**1285.** You have made the whole country shout 'Hari bol',
but I see not the slightest merit in you.

**1286.** I have heard you are learned in the shastras, yet you have nothing to say;
you wander about begging wherever you can.

**1287.** You have neither learning nor knowledge to debate with.
Why then are the foolish so suddenly beguiled?

**1288.** Who knows by what trick and stratagem
you show the subtle truth to everyone?

**1289.** How you will get away after making the fools of this country shout 'Hari bol' —
I shall see through your cunning.

**1290.** If you have the power, then debate;
this time I shall find out your wits and understanding."

**1291.** Saying this, Bharati Gosai hurried off
and came back again and sat down with three companions.

**1292.** The four of them sat on the four sides of the Lord,
and seeing this comedy the Lord began to laugh.

**1293.** Bharati said, "You laugh it off;
but consider carefully what I say.

**1294.** Tell me, which deity is to be worshipped?"
The Lord said, "What is there in the world but Krishna?"

**1295.** Bharati said, "Hear the proof of scripture:
the one Brahman is the lord of all — so the Vedas teach.

*[p. 119]*

**1296.** Wherever I look, I see everything as Brahman.
Tell me, how can this doctrine be refuted?"

**1297.** The Lord said, "I don't know how to debate;
I admit that you are learned in every truth.

**1298.** You are a great pandit in debate, gosai;
Nimai has been defeated by you.

**1299.** If you like, I can write you a certificate of victory:
today I have admitted defeat in your debate."

**1300.** Hearing this, the yogi grumbled and muttered,
and the Lord said, "Practise devotion — logic is far away.

**1301.** Devotion brings Krishna — that is the conclusion.
Reduce the doctrines of the Vedas and Vedanta to dust.

**1302.** Tell me, what is the fruit of discussing many shastras?
Without Krishna there is nowhere to stand."

**1303.** Saying this, my Lord closed his eyes;
his hair stood on end and devotion overflowed.

**1304.** Tears began to flow down his breast,
and the knot of his kaupina gradually came loose.

**1305.** Trembling all over, his heart quaking, his body sweating,
he called out "Krishna!" and began to sway.

**1306.** "O Krishna, where are you, merciful Lord?
Bestow devotion and purify my heart."

*[p. 120]*

**1307.** Saying this, the Lord began to weep,
and the surge of his heart seemed to double.

**1308.** Lord Vishvambhara hears nothing, good or bad,
and sobs and weeps unceasingly.

**1309.** Seeing a tamala tree in front of him,
he ran, crying "Krishna!", and threw his arms around it.[^06-98]

**1310.** Seeing this state with his own eyes,
the yogi then clasped the Lord's feet.

**1311.** The yogi said, "I beg you, let us not debate;
now my longing for Krishna is growing.

**1312.** Seeing your state, young sannyasi,
I no longer have any wish to debate.

**1313.** Give my heart the incomparable jewel of devotion;
this I humbly beg at your feet."

**1314.** But the Lord does not hear these words of the yogi;
my Lord is soaking the earth with his tears.

**1315.** In the surge of great ecstasy his body grew rigid,
and his body, the equal of gold, fell in the dust.

**1316.** Crying "Krishna!", the Lord rolls on the ground,
his body grey with dust and pierced by thorns.

**1317.** The yogi, sitting before him, began to weep,
and at once compassion for him arose in the Lord.

*[p. 121]*

**1318.** Seeing Bharati's devotion, he put his hand on his back,
and with his hand on his back said a few words.

**1319.** At the Lord's touch devotion arose in the yogi,
and his mind was immersed in the rasa of devotion to Krishna.

**1320.** How great the Lord's mercy is cannot be told;
drunk with love, the yogi rolls in the dust.

**1321.** The yogi said, "You are my Krishna.
When, Lord, will you come again and show yourself to me?"

**1322.** The Lord said, "Do not say such words again.
Krishna is the lord of Vrindavana — that is the conclusion.

**1323.** Without devotion the truth of Krishna does not dawn;
know for certain that Krishna is bound by the rope of devotion."

**1324.** The yogi said, "I have seen through your trick.
Why do you throw dust in my eyes with your cunning?

**1325.** Today I shall bind you with the rope of devotion;
today I shall snatch away both your sandals."

**1326.** Saying this, Ishvara Bharati
seized the sandals and began to tug at them hard.

**1327.** The Lord said, "Put your faith in Krishna;
from today your name is Krishnadasa."

**1328.** Saying this, the Lord left Chandapur,
and the excellent yogi came with him a long way.

*[p. 122]*

**1329.** Smiling, the Lord sent the yogi back,
and the excellent yogi bowed and went away.

**1330.** Two days and nights we went on, crossing the mountains,
and in that time, search as we might, we found no village or town.

**1331.** The path is very hard; I could not walk.
Only kadamba trees could be seen, row upon row.

**1332.** Seeing the kadamba trees, Gora said to me,
"My Krishna plays beneath these trees."

**1333.** Saying this, my Lord burst into distraught weeping
and walked on swaying, drunk with love for Krishna.

**1334.** There I saw a tiger and was afraid.
*[the second line of the couplet is missing in the edition]*

**1335.** I pointed out the tiger to the Lord with a sign,
but heard nothing from the Lord's lips, good or bad.

**1336.** The tiger was drinking water at that place,
and I crept carefully along beside the Lord.

**1337.** Gora went to the right, keeping the tiger on the left,
in ecstasy, his body drunk with the Name of Hari.

**1338.** The tiger did not even look round at us,
but I kept looking back and walked quickly on.

**1339.** Seeing how I behaved, with a slight smile
the Lord said, "What are you afraid of?"

---

### Translator's notes

[^06-84]: *Atal-patal*, literally "abysses and nether worlds", i.e. empty, far-fetched speculation.
[^06-85]: The lord of the ancestors (*Pitripati*) is Yama.
[^06-86]: "My beggar" is an affectionate name for Chaitanya the sannyasi.
[^06-87]: An obscure exclamation (*chambani shiori*), apparently words in a local language, which Govinda records by ear.
[^06-88]: *Ishta-goshthi*: conversation about the beloved deity, spiritual discussion.
[^06-89]: The name is unclear in the edition (*Tattvakundi*).
[^06-90]: The *khanjani* is a small tambourine.
[^06-91]: Trivinku is Travancore (Tiruvitankur). The name of King Rudrapati also occurs in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 9) as the king of Travancore.
[^06-92]: Compare "Ramagiri-ashramas" in Kalidasa's *Meghaduta*, to which the 1926 edition points in a footnote.
[^06-93]: Payoshni (Payasvini) is a river and district in Kerala; in CC Madhya 9 Chaitanya sees Shankara-Narayana there.
[^06-94]: Shringeri is the chief matha of the Shankara school.
[^06-95]: The river Bhadra, "daughter of the Krishna", is presumably the Tunga-Bhadra in the Krishna river system.
[^06-96]: Chitol is probably Chitradurga.
[^06-97]: Kotigiri is the source of the Kaveri (Talakaveri in the Coorg hills).
[^06-98]: The tamala is a dark-barked tree whose colour recalls Krishna.

---

# Part 7

*The poor brahmana and his Gopala. Nilagiri, Gurjari, Agastya-kunda. The pandit Arjuna. Sahya-kulachala, Pune: "Krishna is in the pond". Bholeshvar, Devaleshvar, Jejuri and Khandoba's "muraris" (pp. 123–142)*

*[p. 123]*

**1340.** "By the power of the Name of Hari no fear of Yama remains;
call 'Krishna, Krishna' and have no doubt."[^07-99]

**1341.** Hearing these words, my strength revived,
and my bodily strength seemed to double.

**1342.** Walking on, we came near a small hamlet,
hoping for shelter.

**1343.** It is a very small hamlet, all its people poor;
there the sannyasi Nimai went and sat down.

**1344.** The hamlet, ringed by hills, is lovely to see;
I went into the village to beg.

**1345.** There was a very poor brahmana there,
who somehow lived by begging.

**1346.** I went to his house to beg alms,
and the brahmana said, "Wait a little, brother.

**1347.** Sit a while; don't go away empty-handed —
if a guest goes away unfed, hell will be my lot.

**1348.** I will go and beg alms and bring them to you;
wait here a little while, brother."

**1349.** Saying this, the brahmana went off to beg
and brought two coconuts and gave them to me.

**1350.** Bringing the alms, I offered them to the Lord beneath the tree,
and the Lord joyfully offered the fruits as bhoga.

*[p. 124]*

**1351.** Hearing about the brahmana, my Gora Raya
went to see him at dusk.

**1352.** A brahmana and his wife live there together;
for the sake of serving Gopala they beg alms.

**1353.** Seeing the Lord in his own house,
the brahmana came and stood before him with folded hands.

**1354.** The brahmana said, "With what shall I honour my guest?
How can I say to you, Lord, 'Go away'?

**1355.** We live here for the service of Gopala;
we both beg alms and serve.

**1356.** I have no seat — what can I give you to sit on?"
The brahmana's wife said, "Brahmana, offer your head!

**1357.** Look, lightning is playing over the guest's body!
Bring tulasi and place it at the guest's feet."

**1358.** Hurriedly the brahmana brought tulasi
and rushed to place it at the Lord's feet.

**1359.** Then Chaitanya, taking the brahmana by the hand, explained:
"Offer the tulasi at Gopala's feet."

**1360.** Hearing this, the brahmana began to weep,
and at once the merciful Lord embraced him.

**1361.** The Lord said, "You, brahmana, are most fortunate:
Bhagavan himself dwells in your house.

*[p. 125]*

**1362.** What can I say of your good fortune, thakura!
Gopala sports in your house.

**1363.** Your wife is Kamala[^07-100] herself;
think it over and see for yourself."

**1364.** The brahmana said, "I count your mercy my good fortune;
you are no ordinary man, O merciful one.

**1365.** Why else does lightning play over your body?
I feel the fragrance of lotus coming from your body.

**1366.** If you are not Bhagavan, O merciful one,
why does the scent of lotus come from your body?

**1367.** Place your foot on my head" —
saying this, the brahmana bowed his head down.

**1368.** At these words, biting his tongue,
merciful Chaitanyadeva drew back.

**1369.** The brahmana and his wife, distraught,
ran and bowed their heads at his feet.

**1370.** Spreading his arms, the Lord raised the brahmana,
and then began to sing with devotion.

**1371.** The brahmana's house seemed to become Vrindavana,
and the village folk came to hear the Name of Hari:

**1372.** "Hare Krishna, Hare Krishna, Krishna Krishna, Hare Hare,
Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare."[^07-101]

*[p. 126]*

**1373.** Merciful Chaitanya began this song,
and with it all the listeners grew excited.

**1374.** Hearing the Name from the Lord's lips, the village people
all rolled at the Lord's feet.

**1375.** Singing on and on, the night passed,
and in the morning my Lord took his leave.

**1376.** When the Lord had taken leave and gone out,
the people stood gazing after him like puppets.

**1377.** He signalled me, saying "Govinda",
and at once I lifted the load onto my shoulder.

**1378.** Near the land of Kandar the Nilagiri stands in splendour;
in the afternoon we slowly came there.

**1379.** Ah, how beautiful is that king of mountains, the Nilagiri!
He stands like a great being absorbed in meditation.

**1380.** At its foot hundreds of caves appear,
and a wondrous sight crowns its summit.

**1381.** Huge trees, climbing onto its head,
fan it like chowries, swaying in the wind.

**1382.** With a splashing sound the water of the springs falls;
seeing it, my curiosity grew.

**1383.** We wander about at the foot of the mountain
and see ever new beauties.

*[p. 127]*

**1384.** How many creepers have twined round the trees,
lovingly displaying the union of husband and wife!

**1385.** Peacocks sit on the branches and cry,
and birds of many kinds sing with sweet voices.

**1386.** Flowers of every kind have bloomed and lit the place up,
as if a garland were swinging at nature's neck.

**1387.** At night how many creepers glow brightly,
and fireflies shine in swarms on tree after tree.

**1388.** A little river flows with a murmuring sound;
sitting on its bank, the Lord performs his evening worship.

**1389.** At night we sat down beneath a tree,
and the Lord said to me, "Tonight we stay here."

**1390.** Saying just this, Gora closed his eyes
and passed the night in the Name of Hari.

**1391.** By the Lord's grace I feel neither hunger nor thirst,
and so I lie down wherever we happen to be.

**1392.** On the day the Lord tells me to beg,
on that day I go to the householders' doors.

**1393.** In the morning we went to the town of Gurjari;
many people live here happily.

**1394.** Here many fine houses stand in splendour;
Gora Raya went and sat at the edge of the town.

*[p. 128]*

**1395.** Here there is a tank called Agastya-kunda;
bathing in the tank, we were filled with joy.

**1396.** Gora Raya, having bathed in Agastya-kunda,
sat on the bank and sang the glories of Hari.

**1397.** Gradually two or four people appeared;
a brahmana brought milk and sugar and set them by him.

**1398.** Some said, "Guest, come to my house";
others said, "Say Krishna's name once more.

**1399.** The Name of Hari is very sweet in your mouth;
hearing the Name, all my sorrow and pain have gone.

**1400.** Krishna's name in your mouth is like nectar;
speak, speak of Krishna, let my soul be soothed."

**1401.** He listened to no one's words and did not reply;
how many wise men came to see the Lord!

**1402.** Closing his eyes, Gora-chand began to sway,
and tears burst forth from his eyes.

**1403.** My Gora Raya does not see the people;
crying "O Krishna!" he weeps and wets the earth.

**1404.** The Lord began to weep, sobbing;
his cloth came open and his matted hair spread over his back.

**1405.** His hair on end, distraught with weeping,
dishevelled, the Lord says all sorts of confused things.

*[p. 129]*

**1406.** Sometimes the Lord, drunk, rolls on the ground,
sometimes he flails and falls to the earth.

**1407.** "Here is my dear friend Mukunda-Murari!" —
saying this, Chaitanya the beggar rushes forward.

**1408.** Sometimes he says, "Come, Narahari, my life!
Hearing Krishna's name, I embrace you!"

**1409.** In this way Gora Raya says many things
and, drunk with feeling, runs about.

**1410.** Hearing of his wondrous power, all the worthy men
come into the Lord's presence.

**1411.** A great pandit named Arjuna
argued with the Lord, citing the proofs of scripture.

**1412.** Arjuna said, "I do not accept the doctrine of the jiva;
I know the doctrine of the Self and the doctrine of the jiva to be one."

**1413.** The Lord said, "You are a worthy pandit;
hear the proof of scripture and decide.

**1414.** If you know the meaning of the shruti 'Two birds',[^07-102]
why do you take two truths to be one?"

**1415.** Raising the subtle points of Vedanta, Gora Raya
explained everything to Arjuna in minute detail:

**1416.** "The jiva, the Self and the Supreme Self abide thus:
the Self is a great tree, and the jiva is its leaf.

*[p. 130]*

**1417.** What lesson have you learned, pandit thakura?
Put aside all this idle speculation.

**1418.** Maya is the shadow of God; he is not tainted by it.
By his will the jiva becomes subject to maya.

**1419.** One who can give up maya by the power of the Name
is a great sage in this world.

**1420.** Behind the curtain of maya there is Someone;
lift the curtain and behold Him."

**1421.** Saying this, he called out, "O Krishna!" —
and the place seemed to fall silent at once.

**1422.** How many times I had heard the Name from the Lord's lips,
but today my body thrilled.

**1423.** The Lord began to call, "Rama, Rama!" —
and the place then seemed to become Vaikuntha.

**1424.** A favourable breeze began to blow,
and the villagers began to appear in crowds.

**1425.** Hundreds of people standing all round
listened [to the Name] in silence.

**1426.** As if the gods of heaven, to hear the Name,
had come down above our heads and were listening.

**1427.** A bewitching scent of lotus spread,
and Gaura-Hari, unconscious, chants the Name.

*[p. 131]*

**1428.** Everyone's eyes are on the Lord's face,
tears falling unceasingly.

**1429.** Great Marathas[^07-103] come in crowds
and all together listen to the Name.

**1430.** Looking back behind me, I see
hundreds of respectable married women standing there.

**1431.** All listen to the Name of Hari with devotion,
the women wiping their tears with the ends of their saris.

**1432.** Countless Vaishnavas, Shaivas and sannyasis gather
and listen to the Name of Hari with closed eyes.

**1433.** Thus the Lord made this country drunk;
I have never seen such power.

**1434.** Sometimes Gora Raya recites [from the Bhagavata],
sometimes he sings songs and enraptures the listeners.

**1435.** Thus, chanting the Name of Hari,
the Lord, beside himself, began to dance.

**1436.** His matted locks came loose, his kaupina slipped off,
his body grey with dust, like the poorest of the poor.

**1437.** Dancing, the Lord lost consciousness
and then fell crashing to the ground.

**1438.** The Lord lay there like an inert thing,
and seeing this all the people came forward.

*[p. 132]*

**1439.** Some sprinkled water on the Lord's face,
some very carefully lifted him up.

**1440.** After two dandas the Lord rose and sat up,
and all, amazed, raised the cry of "Hari".

**1441.** In the afternoon a brahmana brought alms,
and my Lord made the offering beneath the tree.

**1442.** Leaving the town of Gurjari, my Gora Raya
wished to go to the town of Purna.[^07-104]

**1443.** For seven days he never held any conversation,
and went straight up the mountain at Vijapur.

**1444.** His only provision for the road is the Name of Hari;
climbing the mountain, the Lord rested.

**1445.** Here, climbing to the summit of the mountain,
he rejoiced to behold Hara and Gauri.

**1446.** Coming down from the mountain, Chaitanya Gosai
went north, and I followed behind.

**1447.** At once the Sahya-kulachala came into view;
seeing the kulachala, the Lord was overwhelmed with joy.

**1448.** I have seen the Mahendra and Malaya mountains with my own eyes,
but the beauty of Sahyagiri — ah, it cannot be told.

**1449.** From afar a blue line can be seen;
there my Lord hurries to see it.

*[p. 133]*

**1450.** Majestically the mountain stands;
seeing the mountain, my heart seemed to dance.

**1451.** The Lord said, "This mountain is an abode of joy."
Saying "abode of joy", he bowed down.

**1452.** Having seen the Sahya-kulachala, he went on,
and Lord Vishvambhara seemed filled with thrills.

**1453.** Leaving Sahyagiri, he went north,
saying "Hari, Hari" in boundless joy.

**1454.** He has no desires and goes in the poorest garb,
yet the Lord has flooded many lands with the rasa of devotion.

**1455.** A kaupina for clothing, dust smeared over his whole body;
seeing him, one would think: surely a madman.

**1456.** In time Gora-chand came to the town of Purna,
and many pandits came there in crowds.

**1457.** Many people here study the shastras,
and gradually many people came to see him.

**1458.** There is a pool called Achchhapara;
my Nimai went and sat beside it.

**1459.** A spreading bakula tree stands above it,
and my Lord settled down beneath it.

**1460.** Hundreds of pandits dwell here,
and day and night the pandits expound various shastras.

*[p. 134]*

**1461.** Hundreds of students come here
and study various shastras with their gurus.

**1462.** Here many people are skilled in learning,
and hundreds of Sanskrit schools stand in their midst.

**1463.** One who studies the Bhagavata
is accepted by everyone as a pandit.

**1464.** One who does not know the Gita and the Bhagavata
no one accepts as a pandit.

**1465.** A pandit is expounding the Bhagavata;
hearing it, tears flow from the Lord's eyes.

**1466.** A brahmavadin pandit came,
and the Lord refuted all his arguments.

**1467.** Many Vaishnava sadhus, gathering together,
were awakened on seeing the Lord's devotion.

**1468.** Closing his eyes, the Lord says, "Krishna, Krishna",
tears flowing from his eyes onto his breast.

**1469.** The Lord said, "My life, Mukunda-Murari,
come and rise in my heart.

**1470.** Radha-Krishna is all-powerful, the support of the universe;
who but Krishna bears the burden of this world?

**1471.** In whose pores are millions of universes —
how shall I see that Krishna, my life?

*[p. 135]*

**1472.** He ate earth and, on his mother's lap, opened his mouth,
and at once his mother saw the universe in his mouth.

**1473.** For that Krishna my heart is restless;
without Krishna my soul is in torment."

**1474.** A pandit came up and said,
"Your Krishna is in this pond."

**1475.** Hearing these words, the Lord started
and, his body thrilling, stood up.

**1476.** Never have I seen such a flood of tears;
Nimai, weeping in separation from Krishna, was distraught.

**1477.** Crying "Krishna!" he began to sob,
saying, "Without Krishna life is in vain."

**1478.** He soaked the lap of the earth with tears
and, weeping, said "Hari bol".

**1479.** Once he said to me, "What torment is this?
Without Krishna my soul can bear the pain no longer."

**1480.** Again that man said mockingly,
"Sannyasi, your Krishna is hiding in the water."

**1481.** This time Mahaprabhu, hearing his words,
at once leapt into the water in an ecstasy of love.

**1482.** Many people jumped into the pond,
pulled the Lord out onto the bank and lamented in many ways.

*[p. 136]*

**1483.** The man who had said Krishna was in the water —
all the pandits reproached him.

**1484.** The Lord said, "Why rebuke the maharaja for nothing?
Krishna always abides in water, on land and in the sky.

**1485.** Krishna around, Krishna beside, the world is full of Krishna,
but only one who is a devotee can see him.

**1486.** Devotion is the supreme truth in this world;
the devotee kicks even liberation on the head.

**1487.** One who cannot understand the wheel of maya
is most unfortunate in this world.

**1488.** False is homeland, false the homestead, false house and home;
for the sake of food the mind is distraught.

**1489.** Who is kin, who a stranger, who father and mother?
Tell me, whose neck will you put your arm round and call brother?

**1490.** There is no difference between man and woman — the difference is of the skin;
this the Vedas say again and again.

**1491.** In the darkness of delusion the soul forgets itself
and does not once say 'Hari' with its lips.

**1492.** Do not take false pride in riches, brothers;
always pass your time saying 'Hare Krishna'.

**1493.** This world is covered in the darkness of sin;
apart from Hari nothing in the world is true.

*[p. 137]*

**1494.** On the day the two birds leave the tree of the body,[^07-105]
on that day the inert body will be left lying.

**1495.** Why do you go on dreaming while awake, brothers?
No one will live forever; all will die.

**1496.** Come, brothers, let us all together raise the cry of 'Hari';
hearing the Name, Kritanta[^07-106] himself will tremble.

**1497.** However mighty a king of kings or emperor may be,
one day his royal market will surely break up.

**1498.** The maharaja rules by his might —
why then is his heart scorched by the three miseries?

**1499.** Priceless gems and pearls will not go with you;
understand by experience that wealth is empty and transient.

**1500.** With devotion say 'Hare Krishna', brothers,
and all of you will live in the world in perpetual bliss.

**1501.** Enchanted by maya, forgetting everything,
what are you all so proud of?

**1502.** In time even the seven kulachalas[^07-107] will vanish;
nothing in the inert world will remain.

**1503.** With devotion meditate on that True and Eternal One,
and hold fast to his feet.

**1504.** Lord Gadadhara[^07-108] will take away all suffering,
and this whole world will become like Vaikuntha.

*[p. 138]*

**1505.** One who is entangled in worldly wealth
cannot cut those cruel bonds.

**1506.** One who falls into bondage by his own wish
everyone calls a terrible fool.

**1507.** With the sword of the Name of Hari cut the bonds of maya,
and you will go without effort to the eternal abode.

**1508.** There will be no more birth, death or old age again and again;
call 'Krishna, Krishna' and the darkness will vanish.

**1509.** Work out your prarabdha[^07-109] in utter humility,
and then grief, pain and sorrow will go far away."

**1510.** Crowds of people came to see the Lord,
and countless pandits came to debate.

**1511.** Some say, "This sannyasi is no man";
some say, "This is a great soul."

**1512.** The Lord does not reply to anyone's words;
at the Name of Hari streams of love flow from both his eyes.

**1513.** Closing both eyes, the Lord chants the Name of Hari,
rolling over and over on the ground.

**1514.** The Lord said, "Which holy place shall I go to next?
Show me the way — where is Bholeshvar?"

**1515.** Near the village of Patas is Gora-ghat;
there is the great shrine called Bholeshvar.

*[p. 139]*

**1516.** "In Bholeshvar Mahadeva dwells" —
this was the advice given by the worthy Tunnu.

**1517.** The excellent brahmana named Tunnu is a very learned pandit;
it was from his words that the Lord learned of it.

**1518.** Tunnu said, "Bholeshvar is there."
Hearing this, the Lord set off to Shiva's presence.

**1519.** At Bholeshvar there is a fair every year;
hearing this, the Lord's heart danced.

**1520.** Looking at me, the Lord smiled a little
and said, "Let us turn back and go to Bholeshvar."

**1521.** Walking many roads over the mountains,
we came to Bholeshvar and saw a splendid tirtha.

**1522.** On the mountain stands a huge temple,
and inside it we saw Lord Bholeshvar.

**1523.** Here there is a Siddha-kupa;[^07-110]
drawing water from it, the Lord bathed.

**1524.** Seeing Bholeshvar, love arose in the Lord,
and with folded hands he sang many hymns.

**1525.** Losing consciousness, Gora fell to the ground
and rolled over and over many times.

**1526.** Having seen Bholeshvar, Gora Raya
hurried to see nearby Devaleshvar.

*[p. 140]*

**1527.** Seeing Devaleshvar, the Lord, jewel of virtues,
bowed down and lay rolling on the earth.

**1528.** Choked with love, he sang many hymns,
and people came with devotion to see the Lord.

**1529.** Devaleshvar dwells on a mountain,
and far from it the town of Jijuri[^07-111] stands in splendour.

**1530.** In Jijuri there is a god named Khandoba;
with the Lord we go to see Khandoba.

**1531.** A woman who for various reasons cannot be married
is married by the grace of Khandoba.

**1532.** Father and mother bring their daughter to Khandoba
and with much devotion give the daughter to Khandoba.

**1533.** The daughters of poor fathers live here
and serve Khandoba with affection.

**1534.** Hundreds of women, regarding Khandoba as their husband,
have gradually become beggars on the road.

**1535.** Deceived at Khandoba's shrine,
many women here live as prostitutes.

**1536.** Calling themselves Khandoba's wives, they commit sin,
and afterwards a very hard fate befalls them.

**1537.** Many people come here on pilgrimage,
and by guile they drag them down into hell.

*[p. 141]*

**1538.** All the poor maidens come here;
when they are married they say, "We are Khandoba's wives."

**1539.** Hearing this, the Lord, to see these women,
went there very secretly.

**1540.** People call them "muraris";[^07-112]
seeing the muraris, the Lord was moved to compassion.

**1541.** If one hears of the sorrows of the muraris,
compassion arises even in the cruellest heart.

**1542.** I cannot say how cruel a father is
who makes his own daughter a murari!

**1543.** Hearing this, the Lord went to the fine courtyard
to deliver all the women.

**1544.** I said, "There is no need to go to that place,"
but Chaitanya Gosai did not listen to my words.

**1545.** My Lord went into the muraris' quarter
and purified them all by giving them the Name of Hari.

**1546.** Unable to bear the women's sorrow,
he wants to deliver all the muraris.

**1547.** Hearing of the Lord's wondrous state with their own ears,
gradually many women came to this place.

**1548.** The Lord said to the women, "Chant the Name of Hari;
by the power of the Name you will surely gain the eternal abode.

*[p. 142]*

**1549.** Hari is most merciful, the refuge of the helpless;
all of you think of him as your own husband.

**1550.** All the gopis, to gain Krishna as their husband,
performed the Katyayani vow[^07-113] with pure hearts.

**1551.** If Krishna becomes your husband, no fear of worldly existence will remain;
know for certain that Krishna is the husband of all.

**1552.** Always call 'Krishna, Krishna' with devotion;
always say with your lips, 'Hare Krishna, Hare'."

**1553.** Saying this, my Lord began to chant the Name,
and at once his body was filled with thrills.

**1554.** Seeing the Lord's state, all the women
began to worship the Lord's feet.

**1555.** The Lord said, "I beg alms at the doors of householders;
I am utterly untouchable — do not touch me.

**1556.** Say 'Hari' with devotion and your sufferings will end;
by the power of the Name everyone's sins will be burnt to ashes.

**1557.** One who has sunk into sin without understanding
is freed from sin by the power of the Name of Hari."

**1558.** Hearing the teaching, all of Khandoba's wives
stood in rows before the Lord.

**1559.** Indira Bai came forward and said with folded hands,
"Have mercy on me, worthy sannyasi."

---

### Translator's notes

[^07-99]: In the edition the beginning of p. 123 partly repeats the end of p. 122 (the tiger episode), apparently through overlapping lines in the OCR. The repetition is omitted; the tiger episode is given in Part 6.
[^07-100]: Kamala is Lakshmi.
[^07-101]: The maha-mantra is given in the order "Hare Krishna… Hare Rama…", as it is usually sung in Bengal.
[^07-102]: "Two birds" (*dvā suparṇā*) is the famous mantra of the Mundaka Upanishad (3.1.1) and the Rigveda about two birds on one tree, one eating the fruit, the other only watching. It is the traditional basis for distinguishing the jiva from the Paramatma.
[^07-103]: *Maharahi* in the edition apparently means Maharashtrians (Marathas).
[^07-104]: Purna-nagar is Pune.
[^07-105]: A reference to the same mantra of the two birds.
[^07-106]: Kritanta, "he who makes an end", is Yama.
[^07-107]: The *kulachalas* are the seven principal mountain ranges of India in Puranic geography.
[^07-108]: Gadadhara, "bearer of the mace", is Vishnu.
[^07-109]: *Prarabdha*: karma that has already begun to bear fruit.
[^07-110]: *Siddha-kupa*: "well of perfection".
[^07-111]: Jijuri is Jejuri near Pune, the chief temple of Khandoba.
[^07-112]: *Murari* (*murali*): girls dedicated to Khandoba, comparable to devadasis. This episode is one of the best-known passages of the *Karcha*.
[^07-113]: The Katyayani vow was undertaken by the gopis of Vraja to obtain Krishna (*Bhagavata Purana* 10.22).

---

# Part 8

*Indira Bai. The robber Naroji in the Chorananadi forest. Khandala, Nasik, Panchavati. Ashtabhuja and the sermon against animal sacrifice. The Tapti, Bharuch, Baroda, the death of Naroji. Ahmedabad (pp. 143–162)*

*[p. 143]*

**1560.** "I have grown old doing wicked deeds;
deliver me by giving me the dust of your feet."

**1561.** Saying this, Indira rolled in the dust,
and the Lord delivered Indira by giving her the Name.

**1562.** Having received the Name of Hari, the beautiful Indira
left her home, renouncing everything.

**1563.** From that day all Khandoba's wives
became drunk with the Name of Hari, tears flowing from their eyes.

**1564.** Never have I seen so merciful a Lord;
how many sinners he delivered — there is no counting them.

**1565.** Having seen the muraris' devotion with his own eyes,
in the morning he wished to go to the Chorananadi forest.

**1566.** The village people said, "What business have you there?
The Chorananadi forest is a mine of sin.

**1567.** Many robbers live in the Chorananadi forest;
why do you wish to go there?"

**1568.** The Lord said, "I will go to the Chorananadi forest;
seeing Chorananadi, my purpose will be fulfilled."

**1569.** The village people said, "Do not go there, sannyasi;
we do not like holy men going there.

**1570.** Great thieves and forest robbers live in that place;
if you go there your life will be in danger."

*[p. 144]*

**1571.** The Lord said, "What can the robbers take from me?
I will go there right now."

**1572.** Rama Svami said, "Lord, the Chorananadi forest
is no tirtha; what business have you there?

**1573.** If the robbers do you some harm,
people will die of separation from you."

**1574.** The Lord said, "Have no fear, Rama Svami;
I will make the robbers drunk with the Name of Hari."

**1575.** Saying this, the Lord went to Chorananadi,
and arriving at Chorananadi he sat beneath a tree.

**1576.** Here many wicked men have their den
and live by robbery.

**1577.** A man came along saying "khai mai";
I could not understand anything he said.

**1578.** The Lord, understanding all his speech,
answered him in "khai mai" and explained everything.

**1579.** The man stood staring for a moment,
then, glancing this way and that, went back into the forest.

**1580.** A very powerful man named Naroji
came forward with his weapons and arms.

**1581.** Gradually two or four men appeared;
seeing the sannyasi, they all bowed down.

*[p. 145]*

**1582.** Naroji said, "Come to my place;
you shall spend tonight there."

**1583.** Hearing Naroji's words, the Lord said,
"I will pass tonight here beneath the tree."

**1584.** Hearing the Lord's words, Naroji
told two or four men to bring alms.

**1585.** Hearing Naroji's words, they all ran off,
and Nimai sat absorbed in the Name of Hari.

**1586.** Some bring firewood and sugar, some rice,
some milk, some ghee, some fruits and roots.[^08-114]

**1587.** They brought heaps of food,
and seeing so much food my greed grew.

**1588.** I had wandered through many lands with the Lord,
but nowhere had I seen so much food.

**1589.** Having supplied all kinds of things and surrounding him on all sides,
Naroji's men stood in rows.

**1590.** As he chanted the Name of Hari, my Lord
was then lost in love for Krishna.

**1591.** Where was the milk and sugar, where the rice?
At the touch of his feet the fruits and roots were scattered.

**1592.** Two or four men said, "What kind of sannyasi is this?
He spoils heaps of food on purpose."

*[p. 146]*

**1593.** Naroji said, "I have never seen anything like it.
Why does my heart weep on seeing this sannyasi?

**1594.** Who can tell how many sins I have committed?
Why today do I wish to put on a kaupina?

**1595.** Why today does my heart weep?
Have I set my foot in the sannyasi's snare?

**1596.** All the things are spoilt — have no fear;
I will bring all these things again."

**1597.** Standing to one side, Naroji himself
gazed fixedly at Gora, the jewel of virtues.

**1598.** Tears flowed from the Lord's eyes,
and everyone stood there like puppets.

**1599.** Hearing of this, gradually the band of robbers
came out of the forest one by one.

**1600.** In the afternoon my Gora, the jewel of virtues,
fainted with love and fell to the ground.

**1601.** His body choked with love and grey with dust,
tears streaming down onto his breast.

**1602.** Weeping, Naroji said, "Listen, sannyasi,
tell me plainly: what mantra have you recited?

**1603.** Seeing your state, I think in my heart:
I will sin no more, living in this forest.

*[p. 147]*

**1604.** I am sixty years old;
I will do no more sinful deeds, I will leave worldly life.

**1605.** I, a brahmana's son, am a great villain;
do not hesitate to give me the dust of your feet.

**1606.** I have no children and no household —
why then should I go on doing sinful deeds?

**1607.** My belly will be fed if people give me alms;
why then should I stay with the robbers?

**1608.** I have come to loathe evil deeds;
I will be chief of the robbers no longer."

**1609.** Saying this, Naroji looked at his band
and at that moment threw down his weapons and arms.

**1610.** The Lord said, "Naroji, listen to what I say;
how often must I tell you again and again?

**1611.** Put on a kaupina and cover your nakedness;
feed yourself by begging and asking.

**1612.** For whom do you hoard wealth?
Father, mother, brother, friend — no one belongs to anyone.

**1613.** If a handful of rice keeps the body alive,
why hoard wealth sinfully?

**1614.** Drink spring water from the cup of your hands;
what is the use of collecting many vessels?

*[p. 148]*

**1615.** All the rich men equal to Kubera
will one day go to the city of the dead.

**1616.** The road by which the poor man goes when he leaves this body
is the very road the emperor too must take.

**1617.** Why die in vain, saying 'mine, mine'?
With love and devotion, brother, chant the Name of Hari."

**1618.** Hearing this teaching, the brahmana Naroji
wished to come along with us.

**1619.** Naroji said, "I will show you all the holy places;
from tirtha to tirtha I will follow you.

**1620.** Until now my eyes were blinded by the smoke of delusion;
from today I have thrown my weapons to the ground.

**1621.** With these hands I have committed so many murders,
with this mouth I have spoken so many harsh words.

**1622.** I will be robber chief no longer.
What a path you have shown me, refuge of the helpless!

**1623.** I always lived hidden in the jungle;
my body is riddled with sin, and I never gave it a thought."

**1624.** Saying this, the robber chief gave up everything
and, putting on a kaupina, went with the Lord.

**1625.** The robbers went off who knows where,
and Naroji came along with us.

*[p. 149]*

**1626.** Then from the Chorananadi forest
the Lord set out to see Khandala.

**1627.** The Mula river flows here, very swift;
arriving at Khandala, the Lord said to me:

**1628.** "This river is a holy tirtha;
bathing here, sins are destroyed."

**1629.** At the Lord's command I bathed
and went into the town to beg.

**1630.** Naroji Thakura follows behind me;
having begged, I came back late in the day.

**1631.** Gradually two or four people of Khandala
came and showed themselves to the Lord.

**1632.** With pure hearts they all sat round about;
one said, "Come, Lord, to my estate."

**1633.** All the people of Khandala are very hospitable;
they all tugged at him, eager to take him away.

**1634.** At last they all started a quarrel
and were ready to come to blows.

**1635.** One said, "I saw him first",
another said, "I brought the alms."

**1636.** Thus they quarrelled with one another,
and seeing this my Lord laughed inwardly.

*[p. 150]*

**1637.** A rich man said, "In my garden
I will give you alms today; go and stay there.

**1638.** You have no cloth to wear — what a shame!
I wish to give you a piece of cloth.

**1639.** If you want some money for the road,
whatever you ask, brother, I will bring at once."

**1640.** Smiling, the Lord said, "Listen, maharaja,
I have no use for luxury or wealth.

**1641.** I count even the torn cloth I wear as plenty;
I know I have no need of money.

**1642.** Along with wealth, pride increases,
and with that pride the burden of sin grows.

**1643.** This whole universe that you see with your eyes —
think where it will go.

**1644.** All will become [illegible], all will be destroyed;
only the Lord of Brahma will remain.[^08-115]

**1645.** My two companions have brought alms;
what need is there of more alms?

**1646.** The body must somehow be kept alive,
so on some days one has to take alms.

**1647.** But tell me, what will come of a lot of food?
Give it to the poor and wretched and make up their want.

*[p. 151]*

**1648.** Say 'Hari' with love, sitting beneath a tree,
and then you will all be able to cut your bonds.

**1649.** There is no happiness in the bonds of maya;
with love and devotion say 'Hari' with your lips, brothers.

**1650.** Love and devotion to God are made of rasa;
whoever knows them merges in the nectar of eternity.[^08-116]

**1651.** One who worships Krishna enjoys heaven forever;
for one who abandons Krishna there is hell."

**1652.** Saying this, my Lord spoke no more
and, closing his eyes, began to sway.

**1653.** Under the weight of his thrills his matted hair came loose,
his outer cloth slipped off and he began to dance.

**1654.** Lost in love, his body grey with dust —
what can I say of love? To tell it would be a long story.

**1655.** Chanting the Name of Hari, he passed the night sitting up,
and Naroji, sitting beside him, wiped away his sweat.

**1656.** Rising in the morning, the Lord went to the town of Nasik
to make pilgrimage with a pure heart.

**1657.** Here Thakura Lakshmana
cut off the nose of the rakshasi Shurpanakha.

**1658.** To the west of it, near Tryambak,
is the site of Rama's hut.

*[p. 152]*

**1659.** Going there, Mahaprabhu
offered hymns and praises and at last began kirtana.

**1660.** "Rama's footprints are here" —
hearing this, he ran off into the forest.

**1661.** In the depths of the dense forest, beside a spring,
two footprints stand out on a rock.

**1662.** Touching the footprints,
he was overcome by a still deeper surge of love.

**1663.** With his thrills, the matted locks on his head seemed to dance,
and his frail body seemed to swell.

**1664.** The Lord said, "Where is Rama, lord of my life?
Show yourself lovingly and soothe my heart."

**1665.** At last, clinging to my neck,
he burst into tears, saying, "Where is my Rama?"

**1666.** The fragrance of lotus comes from the Lord's body,
and a breeze began to blow softly.

**1667.** What can I say of love — I am afraid to speak of it;
never have I seen such a wondrous state.

**1668.** At every turn he calls out, "O Krishna!"
and sometimes, like a madman, looks this way and that.

**1669.** Who knows whom he calls, gazing at the sky;
sometimes he starts as though seeing something.

*[p. 153]*

**1670.** A day or two pass in fasting;
from not eating, his body has grown thin.

**1671.** Then, entering Panchavati,
he saw the Ganesha installed by Lakshmana.

**1672.** One day, in a cave in the Panchavati forest,
coming back from begging, I secretly saw:

**1673.** that deserted forest is utterly silent;
only now and then do two or four people live there.

**1674.** The forest is ringing with stillness;
with closed eyes beautiful Gauranga is contemplating something.

**1675.** A mass of radiance is coming forth from his body;
my young sannyasi is absorbed in meditation.

**1676.** Seeing this state, my eyes were dazzled,
and I crept close to watch.

**1677.** Naroji had gone somewhere to bring fruit;
I stood to one side.

**1678.** Hearing footsteps, the Lord seemed suddenly,
before my very eyes, to restrain all his feelings.

**1679.** Naroji, having gathered fruits and roots from somewhere,
stood before him with folded hands.

**1680.** After making the offering and eating a little, Gora Raya
spent the whole night sitting up.

*[p. 154]*

**1681.** Leaving Panchavati, my Gaura-Hari
went in the morning to the town of Daman.

**1682.** He did not stay even a day in the town of Daman;
leaving Daman, the Lord went north.

**1683.** Then for a fortnight, wandering and wandering,
Gora, the delight of hearts, spent his time on the road.

**1684.** Gradually he came into the kingdom of Suratha[^08-117]
and, seeing Ashtabhuja, lay rolling on the ground.

**1685.** Having seen the eight-armed Bhagavati with his own eyes,
the Lord stayed there three days.

**1686.** Ashtabhuja was installed by King Suratha;
seeing Bhagavati, he felt boundless joy.

**1687.** In the goddess's temple there was a sannyasi;
he wished to ask the Lord something.

**1688.** The sannyasi said, "Come, come, sannyasi gosai;
never have I seen a holy man like you.

**1689.** Seeing you, devotion arises in my heart;
tell me, how shall I cross the ocean of worldly existence?

**1690.** How should one worship the Supreme Lord?
Tell me this and remove my anxiety."

**1691.** The Lord said, "I know no essential truth;
Bhavani will dispel all the darkness of your mind.

*[p. 155]*

**1692.** As an ordinary heroine, seeing a handsome hero,
looks at him with her heart full of passion,[^08-118]

**1693.** in that same mood call on Krishna again and again,
and the darkness of your mind will vanish of itself."

**1694.** While he was speaking, a brahmana
came to the goddess to sacrifice a goat.

**1695.** The Lord said, "You make the sacrifice in order to eat it,
and do not know what your fate will be afterwards.

**1696.** The goddess is a pure form — so the shastras say;
how could she eat what is forbidden?

**1697.** King Suratha offered a hundred thousand sacrifices;
in the city of the dead a hundred thousand swords fall upon him.

**1698.** You do not study the words of the shastras,
yet you practise religion by killing animals.

**1699.** It was the meat-eating rakshasas who, in order to eat,
laid down the rule of killing animals.

**1700.** Non-violence is the highest dharma, say all the shastras;
be kind to living beings and joy will arise.

**1701.** Maya has bound you tightly;
how will you cut the bonds without a sword?

**1702.** You crave tamasic food, and that is why
you are so eager to slaughter sheep and goats before the goddess.

*[p. 156]*

**1703.** You think you will be saved by killing an animal,
and that is why you have come to make a sacrifice.

**1704.** You can drive the soul out of the body,
but can you put the soul back into a dead body?

**1705.** If someone before the goddess, with devotion,
were to cut off your head as a human sacrifice —

**1706.** tell me, brother, how would your heart feel?
Let the animal go; I want to see it with my own eyes.

**1707.** The eight-armed Bhagavati would eat wine and meat?
Hearing such a thing, a holy man would laugh it away.

**1708.** Turn your minds to the eternal dharma
and, following the shastras, give up evil practices.

**1709.** The goddess is a supreme Vaishnavi and does not eat meat;
why then do you try to beguile her with sacrifices?

**1710.** If killing living beings were dharma,
why are robbers not called holy men?

**1711.** The fisherman kills many fish every day —
why should I not call him righteous?

**1712.** Killing men and killing animals are great sins;
if you commit this sin, the threefold misery will increase."

**1713.** Going to see the eight-armed Bhagavati,
he gave this teaching, examining the shastras.

*[p. 157]*

**1714.** The man who had come to worship Durga
listened to the Lord's words with devotion.

**1715.** Hearing the Lord's words, he became detached,
and the brahmana let the sacrificial goat go.

**1716.** Worshipping with flowers and bilva leaves, the excellent brahmana
went back home happily.

**1717.** The Lord sat down firmly before the goddess
and with folded hands began a hymn to Bhavani.

**1718.** Having offered praise and obeisance to Bhavani, Gora Raya
hurried towards the river Tapti, a great tirtha.

**1719.** Bathing three times a day in the waters of the Tapti,
he went to see the image of Vamana.

**1720.** On an open plain near the Tapti
an image of Lord Vamana is installed.

**1721.** King Bali installed this image;
for this reason the Tapti became a tirtha.

**1722.** Vamana bathed in the waters of the Tapti,
and that is why the Tapti is called a great tirtha.

**1723.** Having bowed at the feet of Lord Vamana,
Gaura-Hari went to see the sacrificial pit.

**1724.** To see the sacrificial pit in the town of Bharoch,
he left the Tapti and went to the bank of the Narmada.

*[p. 158]*

**1725.** In Bharoch King Bali performed sacrifice at a sacrificial pit;
he goes with a joyful heart to see it.

**1726.** Seeing with his own eyes the huge hollow of the pit,
boundless joy arose in Chaitanya's heart.

**1727.** Having bathed in the Narmada, a great tirtha,
Gora, the delight of hearts, went to the town of Baroda.

**1728.** East of Baroda is Thakura Dakorji;[^08-119]
the Lord wished to see Dakorji.

**1729.** In Dakorji's courtyard there is a huge tamala tree,
and beneath it the darling of Shachi stood.

**1730.** Having seen Dakorji, the Lord offered obeisance and praise
and came back again to the town of Baroda.

**1731.** The king of Baroda is very virtuous;
the worthy king is devoted to the service of Govinda.

**1732.** With his own hands he sweeps Govinda's temple;
people say to one another that the king is like Ambarisha.

**1733.** The maharaja is always busy for Govinda's sake;
the king is always full of love for Govinda's service.

**1734.** Gathering tulasi with his own hands,
the king offers it at Govinda's lotus feet with devotion.

**1735.** In the evening Gora goes to Govinda's house
and, seeing Govinda, rolls on the ground in love.

*[p. 159]*

**1736.** A single torn outer cloth, the look of a madman;
the Lord is always intoxicated, absorbed in Krishna.

**1737.** His whole body smeared with dust, his eyes closed;
seeing Govinda, he sheds tears.

**1738.** After three days a calamity occurred here:
Naroji died of fever.

**1739.** At the time of death Gora Raya sat before him
and stroked Naroji's body with his lotus hand.

**1740.** When Naroji closed his eyes,
he himself gave Krishna's name into his ear from his holy lips.

**1741.** Naroji Thakura is very fortunate:
Bhagavan gave Krishna's name into his ear.

**1742.** At the time of death Naroji, with folded hands,
looking towards the Lord, said "Hari, Hari".

**1743.** Taking Naroji in his arms, Lord Vishvambhara
moved him from beneath the tamala tree.

**1744.** Having begged [what was needed], Naroji was buried,
and the Lord performed kirtana circling the grave.

**1745.** The maharaja, hearing of this by word of mouth,
came himself with a crowd to see the sannyasi.

**1746.** Seeing the Lord, the king bowed down,
and the Lord said nothing, good or bad.

*[p. 160]*

**1747.** Respectfully the king invited him to take alms.
The Lord said, "I take alms at householders' doors.

**1748.** I have no need of alms of luxury;
that is why I do not ask alms at your door."

**1749.** With folded hands the king asked him to accept alms,
and, having no choice, he told me to accept them.

**1750.** At the Lord's sign, from the king,
like an ordinary man, I begged a handful of alms.

**1751.** Having given alms, the maharaja went away,
and Gora-chand completed his daily rites.

**1752.** Rising in the morning, we go westwards
and, going a little way, reach the Mahanadi.[^08-120]

**1753.** A very swift river, beautiful to see,
with many stones rushing along in it.

**1754.** Crossing the river, my Gora, the delight of hearts,
reached the vicinity of Ahmedabad.

**1755.** Wondrous Ahmedabad, a splendid city:
how many gardens, how many charming houses!

**1756.** Huge mansions stand in splendour in its midst;
the people of the country are always devoted to serving guests.

**1757.** The village people regard a guest as equal to a god
and serve guests with all their hearts.

*[p. 161]*

**1758.** Enchanted by the Lord's beauty, people
came with devotion and stood all around.

**1759.** Some said, "Listen, listen, young sannyasi,
come to my house; I will give you alms and serve you."

**1760.** The Lord said, "I will not go to a householder's home;
tonight I will stay by the Nandani."

**1761.** There is a beautiful garden called Nandani;
at its edge Lord Vishvambhara settled down.

**1762.** Seeing this, the village people brought alms,
and at night Gora-chand made the offering.

**1763.** Many people came and surrounded the Lord
and, seeing the sannyasi, spoke with devotion.

**1764.** A pandit came and showed himself
and began to recite verses of the Srimad-Bhagavata.

**1765.** The Lord said, "Sing Krishna's glories well;
I wish to forget everything while hearing the verses.

**1766.** You study the Bhagavata every day;
seeing you, the torment of worldly life goes away.

**1767.** Every day you sing the glories of Krishna;
blessed, blessed are you, brahmana, you are most fortunate."

**1768.** Having talked with the Lord, the brahmana
called all the people and said:

*[p. 162]*

**1769.** "All of you serve this sannyasi well;
this sannyasi is no ordinary man — he must be some god.

**1770.** Seeing him, detachment arises;
know for certain he is no ordinary man."

**1771.** I cannot understand all the speech of the people;
what I can, I write down from gestures and hints.

**1772.** Having long travelled the tirthas of this country,
Shachi's darling understands everyone's speech.

**1773.** Sometimes, asking the Lord about a word or two,
I write it down in my karcha after thinking it over.

**1774.** The lila that I have seen with my own eyes
I record in my karcha very secretly.[^08-121]

**1775.** Always intoxicated, the Lord, in an ecstasy of love for Krishna,
wanders from tirtha to tirtha, from land to land.

**1776.** In Ahmedabad many people gathered;
they all come in crowds to see the Lord.

**1777.** Seeing many people all around, Gora Raya,
drunk with joy, distributes the Name to all.

**1778.** The Lord said, "With devotion all of you chant the Name;
all suffering will go far away and no sorrow will remain.

**1779.** Do not despise anyone out of pride;
free from pride, say 'Hare Krishna Hare'."

---

### Translator's notes

[^08-114]: D. C. Sen quotes this couplet in his introduction as evidence in the dispute over whether Chaitanya ate rice (*ata-chuna*).
[^08-115]: The line is badly corrupted in the edition; the translation is approximate.
[^08-116]: The line is partly corrupted; translated from the sense.
[^08-117]: The edition reads "*Harather rajya*", apparently "the kingdom of Suratha" (cf. below, "Suratha installed Ashtabhuja").
[^08-118]: *Ragatmika*, "full of passion (*raga*)", a term of the Gaudiya Vaishnava theory of spontaneous devotion.
[^08-119]: Dakorji is Ranchhodrai at Dakor (Gujarat), a famous deity of Krishna.
[^08-120]: Probably the river Mahi (Gujarat).
[^08-121]: Another statement by the author, key for the whole book, about how he kept his notes.

---

# Part 9

*The Sabarmati and two Bengalis. Ghogha and the courtesan Barmukhi. Ruined Somnath. Junagadh and Girnar: Dattatreya's footprints and the story of Krishna's footprints (pp. 163–182)*

*[p. 163]*

**1780.** "He is not a pandit who prides himself on learning,
if his heart has not been purified by the rasa of devotion.

**1781.** One who counts millions of universes as straw
and is drunk with love — him I accept as a devotee.

**1782.** Love and devotion are the essence of truth, so the shrutis say;
the devotee of Hari, drunk with love, does not want liberation.

**1783.** One for whom love and devotion are the ornament of his throat
is an eternal associate of Krishna.

**1784.** One who tastes the sweetness[^09-122] of love for Krishna —
him I will serve at his feet without dispute.

**1785.** One who, in this very body, has cut his bonds
and is drunk with love for Krishna — he is a thakura.

**1786.** Mahamaya, throwing dust into the eye of knowledge,
has tied a knot between consciousness and matter.

**1787.** That is why the foolish in this world,
deluded, identify the self with the inert body.

**1788.** One who gives up identification with the inert body
is my master — so the Vedas say.

**1789.** The supreme Vaishnava, immersed in love for Krishna,
does not dissemble by making a great noise.

**1790.** One who does not know the primary meaning of Vedanta
regards the jiva and Brahman as one."

*[p. 164]*

**1791.** Saying this, the next day Gora, the delight of hearts,
left the town and went west.

**1792.** Going a little way, we saw the river Shubhramati,[^09-123]
singing with a murmuring voice, full of rasa.

**1793.** Crossing the river, we saw two or four people
going to Dvaraka on pilgrimage.

**1794.** Among them I saw two Bengalis —
great devotees, Ramananda and Govinda Charana.

**1795.** Seeing people from Gauda after so long,
my heart seemed to dance with joy.

**1796.** I asked Ramananda, "Where is your home?"
Ramananda said, "Brother, in Kulina-gram."[^09-124]

**1797.** The Lord is bathing in the river Shubhramati,
and meanwhile Ramananda is talking with me.

**1798.** Ramananda said, "Where are you going?"
I said, "I am going with the Lord to Dvaraka."

**1799.** Ramananda, hearing Chaitanyadeva's name,
lit up in the face like a flash of lightning.

**1800.** Ramananda ran and bowed down,
and Chaitanya asked him a few questions.

**1801.** Ramananda Dasa is a supreme Vaishnava;
the Lord gave Ramananda Dasa encouragement.

*[p. 165]*

**1802.** The Lord said, "Ramananda, seeing you,
the feeling of Gauda has awakened in my heart.

**1803.** How long ago did you leave home?
How long ago did you come to this holy land?

**1804.** Come then, let us go together to Dvaraka,
and all of us joyfully behold the Lord of Dvaraka."

**1805.** Hearing this from the Lord's lips, Ramananda Dasa
rejoiced to stay with the Lord.

**1806.** Having bathed, the Lord walks slowly on
and arrives at a village called Ghogha.

**1807.** A courtesan named Barmukhi[^09-125] lives here;
there is no telling of her treasures.

**1808.** By her trade she has amassed great wealth;
her clothes and ornaments are very costly.

**1809.** Barmukhi lives in a huge house
and goes about by every trick to rob the rich of their wealth.

**1810.** Wearing [fine clothes], she looks about languidly;
how many libertines go to her house!

**1811.** She lives here with many servants and maids;
everyone knows of her pomp and fame.

**1812.** There is a huge garden called Piyar-kanan;[^09-126]
the Lord went to the edge of that garden.

*[p. 166]*

**1813.** A very large neem tree stands here;
who knows why, but the Lord went and sat there.

**1814.** At his command I go to householders' doors
to beg for fruits, roots and the like.

**1815.** Having begged, I came back at midday,
and the Lord made the offering with a joyful heart.

**1816.** Then the three of us received the prasada —
I, Ramananda and Govinda Charana.

**1817.** Laughing, I called Govinda my "mite".[^09-127]
The Lord said, "Why do you leave Ramananda out?

**1818.** If Govinda has become your mite,
then Ramananda shall be my mite."

**1819.** Laughing, he called Ramananda his mite,
and began the Name, clapping his hands.

**1820.** Ramananda, hearing this from the Lord's lips,
stood to one side, rubbing his hands in embarrassment.

**1821.** Many people gathered to hear the Name,
and tears flowed from the Lord's eyes in a hundred streams.

**1822.** The tears began to spurt like a squirt-gun,
and seeing this the people of Ghogha were amazed.

**1823.** Seeing that Hari-sankirtana of the Lord,
two or four people became drunk with love.

*[p. 167]*

**1824.** Tears flow from the eyes of the village people;
many people came and stood in rows.

**1825.** What a mood of devotion it was cannot be described;
without blinking they all gaze at the Lord's face.

**1826.** Sometimes the Lord laughs, sometimes he weeps,
sometimes, raising his arms, he dances and sings.

**1827.** Sometimes he trembles all over and sweat flows,
sometimes in an ecstasy of love he remains silent.

**1828.** Sometimes he totters, his hair on end,
sometimes he calls aloud, "Krishna, my life!"

**1829.** "The young sannyasi is drunk with love of God" —
so the people of Ghogha whisper to one another.

**1830.** As he says "Hari, Hari", streams of bliss flow;
all stand there like puppets.

**1831.** His eyes half-closed, his matted hair loosened,
his body smeared with dust and grime.

**1832.** "Where is Krishna, my life?" he calls,
sometimes raising his hands and gazing upwards.

**1833.** "Govinda, where is Krishna? Bring him to me!
Where is Krishna, my life? Show him to me!"

**1834.** Once, saying this, he ran off
and, spreading his arms, embraced the neem tree.

*[p. 168]*

**1835.** Nimai became drunk with love of God;
never have I seen such madness.

**1836.** For many days I have been with him, wandering through many lands,
but never have I seen such ecstasy.

**1837.** Ramananda and Govinda Charana on either side
clapped their hands and raised the cry of "Hari" again and again.

**1838.** At the roadside there was a huge pit,
and in his ecstasy he rolled down into it.

**1839.** A wicked man came along, bustling about,
and said to the Lord, "Why are you deceiving people?

**1840.** You will fool the village people and take their money;
that is why you go about shouting 'Hari'.

**1841.** I have come to test the sannyasi;
I have seen hundreds of false sannyasis."

**1842.** When that unbeliever said this,
the village people wanted to thrash him.

**1843.** The Lord said, "Brothers, whom will you beat?
Make him drink the nectar of the Name of Hari.

**1844.** His throat has dried up with thirst;
pour a stream of nectar into his mouth.

**1845.** Without devotion his heart has withered;
give him the Name and destroy his fear of Yama.

*[p. 169]*

**1846.** The unbeliever's heart is like a desert;
give it the power to bear fruit.

**1847.** Come to me, holy man, I will give you the Name of Hari;
I will lift the burden of your sins from you.

**1848.** By the power of this mantra all suffering will go;
reciting the mantra of the Name of Hari bears fruit at once.

**1849.** One who recites this great mantra —
that sinner never goes to hell.

**1850.** When there is such an easy mantra in the world,
why should the sinner wander uselessly on evil paths?"

**1851.** Saying this, Mahaprabhu went up to him
and poured the nectar of the Name of Hari into his ears.

**1852.** Merciful Chaitanya, to deliver living beings,
wanders here and there, undisturbed.

**1853.** Seeing all this from her window,
Barmukhi pondered within herself.

**1854.** Having seen the Lord's wondrous mercy with her own eyes,
sitting alone, she reproached herself.

**1855.** Barmukhi said, "For shame, for shame! For the sake of money
I sell my body a hundred times a day.

**1856.** By sinful trade, uniting with other men —
for shame! — every day I indulge in amorous play.

*[p. 170]*

**1857.** This sannyasi I see is equal to God;
I will leave everything and go to him.

**1858.** The sannyasi has no money or cowries with him —
why then am I happy when I see him?

**1859.** Why should I suffer hell sitting at home?
Will Hari not have mercy on me too?

**1860.** What mantra did this sannyasi recite into the ear of Balaji the villain,[^09-128]
that he delivered him?

**1861.** I will go to him and destroy my sins;
I will go up to him and clasp his feet tightly."

**1862.** So Barmukhi spoke from the window,
and everyone was glad to hear her words.

**1863.** The people all around, taking up the news,
made a great commotion, laughing.

**1864.** A moment later the courtesan came down,
and her maid named Mira went behind her.

**1865.** Then Barmukhi said humbly to Mira,
"From today I give all my wealth to you.

**1866.** I have much money — I count it all as nothing;
from today I have become a beggar on the road."

**1867.** Barmukhi, the maidservant,[^09-129] let down her hair,
like a bank of clouds beside motionless lightning.

*[p. 171]*

**1868.** The long net of her hair falls below her hips,
while Shachi's darling sits with his eyes closed.

**1869.** All, seeing the wondrous radiance of her beauty,
stand gazing at her face.

**1870.** Barmukhi, with folded hands, says again and again,
"Cut my bonds, my sannyasi.

**1871.** I am a great sinner, a worm of hell;
if you do not have mercy, I shall go to perdition.

**1872.** Tell your maidservant by what means I shall be saved,
how, after death, I shall escape the fear of Yama.

**1873.** What further use have I for this sinful body?"
Saying this, she cut off her long hair.

**1874.** Putting on simple clothes, she covered her nakedness
and stood before the Lord with folded hands.

**1875.** The Lord said, "Barmukhi, I will tell you
a few words — carry them out in every respect.

**1876.** Make a tulasi grove here,
and living in its midst, practise the worship of Krishna."

**1877.** "You are Krishna, you are Hari," said Barmukhi,
and saying just this she fell at the Lord's feet.

**1878.** When Barmukhi fell at his feet,
the Lord at once drew back three or four steps.

*[p. 172]*

**1879.** And all the other people standing near
cried, "Blessed, blessed!" on seeing the courtesan.

**1880.** The maid Mirabai wept bitterly,
and Barmukhi said to her with a smile:

**1881.** "Listen carefully, Mira, to my words:
I have given you all the wealth I have.

**1882.** Serve guests well when they come;
in solitude, set your mind on the Name of Hari.

**1883.** Do no sinful deeds — I adjure you;
worship Sri Radha-Krishna with loving attachment.

**1884.** To love is good indeed, but not with a rogue;
make your love with Krishna, Mira.

**1885.** Give body, mind and life — everything — to Krishna;
then you will gain Krishna, the eternal treasure.

**1886.** Listen carefully to what I say, Mira:
keep company with no one but Krishna.

**1887.** Krishna will surely be merciful to you;
never, at any cost, give up the treasure that is Krishna.

**1888.** By the Lord's mercy my bonds are cut;
from today my dwelling is the tulasi grove."
