**1889.** Saying this, Barmukhi took her chanting beads
and made a tulasi grove, forgetting all her pain.

*[p. 173]*

**1890.** Having given devotion to the courtesan Barmukhi,
the Lord hurried off to see Somnath.

**1891.** The Lord goes in the direction of Jafarabad,
and with great difficulty arrives there in three days.

**1892.** The people of Jafarabad are very poor,
yet they show great honour to guests.

**1893.** Many villagers brought alms,
and my Lord made rotis and offered them.

**1894.** Going into a gardener's garden,
we spent the night there happily.

**1895.** Rising in the morning, we go to Somnath
and, after six days, arrive there.

**1896.** There is none of its former beauty, nor that temple;
seeing its sorry state, tears flowed from my eyes.

**1897.** Heaps of rubble and broken remains are there;
seeing them, I felt a great pain in my heart.

**1898.** The beauty of the temple buildings has gone;
seeing this, my Lord weeps in distress.

**1899.** Weeping, my Lord began to say,
"What has the wicked Yavana[^09-130] done here!

**1900.** Where have you hidden, Lord, for fear of the Yavanas?
Show yourself once and soothe my heart."

*[p. 174]*

**1901.** Alas, alas, this sorrow is beyond words;
Gora Raya weeps, calling on Somnath.

**1902.** The Lord said, "Who carried off such splendour?
For money's sake the villain brought it to this.

**1903.** O Lord Somnath, to see you
my heart is in turmoil — I cannot bear it.

**1904.** I can bear separation from you no longer;
calling on you, my heart is breaking.

**1905.** Alas, alas, what sin did the devotees commit,
for what sin do they no longer see you, O god?

**1906.** In separation from you hundreds of pandas[^09-131]
are sunk in an ocean of sorrow.

**1907.** Did you go to Kailasa for fear of the Yavanas
and now forget your dear devotees?

**1908.** Seeing all this my heart is bursting,
and tears stream down onto my breast.

**1909.** Ah, only ruins are left lying here —
how can sinful eyes bear it?

**1910.** Come, Lord Somnath, into my heart;
in my heart I shall behold your form.

**1911.** Where have you hidden, Lord? I do not see you.
To whom shall I tell how my soul feels?

*[p. 175]*

**1912.** Alas, alas, Gangadhara! To see you
people will no longer come from foreign lands.

**1913.** Pilgrims used to come to see you with pride,
but now that glory has been wiped away.

**1914.** The Yavanas in their hatred, committing outrages,
have carried off gems, pearls and other riches.

**1915.** Alas, Lord Smarahara,[^09-132] where have you stayed?
In your mercy you do not show yourself to your devotees."

**1916.** Thus my Lord lamented,
and just then a storm arose in the sky.

**1917.** Dust flew up and darkened all around,
and the pandas shut the doors of their huts.

**1918.** We all sat by the outer door,
and the Lord, the Hari-bola, came and sat in our midst.

**1919.** Just then an avadhuta sannyasi came
and looked again and again at Gora-chand.

**1920.** His whole body smeared with ash, no cloth,
his matted hair tied up high, of wondrous form.

**1921.** His two eyes are red,
"Hara, Hara" on his lips, his heart pure.

**1922.** Two languid eyes, beautiful to see;
he came and blessed him with raised hand.[^09-133]

*[p. 176]*

**1923.** Seeing him, my Lord rose,
and he, saying something, vanished.

**1924.** Where the avadhuta went, he was seen no more.
Then, with a slight smile, my Chaitanya

**1925.** walked three times around Somnath.
I, Ramananda and Govinda Charana

**1926.** sing Hari-sankirtana with the Lord;
for the pleasure of Thakura Somnath

**1927.** the Lord sings kirtana, melting with love.
Two or four pandas came and joined us

**1928.** and began asking us for something.
Smiling, the Lord said, "A sannyasi

**1929.** has no money, cowries, food or cloth to give."
Hearing these words, Govinda Charana

**1930.** placed two coins in the panda's hand.
The panda took us to the edge of the holy tank,

**1931.** and my Lord took water and poured it on his head.
Leaving Somnath, we go to Junagadh.[^09-134]

**1932.** A large place indeed, but there is no tirtha;
many mansions stand in splendour all around.

**1933.** In Junagadh Gora Raya spent two days.

*[p. 177]*

**1934.** In one place there is the worship of Ranchhodji;
in the evening we go there for darshan.

**1935.** An excellent brahmana named Miraji performs the service;
we go and arrive at his house.

**1936.** Miraji received us with devotion,
and the Lord spent the night at his house.

**1937.** The brahmana brings milk, sugar and flour,
and the Lord spends the night joyfully.

**1938.** Nearby is the most charming Girnar hill;[^09-135]
Lord Vishvambhara goes towards it.

**1939.** Miraji, coming up with devotion,
asks the Lord again and again to stay.

**1940.** Humbly the Lord says to the brahmana,
"We shall all go to Girnar hill.

**1941.** There I shall see the feet of Guru Dattatreya;
let me go — that is the alms I ask of you."

**1942.** Hearing the Lord's words, the worthy brahmana
said nothing more, good or bad.

**1943.** Chaitanya Gosai sets out,
and we follow behind him like shadows.

**1944.** A band of sannyasis had come to this place
and were sitting with gloomy faces.

*[p. 178]*

**1945.** Their leader, named Bhargadeva,
had fallen ill and was staying there.

**1946.** Bhargadeva is tossing about beneath a tree;
the Lord came there quickly.

**1947.** Seeing Bhargadeva ill, Gora Raya
orders me to look after him.

**1948.** I, Ramananda and Govinda Charana —
the three of us set to nursing the sick man.

**1949.** The Lord tells us to give him neem juice to drink,
and we make neem juice and give it to him.

**1950.** Freed from his illness, Bhargadeva
bows countless times at the Lord's feet.

**1951.** Bhargadeva rises and praises the Lord;
rubbing his hands, Bharga says with devotion:

**1952.** "Have mercy on me, Lord, you are merciful;
my heart is eager for you.

**1953.** You have freed this lowly one from disease;
in your mercy cut the noose of maya too.

**1954.** Whom are you deceiving, young sannyasi?
I long to attain your feet.

**1955.** If you have no mercy on the lowly,
why should I call you merciful?

*[p. 179]*

**1956.** I am old, and my eyes deceive me:
in your heart I see a golden doll.

**1957.** Everyone says your colour is golden,
but my eyes see you dark, like Krishna.

**1958.** That is why I say my eyes are faulty;
in your mercy deliver this sinner."[^09-136]

**1959.** In his mercy the Lord infused power into Bharga,
and at once devotion welled up in his heart.

**1960.** What the Lord said to Bhargadeva with a wink is not known,
but at once tears flowed from his eyes.

**1961.** The sannyasi's disciples did not grasp the subtle truth,
while Bharga went with the Lord to Girnar.

**1962.** Girnar hill is very high;
the two feet of Guru Dattatreya are there.

**1963.** On the high summit of Girnar are the two feet;
the band of sannyasis goes to see the feet.

**1964.** In the morning we set out to see the feet,
and in the afternoon we reach the feet.

**1965.** Two footprints stand out on a rock;
seeing the feet, the Lord bowed down.

**1966.** Flag, thunderbolt and goad adorn the soles;[^09-137]
seeing the lotus feet, the Lord says "Hari, Hari".

*[p. 180]*

**1967.** A panda lives here permanently;
Vishvambhara asks him about the feet.

**1968.** The panda said, "When the Yadus perished,
then Baladeva came here.

**1969.** Baladeva came here for austerity;
the Lord took his yogic seat and began his austerities.

**1970.** Baladeva sat in austerity in his yogic posture,
while at Prabhasa the Yadavas began to fight.

**1971.** Drunk on mead, all the heroes
fought one another, and blood flowed.

**1972.** Satyaki and all the other heroes
one by one went to the abode of Yama.

**1973.** By Krishna's will all the Yadus perished,
and at last Krishna appeared on the mountain.

**1974.** Here, seeing Baladeva, the Lord of the Yadus
began to speak of his own departure.

**1975.** Krishna says to Baladeva, 'I shall go to Goloka;
my work is accomplished, I shall stay no longer.

**1976.** The earth has been filled with the sins of the Yadavas,
and that is why the Yadus have been destroyed.

**1977.** If the sons of Pandu weep for me,
you will relieve their grief.

*[p. 181]*

**1978.** Drupada's daughter is dearer to me than life;
I beg you, comfort her first.'

**1979.** Hearing this, Baladeva burst into tears
and humbly said these words:

**1980.** 'Vidura, Uddhava and all the other devotees —
what shall I tell them when you are gone?

**1981.** Leave some sign for them,
seeing which they will be filled with love.

**1982.** You are their very life — knowing and understanding this,
how can you go to Goloka?

**1983.** They know nothing but Krishna;
how can you abandon them and go to your own abode?

**1984.** When Panchali raises her cry of grief,
what shall I say to console her? Consider it yourself.'

**1985.** Hearing this, Krishna set his feet here,
and at once the footprints remained here."

**1986.** When the panda had explained this,
love at once arose in the Lord's heart.

**1987.** Gora, the abode of bliss, the home of love,
gazes steadily at the footprints.

**1988.** As he gazed at the prints, a waterfall of love
suddenly overflowed in his heart.

*[p. 182]*

**1989.** Choked with feeling, the Lord says slowly,
"Brother panda, you are a holy man; what a jewel you have shown!

**1990.** Every day you gain happiness from seeing them;
I see no one as virtuous as you.

**1991.** If this print were to fall on a heart of stone,
the bliss of Brahman would arise in it every day."

**1992.** Laying his head on the footprint, Gora, the delight of hearts,
falls prostrate on it again and again.

**1993.** His body, thin as a cane,
swelled with love, finding its chance.

**1994.** Touching the feet, the Lord closed his eyes,
and tears began to flow down his breast.

**1995.** He circles the feet, clapping his hands,
and his waist-cloth and the knot of his hair slipped loose.

**1996.** Seeing this state, Ramananda lost consciousness,
and Govinda Charana fell rolling on the ground.

**1997.** Coming down from the mountain, my Gora Raya
spends the night on the bank of the river called Bhadra.

**1998.** Rising in the morning, we all cross the river
and gradually come in sight of the Dhanvidhara thickets.

**1999.** The Dhanvidhara thickets are very extensive;
seeing those thickets, I was afraid.

---

### Translator's notes

[^09-122]: *Shikharini* is a sweet dish of yogurt with sugar; a metaphor of sweetness.
[^09-123]: Shubhramati is the Sabarmati.
[^09-124]: Kulina-gram is a village in Bardhaman, home of Ramananda Vasu and other associates of Chaitanya.
[^09-125]: The episode of the courtesan Barmukhi (Bar-mukhi) is one of the best known in the *Karcha*.
[^09-126]: *Piyar-kanan*, literally "garden of love".
[^09-127]: *Mite* (*mita*): a sworn friend, a bosom companion. A joking play on names: Govinda is the author's namesake, and Ramananda is the namesake of Ramananda Raya, Chaitanya's friend.
[^09-128]: Apparently the name of the villain of the preceding episode, Balaji.
[^09-129]: "Maidservant" here in the sense of "maidservant of the Lord": Barmukhi already calls herself so.
[^09-130]: *Yavana*: a foreigner, a Muslim. Somnath was destroyed by Mahmud of Ghazni in 1025 and repeatedly thereafter.
[^09-131]: Pandas are temple priests and pilgrim guides.
[^09-132]: Smarahara, "destroyer of Kama", is an epithet of Shiva. Gangadhara, "bearer of the Ganga", is also an epithet of Shiva.
[^09-133]: A passage is repeated here in the edition (pp. 175–176); the repetition is omitted.
[^09-134]: From this point the lines in the edition appear to be out of order (the couplets are broken); the translation follows the order of the lines.
[^09-135]: Girnar (Gunagarh) is the sacred mountain at Junagadh, a place of worship of Dattatreya.
[^09-136]: Compare the similar episode with another Bharga in Part 5.
[^09-137]: Flag, thunderbolt (*vajra*) and goad (*ankusha*) are auspicious marks on the feet of God.

---

# Part 10

*The Dhanvidhara thickets. Prabhasa. Dvaraka. The return journey: Baroda, the Narmada, farewell to Bharga, Dohad, Kukshi — the poor brahmana and the merchant's dream (pp. 183–202)*

*[p. 183]*

**2000.** Lions, tigers and all kinds of beasts live here;
thinking of it, I was very much afraid.

**2001.** Understanding my mind from a hint,
the Lord smiled and said, "Why are you afraid for nothing?

**2002.** If the Name of Hari removes the fear of Yama,
why are you afraid at the sight of these thickets?"

**2003.** With our whole party we were sixteen people;
the son of Shachi entered the thickets.

**2004.** The beauty of the jungle is most charming;
what can I say of its beauty — it would take long to tell.

**2005.** How many wild flowers have bloomed, scenting the air,
how many hundreds of trees and creepers sway in the wind!

**2006.** Sitting on the branches, birds of many kinds are singing;
hearing that song, the heart is overwhelmed.

**2007.** Through the middle runs a single path, jungle on both sides,
and now and then bands of sannyasis appear.

**2008.** I see the sun overhead,
and at once, for hunger, I look this way and that.

**2009.** "To whose door shall I go now for alms?
There are no village people here — where shall I get alms?"

**2010.** On both sides various trees are laden with fruit;
seeing the fruit, my eagerness grew.

*[p. 184]*

**2011.** I cannot describe the wondrous fruit;
so many fruits have ripened and stand out in rows.

**2012.** The fruit is shaped like a star-fruit;
never have I tasted fruit like it.

**2013.** All around the fruit lies in heaps;
all the sannyasis are eating the fruit with both hands.

**2014.** Without permission I cannot eat the fruit,
but seeing the fruit, greed seized me.

**2015.** I take some fruit for the Lord,
and in the afternoon the Lord offers the fruit.

**2016.** Having tasted two or four fruits,
Gora, the delight of hearts, tells us to eat.

**2017.** I eat my fill of fruit, as much as I can,
and put as many more as I find into the bag.

**2018.** Govinda Charana gobbles fruit after fruit,
while Ramananda tastes them slowly.

**2019.** All saw the wondrous property of the fruit:
that wild fruit takes away both hunger and thirst.

**2020.** On a tree like a four-sided cactus,
wondrous fruit — I eat it with great greed.

**2021.** The more I eat, the more fruits I see;
I put more into the bag and eat till I am stuffed.

*[p. 185]*

**2022.** In the dense jungle there is no smell of man;
now and then everyone raises the cry of "Hari".

**2023.** Before evening had come it grew dark on the path;
the son of Shachi sat down beneath a tree.

**2024.** Here and there the king has made halting-places;
we go down to one with the Lord.

**2025.** A place fenced with forest logs, without house or door;
all the sannyasis sat down here.

**2026.** Clapping his hands, the Lord began the Name,
and hearing the Name, the sannyasis grew excited.

**2027.** Gathering wood, they lit a fire;
Bhargadeva chants the Name, clapping his hands.

**2028.** In the middle of that jungle we feel no fear;
chanting the Name of Hari, we all pass the night.

**2029.** Next morning, raising the cry of "Hari",
Gora set out, remembering Sri Hari.

**2030.** The further we go, the denser the jungle;
seeing those thickets, the body trembles.

**2031.** Going a long way, we find a small stream,
and there Shachi's darling bathes.

**2032.** Having bathed, we hurry on ahead
and, after going some distance, all sit down.

*[p. 186]*

**2033.** The Lord tells Ramananda to bring fruit,
and Ramananda brings fruit and puts it down there.

**2034.** He gathers and brings fruits of every kind,
and Gora, the delight of hearts, worships and makes the offering.

**2035.** Never have I seen such sweet fruit;
all together we fill our bellies with fruit.

**2036.** Food for thousands of people lies on the road;
to whom shall I tell how great God's mercy is!

**2037.** Finishing our tasks by midday, we go on,
and in the afternoon we all find another halting-place.

**2038.** A place at the edge of the jungle, fenced with stakes;
there Gora, the delight of hearts, entered.

**2039.** Bringing wood, the sannyasis lit a fire,
and the Lord, clapping his hands, began the song:

**2040.** "Hare Krishna, Hare Krishna, Hare Krishna, Hare" —
this song the Lord sings at any hour.

**2041.** As he sang I saw that he was beside himself;
the Lord was filled with thrills and his body trembled.

**2042.** Gora Raya began to weep aloud,
and seeing his state Bharga cried out.

**2043.** Next day, rising in the morning, we go on;
on the road a band of pilgrims comes back towards us.

*[p. 187]*

**2044.** When the two bands met on the road,
all joyfully raised the cry of "Hari".

**2045.** Thus in seven days the Dhanvidhara thickets
we all crossed, spreading joy.

**2046.** Nearby is Amarapuri, named Gopitala;
we all go there, to the abode of joy.

**2047.** Everyone calls this the holy place of Prabhasa;
seeing Prabhasa, I felt great delight.

**2048.** Where the Yadus gave up their bodies,
there the Lord went and wept a great deal.

**2049.** Drunk on mead, all the Yadu heroes
fought one another and gave up their bodies.

**2050.** Without counting who was kin and who a stranger,
by Krishna's will the Yadu heroes died.

**2051.** Charudeshna, Sura, Satyaki, Yuyudhana,
Samba, Gada and all the other wise ones

**2052.** died there, fighting one another;
with a sorrowful face the Lord weeps at that place.

**2053.** No one gains so much joy from weeping:
weeping, the Lord scatters bliss over the earth.

**2054.** To take away the grief and sorrow of the world,
he preaches the Name of Hari at all times.

*[p. 188]*

**2055.** The Name of Hari, love, devotion, the worship of Hari —
these the Lord teaches the people of the world unceasingly.

**2056.** Day or night, he goes from door to door
and distributes the Name of Hari throughout the world:

**2057.** "Who will take the Name of Hari? Come forward!
I give everyone this jewel without price.

**2058.** All of you, take this priceless jewel with care,
and you will cross the ocean of worldly existence without effort.

**2059.** If once you utter the Name of Hari with your lips,
your bonds will be cut and all will go to the eternal abode.

**2060.** Very tight are the knots of the rope of maya;
who can cut them but with the sword of the Name of Hari?"

**2061.** Saying this, the Lord goes from door to door
and spreads the rasa of love through the world.

**2062.** The people of Amarapuri gathered together
and all were delighted to see the Lord.

**2063.** Like a madman he runs this way and that,
roaming about intoxicated with ecstasy.

**2064.** Sometimes he runs breathlessly, as if senseless,
the pupils of his eyes rolled upwards.

**2065.** The mass of his matted hair has fallen loose over his back,
and tears fall ceaselessly onto his breast.

*[p. 189]*

**2066.** Dressed like a madman, his garment slipping,
chalk flying all over his body, grey with dust.

**2067.** "Where is the sacrificial pit?" says Gora Raya,
and the pandas, staying with him, show him Prabhasa.

**2068.** We go to the southern part of Prabhasa
and there we see the pit.

**2069.** Having dug this pit, the Lord of the Yadus performed a sacrifice,
and at that sacrifice the Yadus fought and died.

**2070.** Where Satyabhama made a garden
and sometimes came there with Krishna,

**2071.** where the virtuous lady lived in supreme joy —
seeing that place, the great-souled Gauranga

**2072.** burst into tears, crying aloud;
crying out again and again, the Lord weeps without end.

**2073.** Gradually ten pandas gathered
and one by one began showing all the places.

**2074.** After three days of spiritual conversation here,
he then told us to go on to the city of Dvaraka.

**2075.** At Prabhasa there are no more tirthas to see;
on the first of Ashvin we go to Dvaraka.

**2076.** The Lord goes chanting Krishna's name;
along the seashore we go to Dvaraka.

*[p. 190]*

**2077.** After four days we come to an inlet of the sea;
it has to be crossed on a rope.

**2078.** Over the rope we cross to Dvaraka
and see a mountain called Raivataka.

**2079.** Seeing the mountain, Gora sways with feeling,
and the Lord smiled quietly.

**2080.** Who knows why, with a radiant face,
Mahaprabhu looked at me with a smile.

**2081.** Looking at me he said, "When you reach Dvaraka,
bow down, all of you, and be fulfilled.

**2082.** With great devotion smear the dust all over your bodies;
by the fruit of your merit you have seen the city of Dvaraka.

**2083.** By the strength of good deeds in former lives
you have all seen the city of Dvaraka today."

**2084.** Hearing this, all of us bowed down together,
and Gora's well of joy overflowed.

**2085.** Saying "Hari bol, Hari bol",
the Lord gradually came down, swaying.

**2086.** The ocean of feeling surged, overstepping all bounds;
who could hold the Lord back now?

**2087.** Rolling over and over, he falls on the ground,
and gradually he entered the city.

*[p. 191]*

**2088.** His hair stood on end and he began to tremble,
and tears seemed to burst from his eyes.

**2089.** "Where are you, Lord of Dvaraka?" — saying this,
he flooded the land of Dvaravati with tears.

**2090.** Everything in disarray, his matted hair came loose,
and Gora burst into very loud weeping.

**2091.** What can I say of his state — it is beyond words;
again and again the Lord cries out, "Krishna!"

**2092.** When he entered the house of the Lord of Dvaraka,
at once he grew twice as drunk with joy.

**2093.** His body bristled like a kadamba,
rolling over and over he falls, grey with dust.

**2094.** Drunk with feeling, the Lord gazes languidly
and lies rolling on the ground before the Lord of Dvaraka.

**2095.** All around, devotion seems to overflow;
my Gora, the delight of hearts, weeps and sobs.

**2096.** Sometimes, closing his eyes, he looks within,
as if he were seeing something in his heart.

**2097.** Sometimes he stands gazing upwards,
tears streaming down from his eyes.

**2098.** Seeing Krishna, his body was filled with thrills,
and he gazed at him fixedly.

*[p. 192]*

**2099.** He circles the holy temple three times,
bowing humbly each time.

**2100.** Gora, the delight of hearts, bows with all eight limbs,
and seeing this Bhargadeva falls prostrate.

**2101.** Gradually word spread through Dvaraka,
and everyone whispered about the Lord.

**2102.** Some say, "Come and see the sannyasi, brother;
no one has ever seen such a sannyasi.

**2103.** What can I say of him — it is beyond words;
surely there is no other sannyasi like him on earth.

**2104.** Never have I seen such a wondrous state;
I would give my life for the sannyasi's beauty and virtues!

**2105.** Seeing him, devotion arises in the heart,
and tears come into the eyes of themselves.

**2106.** I long to go away with the sannyasi;
if you see him, brother, your bonds are cut.

**2107.** Seeing him, one loses all taste for worldly life;
seeing that form, a sinner becomes pure at once.

**2108.** No one so merciful is to be seen;
in his mercy he gives the Name of Hari to all.

**2109.** A head full of matted hair, an outer cloth for clothing;
seeing his form, one's desires are fulfilled.

*[p. 193]*

**2110.** Seeing him, one feels he is an incarnation of God;
his heart is always full of the rasa of devotion.

**2111.** The young sannyasi is always drunk with this ecstasy;
he has stirred up the people of Dvaraka.

**2112.** He has no lust, no anger, no desire;
his faith in the Lord of Dvaraka is unshakeable.

**2113.** He calls the sinners and gives them the Name of Hari;
seeing him, the heart overflows."

**2114.** Gora Raya stayed a fortnight in Dvaraka
and went every day to the Lord of Dvaraka.

**2115.** Every day he went and had darshan to his heart's content,
and made the city of Dvaraka drunk with the rasa of devotion.

**2116.** All the people of Dvaraka who were devoted to God
come to see the Lord.

**2117.** The Lord holds spiritual conversation with all,
and all, singing kirtana, dance full of love.

**2118.** The city sways with the spirit of dharma;
everyone's heart seems to have become pure.

**2119.** A gentle breeze always began to blow,
and everything around was fragrant with lotus.

**2120.** All the people rejoice in the Lord's company;
women and men alike come running.

*[p. 194]*

**2121.** On all sides auspicious signs appeared;
through the Name of Hari every quarter grew bright.

**2122.** Pandas and householders all together
listen to the teaching on dharma with attentive ears.

**2123.** He explains to anyone who does not understand;
speaking in many tongues, the Lord inspires him.

**2124.** Sometimes my Lord speaks "khai mai",
speaks "jhi mai"[^10-138] and explains to everyone.

**2125.** How he makes the people understand — he is all-powerful;
hearing his teaching, all were beside themselves.

**2126.** Wise men and fools alike all came,
and seeing the Lord, all were thrilled.

**2127.** One evening the Lord slowly
went to the temple of Krishna.

**2128.** Many people follow behind the Lord,
but Shachi's son says nothing, good or bad.

**2129.** At the temple door he made the eight-limbed obeisance,
and seeing this all the people rolled on the ground.

**2130.** With folded hands the Lord sings many hymns,
and at once tears pour from his eyes.

**2131.** The Lord's heart brims with the rasa of love:
wherever he looks he sees everything full of Krishna.

*[p. 195]*

**2132.** Closing his eyes, he began to call, "Krishna!"
and his body thrilled with love.

**2133.** One who has not seen that state with his own eyes —
how shall I, a great fool, make him understand?

**2134.** Where there was no [water], a desert,
there Chaitanya Gosai made a river flow.

**2135.** In the whole country no sinner remained;
by giving devotion, the Lord delivered the sinners.

**2136.** One day the pandas, rejoicing,
held a great festival, making an offering.

**2137.** Inviting guests and Vaishnavas,
they distribute thickened milk, yogurt, puris and the rest.

**2138.** Gora, the jewel of virtues, going among the pandas,
distributes the prasada himself.

**2139.** At night we all go together to the hut
and pass the night in supreme joy.

**2140.** Thus, after a fortnight of spiritual conversation,
the next day the Lord leaves the city of Dvaraka.

**2141.** The Lord said, "This time we shall go to Nilachala,
and in Nilachala we shall all live together in joy.

**2142.** Come, let us all go together to Vidyanagara;
I will not go to Puri alone, leaving Rama Raya behind.

*[p. 196]*

**2143.** Ramananda is a great lover of worship;
when I think of him my heart is soothed.

**2144.** Ramananda Raya is the crest-jewel of practitioners;
sitting in solitude, Raya sings the glories of Krishna.

**2145.** One whose tears flow at the name of Hari-Krishna
the Bhagavata calls an eternal Vaishnava.

**2146.** If even a fool calls on the treasure that is Krishna with devotion,
I bow millions of times at his feet.

**2147.** Ramananda, the devotee of Krishna, is worthy of worship;
Ramananda Raya is dearer to me than life.

**2148.** I love Ramananda as my own life;
he is a supreme Vaishnava, a detached sannyasi.

**2149.** Rama Raya is unattached to worldly things;
every day Raya gets to see Radha and Krishna.

**2150.** Ramananda counts wealth as straw
and calls on Krishna with love day and night.

**2151.** I have seen that, as he calls out "Krishna",
Raya, drunk with love, keeps trembling.

**2152.** At Krishna's name he sheds tears of love
and falls unconscious on the ground.

**2153.** My heart can bear separation from Raya no longer;
let us all go quickly to Raya."

*[p. 197]*

**2154.** Saying this, the Lord set out,
and hundreds of people followed him.

**2155.** Sending the village people back with sweet words,
my Gora Raya goes towards the inlet.

**2156.** Bhargadeva with his party
comes to the bank of the inlet to cross.

**2157.** One by one, all having crossed,
my sannyasi of Nadia comes into Gujarat.

**2158.** On the last day of Ashvin, coming back to the city of Baroda,
my Lord chants the Name of Hari.

**2159.** Govinda Charana and I, to beg alms,
presented ourselves at householders' doors.

**2160.** Fruits, roots, flour, parched grain — whatever alms I receive,
I bring and offer with a pure heart.

**2161.** Settling beneath a tree, the Lord made the offering,
and receiving the prasada, all were fulfilled.

**2162.** Next day, setting out from Baroda,
the Lord began to go south.

**2163.** After sixteen days we came to the bank of the Narmada,
and all of us bathed in the waters of the Narmada.

**2164.** The Lord said, "Bhargadeva, where will you go?
Will you come with me to Nilachala?"

*[p. 198]*

**2165.** Bharga, before the Lord, rubbing his hands,
said, "I shall go south.

**2166.** In the city of Bom-bom[^10-139] the mahanta Aditya Raj
serves Ranchhodji with devotion.

**2167.** Accept my obeisance, Lord;
mercifully place your foot on my head."

**2168.** Saying this, Bhargadeva fell prostrate
and clasped both feet tightly with both hands.

**2169.** Bharga said, "You are Krishna, you are my Hari;
give me this alms: that I may die remembering your feet.

**2170.** To see your own lilas and play yourself,
you went to Dvaraka, deceiving people.

**2171.** Be that as it may, put your foot on my head;
you will not be able to deceive me again — I will not be deceived."

**2172.** The Lord said, "Bharga, why do you say such things?
Why do you speak such words to me?

**2173.** I wander the roads as a renunciant,
without food, without clothing, utterly poor and lowly.

**2174.** For alms I go from door to door;
never speak such words to me again.

**2175.** Always say 'Krishna, Krishna' with faith;
faith in Krishna will bring you to Krishna.

*[p. 199]*

**2176.** Hari is a mass of consciousness and bliss, the highest of the high;
meditate on his lotus feet and you will cross the ocean of existence.

**2177.** With love and devotion meditate on Hari's feet,
and he will surely show himself to you.

**2178.** Hari is very kind to his devotees;
think of him — he is the refuge of the helpless."

**2179.** Saying this, the Lord touched Bhargadeva,
and at once Bharga's body was purified.

**2180.** Standing on the bank, Bhargadeva gazes at him
and, fulfilled, at last took his leave.

**2181.** All the other sannyasis who were with Bharga
came and stood before the Lord.

**2182.** One by one they bowed at the Lord's feet,
and the Lord sent them all off with sweet words.

**2183.** Bhargadeva went away to the south,
and the Lord sets out lovingly for Nilachala.

**2184.** I, Ramananda and Govinda Charana
spent that day on the bank of the Narmada.

**2185.** Next day we go along the bank of the Narmada
and all arrive at the town of Dohad.

**2186.** I brought some flour that I had begged,
and Lord Gaura-Hari made rotis and offered them.

*[p. 200]*

**2187.** We pass the night in the town of Dohad;
beneath a tree Gora-chand raises the cry of "Hari".

**2188.** Rising in the morning, we go to the town of Kukshi,
and here we see many Vaishnavas.

**2189.** Wherever we go we see tulasi groves;
every villager, I see, is devoted to Krishna.

**2190.** In the evening all the people raise the cry of "Hari";
seeing this, my Lord thrills with joy.

**2191.** In this place lives a poor brahmana,
and in his house there is Lakshmi-Janardana.

**2192.** The brahmana worships Lakshmi-Janardana with devotion;
hearing of this, the Lord goes to his house.

**2193.** The brahmana, seeing the Lord, offers him hospitality,
welcoming him warmly, regarding him as a guest.

**2194.** The brahmana says, "I am a poor brahmana;
why have you set foot in my house?

**2195.** How shall I serve a sannyasi?
I fear my dharma will be ruined in my house."

**2196.** The Lord said, "Have no anxiety, thakura:
he whose creation it is will give food in plenty.

**2197.** Who worries for whom — that is the error of Kali;
hear the essence: it is Krishna who always cares.

*[p. 201]*

**2198.** The householder says, 'I feed everyone';
but who thinks how the homeless man living under a tree is fed?

**2199.** Who gives food to the tiny insects in the forest?
Why then, brahmana, do you worry for nothing?"

**2200.** Just then a vaishya[^10-140] came to the brahmana's door
bringing milk, sugar and flour to fill his store.

**2201.** The vaishya said, "Listen, listen, brahmana thakura:
the Lord has shown me his mercy.

**2202.** I saw your Janardana in a dream:
he asks me for sweet rice.

**2203.** In the form of a man Narayana is your guest;
Narayana showed me this in a dream.

**2204.** Last night, before dawn, I saw it in a dream,
and I have brought milk and sugar for his sake.

**2205.** Cook sweet rice and offer it to Narayana, brahmana."
Hearing this, the brahmana began to weep.

**2206.** The brahmana said, "Where have the milk and sugar come from?"
The Lord said, "Narayana himself has provided them."

**2207.** [Here came] milk and sugar [the line is corrupt in the edition].
The brahmana said, "I am a poor man — this is a marvel!"

**2208.** The Lord said, "Narayana…" [the line breaks off].
The brahmana said, "I was worrying about you."

**2209.** The Lord said, "Narayana has given and provided."[^10-141]

*[p. 202]*

**2210.** The vaishya, gazing at the Lord's face,
sees something, losing consciousness again and again.

**2211.** The brahmana said, "Vaishya, what are you seeing, brother?"
The vaishya said, "I am bewildered, that is why I look.

**2212.** Listen, best of brahmanas, what shall I tell you:
in my dream I saw him in human form."

**2213.** Hearing this, the Lord said to the vaishya,
"Why do you keep making a fuss for nothing?

**2214.** Whom did you see in an empty dream?
Why then do you make a fuss without reason?

**2215.** Brother vaishya, you are a holy man and very fortunate;
that is why the Lord Bhagavan appeared to you in a dream.

**2216.** I am an ordinary sannyasi, come
to a brahmana's house for the sake of food."

**2217.** The brahmana said, "What need is there for such talk?
Narayana takes care of the service of guests."

**2218.** Then the brahmana, weeping, begged the Lord,
"Cook the sweet rice yourself and make the offering."

**2219.** With a slight smile the Lord cooked the sweet rice,
and the brahmana, staying near, waited upon him.

**2220.** All received the prasada with joy;
the Lord distributed the prasada with his own hands.

---

### Translator's notes

[^10-138]: "Khai mai", "jhi mai": Govinda imitates the sound of languages unknown to him (Gujarati, Kathiawari).
[^10-139]: "Bom-bom nagar" is apparently Bombay (Mumbai), as D. C. Sen suggests.
[^10-140]: A vaishya is a member of the merchant class.
[^10-141]: Lines 2207–2209 are badly damaged in the edition (some words are missing); the translation gives the surviving sense.

---

# Part 11

*Back through Central India: Amjhora, Mandu, Devghar (the healing of the leper), Vidyanagara and Ramananda Raya, Sambalpur, Rasalkunda (the Marwari brahmana and his son). The return to Puri (pp. 203–222)*

*[p. 203]*

**2221.** There was a great festival in the brahmana's house;
next morning the Lord sets out.

**2222.** The excellent brahmana, coming up lovingly,
pleaded with him long, with folded hands.

**2223.** Taking leave of the brahmana,
my Gora Raya set out in the morning.

**2224.** The vaishya, who had been waiting in hiding,
followed the Lord along the road.

**2225.** Clasping his feet, the vaishya began to weep,
and merciful Chaitanya raised him up.

**2226.** The Lord said, "Holy man, what are you doing, brother?"
The vaishya said, "Have mercy on me, Gosai.

**2227.** I will not let you go — I have recognised you;
give me the dust of your feet and be merciful to me."

**2228.** Smiling, Lord Chaitanya
poured the nectar of the Name of Hari into his ears once.

**2229.** By the Lord's mercy his sins were destroyed,
and the vaishya, greatly blessed, went away.

**2230.** By the Lord's grace the vaishya gave up worldly things,
went far away and lives there, having made a tulasi grove.

**2231.** He does not converse with anyone
and always meditates on Krishna with the flute at his lips.

*[p. 204]*

**2232.** With his lips he says, "O Hari, have mercy on me;
in your kindness take away all this sinner's suffering."

**2233.** He lives as a hermit and does not go home,
and saying "Hari" he begs alms from door to door.

**2234.** Having shown mercy to the vaishya, Lord Vishvambhara
left the town and went on through the jungle.

**2235.** We all go on through dense jungle,
and for two days we see no town or village.

**2236.** After two days, leaving the jungle,
we reach the town of Amjhora.

**2237.** We writhe in the pangs of hunger,
while my Lord, unperturbed, says "Hari, Hari".

**2238.** The Lord said, "Hari provides food for all."
That same day food came to us of itself.

**2239.** I brought two handfuls of flour I had begged,
and the Lord himself made sixteen rotis.

**2240.** Just then a woman with a child
said, "Give me something — I am dying of hunger.

**2241.** I have no food, no clothing, nothing to eat;
I go along the roads with my child, living on alms."

**2242.** Hearing her words, the merciful Lord
gave her his own share.

*[p. 205]*

**2243.** The wretched woman went away content,
and the Lord passed the day without food.

**2244.** At night I brought some fruit I had begged,
and the Lord, partaking of the fruit, passed the night.

**2245.** There is a tank of Lakshmana here;
hearing of it in the morning, we go there to bathe.

**2246.** The tank at the edge of the town is most charming,
surrounded by hills, of small extent.

**2247.** When Janaki's throat was parched with thirst,
Lakshmana dug this tank by shooting an arrow.

**2248.** It became famous as the tank of Lakshmana;
Janaki called this tank a great tirtha.

**2249.** A most delightful tank, very deep;
bathing in it, our bodies were cooled.

**2250.** Having bathed in this tirtha, merciful Gora
raises the cry of "Hari" — hearing it, the fear of Yama goes.

**2251.** Next day we go up onto the Vindhya mountains,
where the town of Mandura[^11-142] stands in splendour.

**2252.** In the midst of the mountains, inside a cave,
an ascetic lives and performs austerities.

**2253.** Hearing of the ascetic, my Gora Raya
goes there to see him.

*[p. 206]*

**2254.** The sannyasi thakura is sitting in meditation;
the ascetic's form is most lovely.

**2255.** His body is the colour of molten gold,
and rays of radiance stream out all around.

**2256.** His long, long nails have curled over,
and his white beard covers his chest.

**2257.** Only skin and bone remain, his body emaciated;
his ribs can be seen.

**2258.** He sits motionless, naked;
seeing that form, devotion arose in me.

**2259.** He looks like a wooden image;
opening my eyes wide, I look at his face again and again.

**2260.** Mahaprabhu went and stood before him,
and the ascetic, breaking his meditation, began to look.

**2261.** The moment their eyes met,
the excellent ascetic smiled.

**2262.** Having conversed with the ascetic,
the Lord comes down to the foot of the mountain, to the town of Mandul.

**2263.** On the left the Vindhya range stands in splendour, on the right the Narmada;
from there we reach Devghar in three days.

**2264.** There was a leper in Devghar;
this sick man was named Adi Narayana.

*[p. 207]*

**2265.** Adi Narayana is the foremost of merchants;
he has much wealth, but his mind is always troubled.

**2266.** Outside the village there is a banyan tree;
the merciful Lord went and sat beside it.

**2267.** The Lord's beauty lights up everything around,
and gradually word spread in the town.

**2268.** Two or four people come to see the sannyasi,
and I go into the town to beg.

**2269.** Ramananda goes to bring flowers,
Govinda Charana went to the riverbank.

**2270.** That day I received unboiled rice as alms,
and Ramananda brings flowers of every kind.

**2271.** Having bathed, my Lord began the worship,
and Govinda Charana brought dry wood.

**2272.** After making the offering, Gora Raya began the Name,
and as he chanted the Name his thrills increased.

**2273.** His body choked with love, he began to dance
and at last fell senseless to the ground.

**2274.** Hearing of this, many people come there,
and that leper came and stood close by.

**2275.** Adi Narayana weeps with folded hands,
and, saying "Save me", wept a great deal.

*[p. 208]*

**2276.** Adi Narayana is a supreme Vaishnava;
they gave him prasada to eat.

**2277.** Partaking of the prasada with devotion,
he was at once rid of his leprosy.

**2278.** Receiving prasada, his leprosy left him;
hearing this news, many sick people come.

**2279.** Seeing the difficulty, the Lord began to look around,
then, looking at me, made a sign.

**2280.** I took the sandals and set out,
and on that pretext the Lord leaves the town.

**2281.** Then Adi Narayana follows along.
The Lord said, "By Krishna's mercy you are freed —

**2282.** why then do you come with me?
Go home and always meditate on Krishna's feet."

**2283.** Adi Narayana said, "I will not go home;
I will wander from land to land with you."

**2284.** The Lord said, "Go home and enjoy your wealth."
Narayana said, "What use have I for wealth?

**2285.** If you will not take me with you, merciful one,
I shall build a hut and pass my time there."

**2286.** The Lord said, "Go and make a tulasi grove,
and sitting there, pass your time."

*[p. 209]*

**2287.** Hearing the Lord's words, Adi Narayana gave up everything
and says "Hari, Hari" with his lips.

**2288.** Adi Narayana became the best of holy men;
he passes his time chanting Krishna's name.

**2289.** Bowing at his feet, Adi Narayana
took his leave of the Lord.

**2290.** Thirty kroshas away is the town of Shivana;
in two days Vishvambhara hurries there.

**2291.** East of Shivana is the Mahal mountain;
there the Lord goes, full of love for Krishna.

**2292.** Having seen the Mahal mountain,
the Lord comes to the town of Chandapur.

**2293.** Having had darshan of the goddess Chandi in Chandapur,
Gora goes on to Raipur, remembering Hari.

**2294.** Many people in Raipur, wishing to see him,
came into Chaitanya's presence.

**2295.** Seeing the wretched state of living beings, my Gora Raya
joyfully distributes the Name of Hari from house to house.

**2296.** Then the Lord came to Vidyanagara,
and Ramananda meets him with folded hands.

**2297.** Ramananda Raya came and bowed down,
and the Lord, raising him by the hand, embraced him.

*[p. 210]*

**2298.** The supreme Vaishnava Raya, drawing far back,
began to weep with great humility.

**2299.** The Lord said, "Raya, come with me;
let us go together and behold Lord Jagannatha.

**2300.** You, I and the Bhatta, going to Nilachala,
will chant the Name of Hari to our hearts' content.

**2301.** With you I shall find great joy in discussing the truth;
come, you shall go with me to Nilachala."

**2302.** Raya said, "You go on ahead;
in a few days I shall go to Nilachala."

**2303.** Hearing this, Mahaprabhu smiled a little,
and, rising in the morning, went north.

**2304.** That day we covered a great distance,
and in six days the four of us reach Ratnapur.

**2305.** Leaving Ratnapur we come to the Mahanadi,
and all go east along its bank.

**2306.** A little further on Mahaprabhu came to Svarnagarh
and gazed attentively at the beauty of the town.

**2307.** Wondrous is the beauty of the fort — what more can I say?
Looking all around, I was astonished.

**2308.** A king named Shantishvara lives in this fort;
a messenger went and told this to the king.

*[p. 211]*

**2309.** Hearing of us, the worthy king
comes humbly to see the Lord.

**2310.** The most righteous king, seeing the Lord,
with folded hands lay prostrate on the ground.

**2311.** The king said, "Listen, worthy sannyasi,
sanctify my house today.

**2312.** Today, in your kindness, take alms in my house."
Saying this, the king sang many praises.

**2313.** Hearing this, the Lord looked at me,
and I asked the king for alms.

**2314.** The maharaja brought and gave full alms,
and having given the alms, remained standing with folded hands.

**2315.** In the afternoon the maharaja took his leave,
and Mahaprabhu passed the night beneath a tree.

**2316.** In the morning we all go to Sambalpur
and arrive there at dusk.

**2317.** The town, surrounded by hills, is very beautiful;
the Lord passes the night joyfully in Sambalpur.

**2318.** Ten kroshas away is the town of Bhramara;[^11-143]
there Mahaprabhu hastened on.

**2319.** Many Vaishnavas live in the town of Bhramara;
here the Lord stayed four days.

*[p. 212]*

**2320.** An Oriya brahmana named Vishnu Rudra
lives here and serves Krishna.

**2321.** The brahmana Vishnu Rudra is very devoted;
the Lord went to see him.

**2322.** Having conversed with Vishnu Rudra,
the Lord goes joyfully on to the town of Pratapnagari.

**2323.** Having given the Name of Hari to the people of this town,
he went on to the town of Dasapalla.

**2324.** Unbelievers, Mayavadis and sufferers, wherever they were,
the Lord made drunk by giving the Name of Hari.

**2325.** Gora is always drunk with joy;
how many sinners he delivered by giving the Name of Hari!

**2326.** Next day we go to Rasalkunda[^11-144]
and there we see the deity Kurma.

**2327.** Seeing Kurmadeva, the Lord was drunk with love;
tears streamed from both his eyes.

**2328.** With folded hands he sings many hymns to Kurmadeva
and falls crashing to the ground.

**2329.** The people of Rasalkunda are very lacking in devotion;
seeing this, the Lord stays there three days.

**2330.** Calling everyone, men and women alike,
the Lord delivers them by giving the Name of Hari.

*[p. 213]*

**2331.** By the Lord's mercy everyone grew excited
and began to chant the Name of Hari with devotion.

**2332.** There was a Marwari brahmana here;
his son met the Lord.

**2333.** The brahmana's son said, "Have mercy on me;
give me the dust of your feet, Lord, and take away my sorrow.

**2334.** I am a great unbeliever and know nothing;
give me devotion and save me yourself.

**2335.** My father cannot bear the name of Krishna;
in your mercy give devotion to his heart.

**2336.** This is my great sorrow, that my father hates Krishna;
give devotion to his mind, merciful Lord.

**2337.** When my father sees a Vaishnava he abuses him;
in your mercy remove all his sins.

**2338.** I have heard that you are an abode of mercy;
give me this alms, O merciful one."

**2339.** Hearing this, the Lord put his hand on the boy's back,
and at once devotion arose in his heart.

**2340.** Hearing of this, the brahmana, blind with rage,
ran up to the Lord with a stick in his hand.

**2341.** The brahmana said, "Listen, you hypocrite, you villain!
You have ruined my only son!

*[p. 214]*

**2342.** I will strike you with this stick;
I will see right now who will protect you!"

**2343.** With folded hands, weeping, the brahmana's son said,
"Merciful one, forgive my father's offence.

**2344.** My father is utterly ignorant and does not recognise you;
count me as a servant of your feet and forgive me."

**2345.** Hearing this, two or four people,
rebuking the Marwari, went for him.

**2346.** The Marwari brahmana heeded no one's words
and, stick in hand, rose up to beat Chaitanya.

**2347.** The brahmana said, "Having made my son a Vaishnava,
you will lead him away with you, beguiling him!

**2348.** Where will you go, having beguiled the boy?
This time I will punish you and teach you a lesson.

**2349.** I have seen many sannyasis with my own eyes;
this time you will get a lesson from me."

**2350.** Smiling, Chaitanya said, "Listen, my brother:
if you want to beat me, you must say the Name of Hari.

**2351.** As many times as you utter the Name of Hari,
so many blows of the stick you may give.

**2352.** If in your anger you want to beat me,
then say the name 'Hare Krishna' with your lips.

*[p. 215]*

**2353.** Look, here I have bared my back to you;
say 'Hari' once and strike me."

**2354.** Hearing this again, the brahmana's son,
with folded hands, spoke again before the Lord:

**2355.** The boy said, "Lord, forgive my father,
save him from hell.

**2356.** This alms I beg at your lotus feet,
that people may not say, 'Cruel Nimai'."

**2357.** Then the Lord, with a slight smile, said to him,
"In the family into which you have been born

**2358.** no one has any fear of hell;
millions of generations will have a place in Vaikuntha."

**2359.** Saying this, he looked at the brahmana:
"Say, brahmana, say 'Hari' and strike me.

**2360.** Your hard heart is like a desert;
may it become full of sap today by Krishna's mercy.

**2361.** Strike me — that does no harm, brahmana;
just once say 'Hare Krishna' with your lips, brother."

**2362.** Hearing such words, the brahmana began to tremble
and in his fear let his head sink onto his chest.

**2363.** Shrinking with fear, the brahmana cannot see;
weeping in distress, he fell to the ground.

*[p. 216]*

**2364.** Overwhelmed by the Lord's power, the brahmana
clasped both his feet tightly with both hands.

**2365.** The brahmana said, "Merciful one, I beseech you:
save me from hell.

**2366.** Having committed a great offence, I am afraid;
in your mercy forgive my offence, merciful one.

**2367.** Without understanding, I said so many things to you;
punish or protect me, as you judge best."

**2368.** Seeing the brahmana's humility, Gora, the delight of hearts,
poured the nectar of the Name of Hari into his ears.

**2369.** The brahmana was fulfilled, his mind became pure,
and at last he took his leave, clasping the Lord's feet.

**2370.** Having delivered the unbelieving brahmana, the Lord
went on to the bank of the river Rishikulya.

**2371.** Many rishis live on both banks of the river;
all together they welcomed Gora.

**2372.** When the Lord came to the river Rishikulya,
this news gradually reached Puri.

**2373.** Staying three nights at the abode of Rishikulya,
the Lord sanctified Rishikulya with the Name of Hari.

**2374.** When the Lord came near Alalanatha,
Gadadhara and Murari came running to him.

*[p. 217]*

**2375.** Khanjan Acharya[^11-145] comes with deep love;
lame as he is, he comes ahead of everyone.

**2376.** Sarvabhauma comes, beating two kettle-drums,
and Narahari appears carrying a banner.

**2377.** Haridasa, Ramadasa and Krishnadasa
come eagerly, breathing hard.

**2378.** Jagannatha Dasa and Devaki-nandana,
Junior Haridasa and the singer Lakshmana,

**2379.** Vishnudasa, Puridasa and Damodara,
Narayana Tirtha and Giridhara Dasa —

**2380.** giris, puris, sarasvatis,[^11-146] countless brahmanas —
all come to see Gora.

**2381.** A great master at playing the shinga horn,
Balarama Dasa comes, full of thrills.

**2382.** Hundreds of learned gosais appeared,
and my heart began to dance with joy.

**2383.** Some dance, some laugh, some sing songs;
with one mouth that joy cannot be told.

**2384.** Thousands upon thousands, surrounding the Lord,
began the Name, drunk with joy.

**2385.** The Lord went to embrace Murari and Mukunda,
and Gupta[^11-147] collapsed near his knees.

*[p. 218]*

**2386.** Siddha Krishnadasa came and bowed down,
and the Lord, raising him by the hand, embraced him.

**2387.** Gathering together, all the other devotees too
come to take the Lord with them.

**2388.** All the bands of Vaishnavas play their madals;
in his joy the Lord's eyes grow moist.

**2389.** All the Vaishnavas together sing kirtana,
and Gora, the delight of hearts, dances, swaying his head.

**2390.** Seeing Khanjan, the Lord, crying "Hari bol",
spread his arms and embraced him.

**2391.** Gora began to dance with arms outstretched,
and Sarvabhauma fell prostrate at his feet.

**2392.** With folded hands Sarvabhauma began to say,
"The arrow of separation from you has pierced my heart.

**2393.** I am a great fool; that is why I have borne separation from you
and kept my life in me all these days.

**2394.** In your mercy trample my body with your feet;
then I shall know, Lord, that you love me."

**2395.** Saying this, Sarvabhauma rolls on the ground,
and Gora Raya, raising him, embraces him.

**2396.** Thus, raising the cry of "Hari",
they all set off to Puri, taking the Lord.

*[p. 219]*

**2397.** Hundreds of white, blue and many-coloured banners,
how many kettle-drums sound, rumbling!

**2398.** Some dance, some sing, drunk with joy;
how many people stand pressing close, gazing fixedly!

**2399.** Swaying, Shachi's darling goes along,
while sweet mridangas play, delightful to hear.

**2400.** Raising his arms, Gadadhara began to dance;
Raghunatha Dasa dances, and Damodara too.

**2401.** The Lord asks after Raghunatha affectionately:
"I am very happy to see you."

**2402.** Gora Raya goes to embrace Raghunatha,
but Raghunatha falls and lies prostrate at his feet.

**2403.** On the third day of Magha my Gora Raya[^11-148]
with all his companions reaches Puri.

**2404.** In the afternoon Mahaprabhu reached Puri,
and millions of people thronged there.

**2405.** With the dust of the road on his feet, the Lord, with many people,
entered the temple and beheld Jagannatha.

**2406.** As he gazed fixedly at Mahavishnu,
tears of love began to stream down.

**2407.** All at once losing consciousness, Gora Raya
immediately fell crashing to the ground.

*[p. 220]*

**2408.** His matted locks came loose, his kaupina slipped,
his body grey with dust, like the poorest of the poor.

**2409.** All around, the devotees raise the cry of "Hari",
and Sarvabhauma lifted him up and held him.

**2410.** His hair bristled like a kadamba,
and sweat flowed in streams.

**2411.** Regaining consciousness, the Lord stood up
and began to gaze fixedly at Mahavishnu.

**2412.** Sarvabhauma says, "Lord, seeing your own form,
you have made the well of your feeling overflow.

**2413.** Seeing your own image, to teach the people,
you began to weep, drunk with great emotion.

**2414.** Before you is the immovable Vishnu, and you are the moving one;[^11-149]
why then do you weep, Lord, and play so many tricks?

**2415.** You are Krishna in person, the son of the king of Vraja;
why do you blind my eyes with your moods?

**2416.** Put on airs before someone who does not understand,
but do not hide your own form from me.

**2417.** You are the lifter of Govardhana, the lord of Vrindavana,
the life of the gopis, the refuge of the helpless.

**2418.** You were born in the Yadu race, yet they did not recognise you;
the unfortunate Yadavas understood nothing.

*[p. 221]*

**2419.** Having got you in my hands, I will not let you go;
take up the flute and show me your own form.

**2420.** A golden doll is painted on your breast,
by whose radiance your dark form cannot be seen."[^11-150]

**2421.** The Lord said, "Sarvabhauma, talk of something else.
Why do you talk such idle nonsense?

**2422.** Why do you get excited for nothing and say all sorts of things?
Hearing your words, I put my hands over my ears.

**2423.** Calling me the son of the king of Vraja,
why do you make me an offender without cause?

**2424.** Talk of Krishna in your mouth is like nectar;
speak of Krishna, Bhatta, let my soul be soothed."

**2425.** The Bhatta said, "What you, Lord, make me say —
what else can I say, a beast in human form?"

**2426.** The Lord said, "There is no need for more words;
let us all go together today to our place."[^11-151]

**2427.** Having seen the arati, to the house of Kashi Mishra
he came with all his companions.

**2428.** Just then Sarvabhauma, bringing prasada,
arrived at that place.

**2429.** Gora, the delight of hearts, distributes the prasada,
and all rejoice on receiving the prasada.

*[p. 222]*

**2430.** In Kashi Mishra's house there is a huge courtyard;
many people come to see the Lord.

**2431.** Staying in Mishra's house, merciful Gora
sings Krishna's glories every day in supreme joy.

**2432.** How can I tell how many people come and go?
Every day there are new joys in Mishra's house.

**2433.** Hearing from people of the Lord's arrival,
how many people of Gauda come to see him!

**2434.** The worthy Bhatta brings prasada every day,
and receiving the prasada, the Lord is filled with joy.

**2435.** Gora, the delight of hearts, joyfully taking the prasada,
distributes the prasada into everyone's hands.

**2436.** Before the prasada there is nama-sankirtana,
and all eat the prasada with loving attachment.

**2437.** "Today we have become blessed" — saying this,
all dance joyfully, clapping their hands.

**2438.** Ramananda, Vasanta and Govinda Charana
took their leave and went to Gauda.

**2439.** Again, to see Gauranga,
hundreds of people come, full of love.

**2440.** Srivasa, Keshava Dasa and Siddha Haridasa
all together come to Chaitanya.

---

### Translator's notes

[^11-142]: Mandura (Mandua) is Mandu (Mandavgarh), the fortress-city on the spurs of the Vindhyas above the Narmada; below it is called Mandul.
[^11-143]: Bhramara is Bamra (Orissa).
[^11-144]: Rasalkunda is Russellkonda (Bhanjanagar) in southern Orissa; nearby is Kurmakshetra (Srikurmam).
[^11-145]: Khanjan ("the lame") Acharya is apparently Khanja Bhagavan Acharya, one of Chaitanya's companions at Puri.
[^11-146]: Giri, puri and sarasvati are names of branches of the order of sannyasis (Dashanami).
[^11-147]: Murari Gupta.
[^11-148]: That is, a little more than two years after leaving Puri (the departure was on 7 Vaishakha). The traditional chronology (the *Charitamrita*) allows about two years for the southern pilgrimage.
[^11-149]: A play on words: Jagannatha is the *achala* (immovable) Vishnu, Chaitanya the *chala* (moving) one.
[^11-150]: Another formula of the doctrine of Chaitanya as Krishna concealed by the "golden doll", Radha.
[^11-151]: That is, to the place where they will stay (Kashi Mishra's house).

---

# Part 12

*Puri: the festival of the Name, Prataparudra in the procession. Govinda is sent to Shantipur for Advaita. The manuscript breaks off (pp. 223–224 and the ending from the 1926 edition)*

*[p. 223]*

**2441.** Shantacharya, Vipradasa, Rupa and Sanatana[^12-152]
came in a crowd to see him.

**2442.** All, drunk with joy, chant the Name of Hari,
and merciful Chaitanya gives devotion from house to house.

**2443.** "Who will take the Name of Hari? Come, my brothers!" —
saying this, Nimai distributes the Name of Hari.

**2444.** By the Lord's mercy no sinner or sufferer remained;
the Lord gives the Name of Hari wherever he may be.

**2445.** The great tirtha of Puri became an abode of joy;
children, women and the old all chant the Name of Hari.

**2446.** Beasts and birds dance on hearing the Name;
before him the sea dances with arms outstretched.

**2447.** The old dance, the young dance, the children dance;
respectable women come out onto the road to see him.

**2448.** On one side the lord of rivers[^12-153] began to dance,
on the other the ocean of love surged up.

**2449.** As if the trees and creepers, drunk with love,
were shedding tears in the guise of dew.

**2450.** Every day there are new joys in Puri;
whoever has seen it knows — to whom shall I tell it?

**2451.** Mridangas, kettle-drums and kartals are playing,
and in their midst my darling of Shachi dances.

*[p. 224]*

**2452.** Ramadasa is a great master at beating the kettle-drum;
that is why he comes to the kirtana every day.

**2453.** The great devotee Ramadasa, full of loving attachment,
goes ahead of the kirtana, beating the drum.

**2454.** Prataparudra, joyfully leaving his royal throne,
comes every day to Mishra's house to watch the dance.

**2455.** When Mahaprabhu goes out in nagara-kirtana,
the maharaja, dressed as a pauper, runs behind.[^12-154]

**2456.** With both arms raised high and his limbs loosened,
the Lord goes dancing along, drunk with love.

**2457.** With half-closed eyes he gazes upwards,
now and then falling crashing to the ground.

**2458.** All are drunk with the Name of Hari, men and women alike;
drunk, the respectable women run along in rows.

**2459.** Thousands upon thousands go on all sides,
and ahead the Lord goes, dancing.

**2460.** Thus, chanting the Name, he passes his days,
and spends his nights in seclusion with Raya.[^12-155]

**2461.** One day Mahaprabhu, in his love for Krishna,
rushed forward to embrace Mahavishnu.

**2462.** Heeding no obstacle, he runs in his longing
and falls to the ground before the barrier.[^12-156]

*[The ending, from the 1926 edition; in the base edition the text breaks off here]*

**2463.** From that day the Lord does not enter the temple,
but every day has darshan from a distance.

**2464.** Standing at the door of the Bhoga-mandira,
he gazes fixedly at Mahavishnu.

**2465.** Resting his left hand on the Garuda pillar,
the Lord has darshan, drunk with love.

**2466.** Thus, staying in Puri for some time,
he gazed at Jagannatha with love.

**2467.** One day my Lord, in Mishra's house,
was singing Krishna's glories with the devotees.

**2468.** Calling me, "Govinda!",
he told me to go to the Acharya.[^12-157]

**2469.** As soon as I received the order, I took leave of the Lord,
bowed down and set out for Shantipur.

**2470.** The Lord patted me on the back and blessed me,
and tears flowed from my eyes in a hundred streams.

**2471.** The Lord said, "Do not weep, Govinda, my dear;
bring the Acharya here and rejoice."

**2472.** Hearing these words, tears flowed from my eyes;
my heart cannot bear the arrow of separation from the Lord.

**2473.** How shall I bear the force of separation from the Lord?
A cruel anguish has arisen in my heart…

*(The manuscript is incomplete.)*[^12-158]

---

### Translator's notes

[^12-152]: Shantacharya and Vipradasa are associates of Chaitanya. The mention of Rupa and Sanatana at Puri in 1512 is an obvious anachronism: according to all other sources they first met Chaitanya at Ramakeli in 1514 and came to Puri later still. This is one of the passages relied on by those who deny the *Karcha*'s authenticity.
[^12-153]: The lord of rivers is the ocean.
[^12-154]: Compare the famous episode of the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 13) in which King Prataparudra sweeps the road before Jagannatha's chariot.
[^12-155]: With Ramananda Raya.
[^12-156]: Compare CC Madhya 1: when Chaitanya first saw Jagannatha he rushed forward to embrace him and fell unconscious; later he viewed the Lord from the Garuda pillar, holding it with his left hand. The *Karcha* places this episode after the return from the South.
[^12-157]: To Advaita Acharya at Shantipur, to announce Mahaprabhu's return.
[^12-158]: In the 1926 edition these lines are followed by the editor's mark "(*khandita*)", "incomplete", and a note: "Something of what happened after this is to be found in the *Chaitanya-chandrodaya*, but much more in Prema Dasa's *Chaitanya-chandrodaya-kaumudi*." The manuscript on which the edition was based thus broke off at this point.
